## 2026-11-03

- **build: the generated PNG decoder is regenerated from the fixed emitter; the pellegrino embed is not.** `packages/world/png_decode_gen.c` now comes from the command PARENA's own Makefile records (`bytes.prn compress/inflate.prn image/png.prn`); the diff is 18 lines, every one a binding the emitter could not type before (`__typeof__`) or a zero-initialized `__let_val_N` declaration (`= {0}`). Both `-Wuninitialized` warnings in it are gone and `make -B lobby` no longer reports them. `packages/pellegrino_embed/pellegrino_gen.inc` is deliberately NOT regenerated: current PARENA emits 515 lines of unrelated drift there (type errors in `h3_done`, `void *` passed where `Bytes` is expected) that predates this work, so it keeps its two warnings until that drift is fixed on purpose. PARENA now types those bindings with `__typeof__` instead of the `void *` fallback, which makes the emitted types faithful but does NOT by itself fix this file: a regeneration was attempted and still fails, because the binding now takes the callee's real declared type and the quic entry points are not declared as returning `Bytes` in that translation unit. So the remaining work is a type mismatch between the generated engine and those declarations, not the emitter -- an earlier revision of this note said the regeneration “should compile”, which the attempt disproved. Checked rather than assumed: the emit.c diff behind this change touches only the declaration's initializer text, never its type, and the failing lines come from a `let` bound to a runtime call whose return type the emitter does not know, which is emitted as `void *` (see PARENA's changelog; the loop/match temp fallback this note first blamed was the wrong mechanism, corrected there).

- **fix(lobby): the unused-variable warnings were guarded debug code, not leftovers.** `prev_vx/vy/vz`, `prev_ground` and `prev_jump` are read only inside `#if NET_PARITY_DEBUG`, and three `dt_ms` locals are read only by `NET_CLIENT_LOG(...)`, whose arguments the macro strips when verbose logging is off -- so deleting them (the obvious fix the warning suggests) would have broken the parity and verbose builds. The five are now declared under the same `#if` that reads them, and each `dt_ms` is marked as captured. `make -B lobby` reports none of them any more. Verified: `make -B lobby`, `test-wow-client`, E2E 3/3. The lobby still carries 43 older warnings (format truncation, misleading indentation, unused static draw helpers) that predate this work and are deliberately not touched here.

- **fix(server): the three build warnings, without deleting the code they were about.** `mode_before` and `spawned` are read only by log calls that strip their arguments when verbose logging is off, so they are not dead -- removing them would reintroduce the latent `NET_VERBOSE_LOG=1` compile error the comment above `mode_before` documents; both are now explicitly marked as captured. The unchecked `system("mkdir -p var/maps")` now reports its exit status, so a failed zone write distinguishes a missing directory from an unwritable one. Verified: warning-free default build, `test-proc-dungeon`, `test-zone-sync`, `test-wow-wire`, E2E 3/3, and the verbose path these two captures exist for -- `NET_VERBOSE_LOG` is `#ifndef`-guarded, so the server was rebuilt from the Makefile's own compile line with `-DNET_VERBOSE_LOG=1` (output to /tmp, `bin/` untouched) and compiles clean with its log arguments live -- the only mode where `mode_before` and `spawned` are read. The lobby's `main.c` compiles clean the same way -- `-fsyntax-only` with its own include and define flags plus `-DNET_VERBOSE_LOG=1`. An earlier attempt at that half reported a false negative because it reused the `make -n` output for the EDITOR.GAME test-build rule instead of the lobby's own line. `make` itself still offers no switch for it: `EXTRA_DEFS` and `EXTRA_CFLAGS` are ignored, so it takes an explicit flag.

- **obs(server): a refused wire op is now logged instead of vanishing.** `wow_server_rx` returns 0 for a full cast queue, an undefined macro id, an unknown weapon in a macro, or a target intent older than the applied one, and the UDP handler did nothing with that value -- so the refusal existed only as the client's "the server never acknowledged a ..." chat line, which reads like packet loss. The handler now logs `WOW_REFUSED client_id op total` for the first three and every hundredth after; the wire deliberately stays silent (an ACK means the op was taken, and ACKing a macro that was not stored would be a lie), which the log line says out loud. Verified: `make server`, `make lobby`, `test-wow-wire`, three E2E phases.

- **fix(lobby): wire retries are polled on socket readiness, not on snapshot sync.** `wow_net_poll_retries()` lived inside the in-game input gate, which also waits for `net_have_initial_local_snapshot_sync`, so a frame sent between connect and the first snapshot -- the AUTH ticket, the first target change -- could not be retried or given up until a snapshot had already arrived. The poll now sits beside `client_decay_pending_correction` and is gated only by `wow_net_wire_ready()`; target sync stays in the input gate because targets only change there. Compile-verified (`make lobby`, `make server`), `test-wow-client` green; the call site itself cannot be exercised here (no display). An earlier revision of this entry said the ticket is sent from the menu -- `wow_net_wire_ready()` requires `STATE_GAME_NET`, so that was wrong and is corrected here.

- **docs(wire): `NetWowWire`'s 64-byte array is documented as a receive bound, not the wire size.** The struct had no comment at all, which is how the 80-byte habit survived the format change: the comment now states that a sender writes exactly `header + 1 + len` (19 for a cast, 51 for an AUTH frame) and that the receiver refuses a datagram smaller than the frame it claims. Also `docs/MASTER_AFFORDANCE_LIBRARY.md`: the schema, HP/density and rotation questions now carry a recommendation **and** its cost, so the decision is one line rather than an essay. Verified: `make lobby`, `make server`, `test-wow-wire`, all three E2E phases.

- **docs(common): the unit layer's own header stops lying about itself.** `packages/common/units_rules.h` still said the emitter "cannot take F32 or I64" and that "nothing in the engine calls this layer yet" -- both false since the emitter fix and the cast-range/gate work. It now says why F64 is the right choice for exact factors (1 cu ft = 28.316846592 L does not fit a float) and names the two real consumers. Comment only; `test-units`, `check-units`, `test-proc-dungeon`, `make lobby` and `make server` rebuilt and green.

- **test(wire): one consolidated pass over both repos, all green.** `check-wow-wire-rules`, `test-wow-wire` (plain + ASan/UBSan), `test-wow-client`, `test-wow-macros`, `test-wow-ticket`, `test-units`, `check-units`, `test-proc-dungeon`, `test-zone-sync`, `check-levels`, `test-leveltool`, `make lobby`, `make server`, the three E2E phases on ephemeral ports, and PARENA's `make test` (374/374) all exited 0. The same run re-checked the blocked lanes rather than asserting them: no `go`, no `DISPLAY`, no `/dev/dri`, no IDUNA ticket. Recorded in `docs/WOW_WIRE_NEXT_STEPS.md`.

- **perf(wire): a cast datagram drops from 80 bytes to 19.** The 64-byte frame buffer was being transmitted even though the length byte already frames the datagram and the server validates `header + 1 + len <= size`; both senders now send exactly the frame. Round trip goes 98 -> 37 bytes, a slow-preset give-up 240 -> 57, and at 1 kbit/s that is 784 -> 296 ms per successful cast. `wow_wire_test` pins the new numbers and the E2E exercises the short datagram against the real server (auth 51 bytes, cast 19). Verified: `test-wow-wire`, all three E2E phases, `make lobby`, `make server`.

- **feat(zones): the dungeon gate's height is authored in feet and converted once.** `zone-door-height-ft` (7 ft) joins the zone layout rules in PARENA; `packages/simulation/proc_dungeon.h` converts it with `units_author_length_m(..., 4)` and builds the gate box at exactly that height instead of spanning the full 18-30 unit wall. This is the unit layer's second real consumer (`docs/UNITS.md`) and lane 7's done-when: changing the authored feet value changes geometry, with one conversion site. The lobby had to link `packages/common/units_rules.c`, which is the proof the call is live. Verified: `test-proc-dungeon` (gate height == 7 ft, shorter than the wall, PARENA vs C defaults agree), `test-units`, `check-units`, `test-zone-sync`, `make server`, `make lobby`.

- **test(wire): the air budget is measured and pinned, and the measurement corrected the prose.** `wow_wire_test` asserts the 1 kbit story from `sizeof`: 6-byte CAST payload, 80-byte envelope, 18-byte ACK, 98 bytes per successful cast, 240 bytes for a slow-preset give-up. At 125 B/s that is 784 ms and 1920 ms. The first run failed against my own table, which had assumed a 10-byte header -- `NetHeader` is 12 -- which is exactly why the numbers are now assertions instead of a paragraph. The numbers live in `docs/WOW_WIRE.md`; lane 9 of the report is measured but not trimmed, with a target (pack the header or drop the 64-byte padding; a compressor cannot win at 6 bytes). Verified: `make test-wow-wire` green plain and ASan/UBSan.

- **docs(wire): lane 6 of the next-steps report is closed** -- PARENA's stdlib no longer justifies integer ABIs with a limitation that was removed on 2026-11-03; the report now records the sweep and its grep-based done-when.

- **test(wire): a randomized decoder sweep under the sanitizers, and E2E ports that cannot collide.** `test-wow-wire` ends with 20,000 random buffers (length 0..96) through every decoder -- ack, cast, invoke, targets, server_rx -- each read from an exactly-sized heap buffer so an overread becomes an ASan report, with encoder caps smaller than the frames; plus 2,000 single-bit mutations of a valid cast asserting that a frame that still decodes agrees with its own bytes. Seed fixed, so failures reproduce. Separately, `test-wow-e2e` now defaults to `E2E_PORT=0`: it binds an ephemeral port, asks the OS for the number and hands it to the child, so a dev server on 6979 no longer makes the wire look broken (the run printed 56622, 33179 and 41544). Both close lanes 3 and 8 of `docs/WOW_WIRE_NEXT_STEPS.md`. Honest scope: a sweep, not a coverage-guided fuzzer. Verified: `make test-wow-wire` green plain and ASan/UBSan, `make test-wow-e2e` green on all three phases with ephemeral ports.

- **docs(world): `docs/MASTER_AFFORDANCE_LIBRARY.md` -- the script-to-prop register, with the engine column actually checked.** The pasted Builds 0183/0185 assumed `level_props.h`, `LevelPropInstance`, `fluid_state.h` and a `PROP_OFFICE_TABLE` id: none of them exists. What does exist is a working widget system whose JSON keys are `entity_id` / `machine_type` (1..8) / `memory_address` / `pins` / `box_index`, plus `--edit-map`, local NOCK, `make check-levels`, and generated rules behind `widget_runtime`/`widget_circuit`. The library keeps the canon and the trailer-first prop list, but every row now names a real engine path and a status (PLACE NOW / WIDGET / NEW SYSTEM / NOT AN ASSET), and it records that `LevelBox` has no rotation field, which blocks "table on its side" and the swivel stool before any art does. The pasted PARENA modules are marked as design notes that do not compile as written.

- **feat(wire): the cast range is per weapon, and measured in 3D.** `wow_cast_range_yd_for(WPN_*)` (PARENA) now decides reach: knife/hammer 2 yd, spray can 8, flashlight 15, shotgun 20, magnum 25, rifle 55, missile 90, sniper 120, unknown ids back to 40. `apps/server/src/main.c` converts the chosen value once with `units_author_length_m` and keeps comparing squared distances, so elevation is paid out of the same budget instead of being capped: 12 m up and 10 m away is 15.6 m and in range for a rifle, 40 m straight overhead is not. `make test-wow-wire` asserts the table, its ordering and its fallback by `WPN_*` name; `check-wow-wire-rules`, `test-units`, `check-units`, `test-wow-client`, the three E2E phases, `make lobby` and `make server` all green. Not verified: nobody has watched a shot refuse at range in game -- no display here.

- **test(wire): the retry path is proven under real ACK loss, not just duplication.** `--drop-cast-acks N` on the server (test-only, default 0) swallows the next N CAST ACKs; `make test-wow-e2e` now runs three phases on three ports -- 0 (happy), 2 (only the client's real re-send can recover), 99 (one abandonment with `last_abandoned_kind == WOW_OP_CAST`, nothing pending). This closes lane 1 of `docs/WOW_WIRE_NEXT_STEPS.md`, whose whole complaint was that no ACK had ever been dropped, so the resend was only ever exercised by calling `wow_client_retry` directly. The first run failed on a bug in the test itself: the final "cast was ACKed" assertion fired during the give-up phase, i.e. it asserted the exact thing that phase exists to disprove. Verified: `make test-wow-e2e` green on all three phases, `make test-wow-client`, `make test-wow-wire`, `make server`.

- **docs(squad): `docs/REDGARDEN_IN_SHANKPIT_NEXT_STEPS.md` -- the REDGARDEN/ECOWAR direction with the invented API surface stripped out.** The pasted blueprint was checked line by line against this tree before anything was written down: `SCENE_ECOWAR`, `PACKET_ECOWAR_MATCH_STATE`, `PACKET_JUNGLE_BUFF_STATE`, `PACKET_ARENA_CARD_PLAY`, `commander_ai_rules.prn`, `jungle_creep_rules.prn`, `pheromone_targeting.prn` and `rg_account.h` do not exist, REDGARDEN and ECOWAR are not even checked out here, and there is no lane/creep/hero simulation to port -- while the pheromone marker, brick/structure cascade, ragdolls, zone generator and 39 checked-in PARENA rules are real and are the actual seams. The doc keeps the direction (PARENA owns the rules, SHANKPIT owns the host) and orders six lanes from "clone and read the source" through a squad lane on `SCENE_CUSTOM_LEVEL`, creeps as tables over the existing AI, a zone-shaped state packet, hero assets, and match auth on the existing IDUNA ticket, each with a command-checkable done-when.

- **docs(wire): `docs/WOW_WIRE_NEXT_STEPS.md` -- the wire's real state and the ordered lanes after it.** The report separates what a command backs (`make test-wow-e2e` over real UDP, byte layouts, retry state machine, ticket MAC, the 40 yd range through the unit layer) from what has only ever compiled: no display and no IDUNA-issued ticket, so nothing has been seen and no real player has cast; the GFD/MUD side has no code because this box has no Go toolchain. Lanes, each with a "done when": inject ACK loss into the E2E (the resend path is currently only proven by calling `wow_client_retry` directly), per-weapon range with a vertical band, fuzz the decoders, the blocked GFD adapter, the visual pass, a comment-only sweep of the 13 PARENA stdlib sites that still claim "VS0 has no F32 params" (listed with file:line), a second consumer for the unit layer, E2E port hygiene, and a measured bytes-per-cast figure before trimming the 80-byte envelope on the 1 kbit preset.

- **feat(wire): end to end over a real UDP socket, a cast range in yards, and a give-up the player can see.** The named gap was that everything so far was driven in-process: `wow_server_rx()` agrees with itself, but no server, socket or ticket had ever been in the same test. New `packages/protocol/wow_e2e_test.c` (`make test-wow-e2e`, also in CI) forks the real `bin/shank_server` on a private port with `SHANKPIT_BAKED_LEVELS=1`, `--beacon-port 0` and `--fast-forward`, and drives it through loopback UDP: it retries CONNECT until WELCOME (a single CONNECT raced the server's own bind and every later packet was dropped as an unknown sender -- the first real bug this test found, in the test itself), proves a ticket signed with another secret is never ACKed, then authenticates with a real IDUNA-shaped ticket, sends a target intent, re-sends it and gets the idempotent re-ACK, retires the pending frame with that ACK, and sends a cast -- every ACK decoded off the wire, not returned by a function. Second, the cast's aim is now range-bounded and the range is a **rule in yards**: `wow-cast-range-yd` (40) is PARENA (`stdlib/shankpit/wow_wire.prn`), and `apps/server/src/main.c` converts it with `units_author_length_m(..., 5)` before comparing squared distances, so 36.576 is never restated in C and `packages/common/units_rules.c` finally has a real consumer (`SERVER_SRC` gained it). Past range the shot still fires along the player's own aim and the refusal is logged. Third, a frame that exhausts its retries is no longer only a log line: the lobby pushes `WOW: the server never acknowledged a <cast|target change|login ticket|macro> -- dropped after N tries` into the chat pane, with the kind taken from the abandoned frame (`WowClient.last_abandoned_kind`) rather than guessed. Verified: `make test-wow-e2e` green (real UDP, real server), `test-units` + `check-units`, `test-wow-wire`, `test-wow-client`, `test-wow-macros`, `test-wow-ticket`, `check-wow-wire-rules`, `make lobby`, `make server`. NOT verified: still no display and no real IDUNA-issued ticket, so the visual path and a genuine SSO login remain untested; the E2E server is a private instance, never a shared service.

- **feat(wire): retry and ACK -- a lost receipt is no longer a lost action, and the server is idempotent about it.** The wire had ACKs from the start and a client that ignored them, so a dropped datagram meant a dropped cast or target change with no recovery. `packages/protocol/wow_client.h` now keeps a bounded table (`WOW_CLIENT_PENDING` = 4: AUTH + two targets + one cast) of frames sent and not yet acknowledged: `wow_client_track` remembers one, `wow_client_ack` retires the one whose `kind`+`seq` the ACK names (so a late ACK cannot retire a newer frame), and `wow_client_retry` returns the oldest due frame **byte-identical** -- rebuilding it with a fresh sequence would be dropped by the receiver's own strictly-newer rule, which is the failure retransmission exists to avoid. Period and cap are PARENA's, not new constants: `scripts/gen_wow_wire_rules.sh` now also emits `wow_retry_max` (2 / 4 / 5 by preset) alongside `wow_ack_ms` (500 / 250 / 120 ms), and an exhausted frame returns `-2` and is dropped rather than retried forever. That only works against an idempotent receiver, so `wow_targets_apply` now answers **1** for "the sequence I already have" (re-ACK) and **0** only for a genuinely older one, and `wow_server_queue_cast` answers 2 for an already-accepted cast; silence on a resend would have stranded exactly the frame it took. The lobby is wired: `wow_net_send_tracked` sends and tracks, `wow_net_poll_retries` runs once per input pass, incoming `PACKET_WOW_WIRE` is decoded as an ACK, and a give-up is logged (`WOW_GIVEUP`) instead of vanishing. Verified: `make test-wow-client` plain + ASan/UBSan -- no resend before `wow_ack_ms`, the resend is byte-identical, an ACK retires the frame, a cleared frame is never retried, a duplicate ACK retires nothing, the retry cap fires at `wow_retry_max` and drops the frame, and against the real server a resent intent is re-ACKed with the target unchanged; `make test-wow-wire` pins the new `wow_targets_apply == 1` idempotence while a genuinely older intent still returns 0; `make check-wow-wire-rules` byte-identical; `make lobby` and `make server` link, `test-wow-macros` and `test-wow-ticket` stay green. NOT verified: no display, no running server, so this is driven in-process, and a cast abandoned after its cap is simply gone -- there is no application-level "your shot was dropped" message.

- **feat(units): F32 authoring entry points, and the layer written in `fn`/`case` now that the emitter accepts them.** `units_author_length_mm`/`length_m`/`box_m3`/`mass_kg`/`snap` take and return **F32** because the geometry they feed is F32; they are thin wrappers over the exact F64 core, so an authoring path cannot grow its own rounded copy of 25.4 and the only error is one narrowing on an already-correct number. `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/units.prn` was rewritten from nested `if` chains to `fn` + `case` (PARENA's C emitter gained both in the same session, in a separate commit) with the factors, round-trip assertions and codes unchanged -- `make check-units` regenerated a byte-different but behaviour-identical `units_rules.c`, and `make test-units` (plain + ASan/UBSan) still pins every exact factor plus the new F32 paths (12 ft = 3657.6 mm = 3.6576 m, 2x1x0.5 ft = 0.0283168 m3, 3 lb = 1.36077711 kg, a bad authoring code returns 0). NOT done: nothing in the engine consumes the authoring API yet -- `docs/UNITS.md` names it as the entry point for the next level/brick/renderer change.

- **feat(units): a metric/imperial unit affordance layer, generated from PARENA.** Lengths, masses and volumes now convert through one canonical unit per family (millimetre, milligram, cubic metre) with `units_convert(v, family, from, to)` for any pair, plus `units_box_volume_m3` (author in inches/feet/cm/yards), `units_box_mass_kg` (volume x density) and `units_snap` for the metric CMU/tile and imperial 16-inch/8-inch grids. The factors and every branch are `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/units.prn`, generated into `packages/common/units_rules.c` by `scripts/gen_units.sh` (`make regen-units` / `make check-units` fails on drift); `packages/common/units_rules.h` is the C contract and `docs/UNITS.md` the codes and the reasoning. The sketch this replaces was **not valid PARENA** and did not compile: the emitter rejects `F32` and `I64` (`unsupported return type symbol 'F32'`), `fn` is `defn`, `defenum` variants are `(Name)`, and the C emitter's `case` over I32 emits `case(u, ...)` which gcc rejects -- so this is F64 with checked I32 codes and nested `if`, the same tag convention `stdlib/pentest/scan.prn` already uses. Verified: `make test-units` plain + ASan/UBSan -- the exact by-definition factors (25.4 mm/in, 453592.37 mg/lb, 907.18474 kg/short ton, 16.387064 mL/cu in, 28.316846592 L/cu ft, 3.785411784 L/gal), metric<->imperial in all three families, a round trip through every code, bad codes refused rather than converted, box volume in feet and cm, concrete mass for a 2x1x0.5 ft form, and snapping on both grids including negatives and profile 0. `make check-units` clean. NOT done: nothing in the engine consumes the layer yet, and there is no force/pressure/weight distinction beyond mass.

- **feat(lobby): the client half of the macro wire, so a match actually sends targets, casts and a ticket.** Everything up to now was one-directional: the server decoded frames and fired casts, but the lobby never produced a `PACKET_WOW_WIRE` datagram at all, so nothing in a real session exercised the wire. `packages/protocol/wow_client.h` is the missing half -- `wow_client_target` returns 7 bytes **only when the slot changed** and `0` otherwise (holding a target costs no air), `wow_client_cast` always returns 6 bytes with a sequence advanced by PARENA's `wow_seq_next` (never 0, wraps), and `wow_client_auth` copies the 36-byte IDUNA ticket verbatim into the 38-byte AUTH frame. The lobby wires it: `net_send_wow_frame` beside the other fire-and-forget sends, `wow_net_begin_session()` per connect attempt (fresh mirror + AUTH if a ticket is held), `wow_net_sync_targets()` once per input pass, and `/cast` in a net match now **sends** the cast instead of arming the local trigger, because the server's cast queue is what fires there. The ticket comes from `/ticket <72 hex>` or `SHANKPIT_TICKET` and is kept as 36 raw bytes; that is a paste-in bridge, not SSO -- the client still has no HTTP client. `LOBBY_SRC` gained the generated `wow_wire_rules.c` because the client now calls `wow_seq_next`/`wow_entity_*` too. Verified: `make test-wow-client` (new, plain + ASan/UBSan) drives the client into the real server -- a change encodes with the right slot/entity, the same target sends nothing, clearing encodes `CLEAR_TARGET` and an empty slot sends nothing, a bad slot is refused, AUTH with a real ticket binds the player id, an authenticated change applies, a replayed older intent is dropped while the newer one survives, two casts queue FIFO with the weapon/token intact, and out-of-range weapon ids are refused; `make lobby` links, and `test-wow-wire`, `test-wow-ticket`, `test-hmac`, `test-aim`, `test-wow-macros` stay green. NOT verified: no client, display or running server here, so no frame was ever sent over a socket -- the encoding and the server's acceptance are tested, the transport is compiled.

- **feat(auth): IDUNA's player ticket is now verified on the game server, locally -- the fail-closed default has a real verifier behind it.** The previous `AUTH` frame was specified for a 46-char ticket string, which could not hold IDUNA's ticket at all: IDUNA mints **36 raw bytes** and hex-encodes them to 72 chars only for its JSON reply (`IDUNA/internal/http/handlers/shankpit_ticket.go`: `player_id` 16 || `expires_at` 4 LE || `HMAC-SHA256(secret, payload)[:16]`, 5-minute TTL). `AUTH` is now **38 bytes** carrying those 36 raw bytes (`WOW_WIRE_VERSION` 5), so the server does no hex parsing on the receive path, and `WowAuthFrame`/`wow_auth_*`/`WowAuthFn` all speak bytes with a fixed length instead of a NUL-terminated string. New `packages/protocol/wow_ticket.h` is IDUNA's layout, not a local one -- `wow_ticket_make` (tests only), `wow_ticket_uuid_text`, and `wow_ticket_verify(secret, len, ticket, now_unix, ...)` returning 1/0/-1, checking the MAC with a constant-time compare **before** the expiry so a forged ticket cannot probe the clock. The MAC itself is new C: `packages/common/hmac_sha256.h` (RFC 2104 over the existing `packages/goldenband/sha256.h`, key wiped after use), the path IDUNA's own comment already pointed at. `apps/server/src/main.c` installs the verifier at wire init from `SHANKPIT_TICKET_SECRET` and logs which happened; with no secret (or one too long for the buffer) it installs nothing and every op stays refused. Verified: `make test-hmac` -- RFC 4231 cases 1, 2, 3 and 6 match the published digests, and the constant-time compare distinguishes a one-bit difference; `make test-wow-ticket` -- a self-signed ticket verifies and its UUID binds, expiry `now+1` accepted / `now` refused / `now-1` refused, another secret's ticket refused, tampered player id and tampered MAC refused, malformed arguments return -1, and through the wire: AUTH refused with no verifier, accepted with a real one (player id bound, ACK'd), another secret's ticket and an expired ticket both refused leaving the client unauthenticated, an unauthenticated target still refused; `make test-wow-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan still passes with the binary ticket, `make server` and `make lobby` link. NOT verified: no ticket from a running IDUNA was presented -- IDUNA is not running here and no `SHANKPIT_TICKET_SECRET` exists in this environment, so the issuer side is matched by reading its code and the MAC is matched by RFC vectors, not by an end-to-end SSO handshake.

- **feat(server): a cast at `[@target]` / `[@focus]` aims at that entity, not just fires.** The previous slice drained the cast queue and pulled the trigger, but the shot still left along whatever yaw/pitch the player's crosshair happened to be on, so a macro's unit token was carried, evaluated and then discarded at the last step. `process_user_cmd` now resolves the token's slot in the client's target table to a simulation slot and points the caster at the target's eye with `tp_yaw_pitch_to_point` -- the **inverse** of `tp_dir_from_yaw_pitch`, which the third-person camera already used, so there is still exactly one statement of the engine's yaw/pitch convention. `tp_aim_yaw_pitch` was rewritten to call the new helper and keeps its own degenerate-aim fallback, so the camera path is behaviour-identical. Refused aims fall back to the player's own aim rather than slewing the crosshair: out-of-range entity, dead, not active, other scene, or self. Verified: `make test-aim` (new, plain + ASan/UBSan) round-trips a set of directions through aim -> forward and compares the recovered unit vector, and pins the convention independently (+X is yaw -90, -Z is yaw 0) so the two sides cannot flip together; `make test-third-person` still passes after the refactor, `make test-wow-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan, `make test-wow-macros`, `make server` and `make lobby` all green. NOT verified: still no client and no running match here, so the aim was never observed on screen -- the numbers are checked, the visual is not.

- **feat(server): a queued cast fires, on the client's own tick.** `process_user_cmd` (apps/server/src/main.c) now drains `wow_server_take_cast()` immediately after `shankpit_apply_usercmd_inputs`: the accepted weapon is equipped and `in_shoot` is set for that one command, so the shot is ordered against the client's movement like a mouse click and cannot fire from the receive path. The engine's own weapon gate still applies (a macro cannot equip a weapon the player does not own), and the cast is logged once per fire. Verified: `make server` and `make lobby` link, `make test-wow-wire` still passes plain + ASan/UBSan (queue FIFO, depth bound, auth, macro invoke). NOT verified: the cast -> fire path is compiled, not driven -- there is no client here to send a cast to a running server, and the unit token is carried but does not aim yet.

- **feat(server): PACKET_WOW_WIRE -- per-client targets, casts and macro bytecode on the server, behind IDUNA ticket auth.** `apps/server/src/main.c` now routes a new `PACKET_WOW_WIRE` (21, `NetHeader` + length + 64-byte body) through `wow_server_rx()` and ACKs what it accepts. Each client slot gets its own four-slot target table, up to 8 macro definitions by id and a cast queue bounded by PARENA's `wow_cast_queue` (2 / 1 / 3 by preset); `wow_server_take_cast()` is the FIFO the simulation drains. `[harm]` is evaluated against `WowServer.unit_truth`, installed by the simulation -- with no hook a target is "exists, unknown otherwise", so `[harm]` does not hold rather than guessing. **IDUNA SSO**: AUTH carries a 48-byte player TICKET (never the 4 KB JWT, which stays in the client) and verification is a `WowAuthFn`; the server starts fail-closed (`require_auth = 1`, no verifier) so every op is refused until the IDUNA call is installed, with the refusal logged once. **PARENA port**: the wire's arithmetic -- entity lo/hi/validity, definition size, sequence advance -- is now generated from `stdlib/shankpit/wow_wire.prn` (`wow_entity_lo/hi/valid`, `wow_def_bytes`, `wow_seq_next`) and used by both encoder and decoder, so the two cannot disagree; only the byte stores remain C, because PARENA has no arrays. Verified: `make test-wow-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan -- auth refused with no verifier, refused with a bad ticket, identity bound on a good one, unauthenticated client refused, duplicate target intent not acknowledged, casts queued/taken/bounded (third refused at depth 2), unknown weapon id refused, macro define + invoke resolves `[harm]` to a magnum at the target and falls through to `[@player] knife` when the target clears, undefined macro id refused. `make check-wow-wire-rules` byte-identical, `make lobby` and `make server` link. NOT built, named: nothing drains the cast queue in the tick loop yet (a queued cast is a validated intent, not a shot), the IDUNA verifier is not installed, and slots above 8 are ignored.

- **feat(protocol): a low-bandwidth macro/mod wire with multiple targets per client.** Text is an authoring format, not a wire format, so the compiled macro now has a byte form: `DEFINE_MACRO` (28 bytes for the two-clause example, once), `INVOKE_MACRO` (5 bytes per press), `CAST` (6 bytes, weapon id + unit token -- never a name) and a 7-byte target intent sent **only when the target changes**. `WowTargets` is a four-slot per-client table (target, focus, two mod-named) with an explicit presence bit per slot, and intents carry a per-client 16-bit sequence that the receiver applies only when strictly newer (wrap-safe), so a duplicated or reordered datagram cannot resurrect a stale target. Every number -- slot count, cast queue depth, clause cap, ack period, target resend period -- is PARENA (`stdlib/shankpit/wow_wire.prn` -> generated `wow_wire_rules.c`, `make check-wow-wire-rules`). `packages/ui/wow_mod.h` is the mod surface: macros registered by name+id with the text compiled at registration (refused if the compiler refuses it, duplicate name or id refused), and `/cast` aliases that can only name a weapon the engine actually has -- a table, not a VM, and `/cast` now consults it first. Verified: `make test-wow-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan -- the two-clause macro survives compile -> 28 bytes -> decode with every mask/token/argument equal, stale/duplicate/foreign-version/truncated intents are refused or dropped, unchanged targets send nothing, a second target slot round-trips, `trailing bytes` are a refusal, and the mod registry refuses duplicates, bad text and unknown weapons. `make check-wow-wire-rules` byte-identical, `make test-wow-macros` and `make lobby` still green. NOT built, named in `docs/WOW_WIRE.md`: no Go toolchain exists here, so the GoblinFoxDragon MUD adapter (a binary listener beside its line protocol) was not written -- GoblinFoxDragon is cloned at `MONOREPO/GoblinFoxDragon` and the adapter contract is specified instead of guessed.

- **feat(lobby): the WoW macro language now runs against the real actor pool.** `/cast [harm,nodead,@mouseover] magnum`, `/target`, `/focus`, `/cleartarget`, `/clearfocus`, `/startattack` and `/use <1-9>` dispatch through `wow_macro_parse`/`wow_macro_execute` from chat, from `shankpit_macros.cfg`, and from a bound key (binds still win over built-in keys). `[@mouseover]` reuses the guns' own eye ray and `check_hit_location` (nearest hostile); `[@player]` is the controlled local slot; `[harm]` is `phys_is_friendly`'s own rule. `/cast <weapon>` equips and pulls the trigger for the next input pass, with the name table **generated** from `packages/common/protocol.h` (`scripts/gen_wow_weapons.py` -> `packages/ui/wow_weapons.h`, `make check-wow-weapons` fails on drift), so `/cast magnum` and key 2 cannot disagree and an unknown name is refused. `/use 7|8|9` maps to flashlight/hammer/spraycan exactly as the number keys do. Named limits: local matches only (a net match answers "no unit" instead of guessing a slot), `[stealth]` never holds, target/focus are client-side and not on the wire, and a cast fires rather than queueing through a server-authoritative ability buffer. Verified: `make test-wow-macros` (weapon table uniqueness, case-insensitive names, slot 1..9 mapping incl. the trailing-comment WPN_ defines), `make check-wow-weapons`, and `make lobby` / `make server` compile clean. NOT verified: no display on this box, so key/chat dispatch was never exercised in a running client.

- **feat(ui): a WoW-style macro compiler, unit-token host contract, and .toc loader.** `packages/ui/wow_macro.h` compiles `/cast [harm,nodead,@mouseover] Blaster; [@player] Mend` once into at most four clauses of two `uint32` masks, a unit token and an argument -- evaluation is two ANDs and a bounds check, with the FIRST matching clause winning (WoW's rule). `no<word>` is a FORBID bit rather than a special case, bracket groups accumulate, and `[@none]` is a real token so `[noexists]` needs no second path. Malformed text is rejected with a specific code (-1 unknown command ... -6 line too long), never reinterpreted. `packages/ui/wow_api.h` is the host vtable (`cast_spell`, `set_target`, `unit_exists`, ...) that makes text macros and an add-on's `CastSpellByName` the same call, plus a declarative table of 31 WoW globals with arity and what backs each today (`host` / `frame` / `todo`). `packages/ui/addon_toc.h` parses `##` metadata and orders add-ons by required dependency with named failures -- `-1` not installed, `-2` cycle -- instead of `exit()` or an alphabetical guess. Verified: `make test-wow-macros` -- canonical line compiles to the right bits/tokens/args, first-match-wins on live/dead/no target, `[noexists]`, `[@none]`, multi-bracket accumulation, every rejection code, execution through the host vtable (cast/target/use/clear), the contract table (unique names, sane arity, no invented globals), `.toc` parsing and load order, cycle and missing-dependency detection. NOT built, named in `docs/WOW_MACRO_API.md`: no Lua VM (deliberately not vendored), no XML frames, no event bus, no combat log framing, no secure templates, and the client does not yet dispatch through this path.

- **feat(protocol): a ranged "everything since tick N" catch-up request.** `TICK` only heals one lost tick; a client that faded for a minute had to walk the ring a tick at a time. `RANGE` (request kind 2, same 8-byte frame, tick = the first tick wanted) answers from the ring, oldest first **ordered by tick number** (the ring wraps, so position is not air order), capped by `lora-range-max-ticks` from PARENA (4 / 2 / 6 by preset) and by the caller's frame slots -- and a tick that does not fit whole is not sent at all, because half a tick is worse than none. The answer is a `LoraRangeResult`: `ticks`, `frames`, `first_tick`, `more` (ask again from `first_tick + ticks`) and `gap` (the asked-for tick has fallen out of the ring -- only a snapshot can cover it). Like `TICK`, a range spends no snapshot budget, which is asserted. Verified: `make test-lora-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan -- two ticks served oldest first with the right record counts, the LONG_SLOW cap stopping at 2 with `more` set, the resumed ask serving tick 102 alone, a range older than the ring reporting `gap` while serving everything it still has, a range past the newest tick returning -1 rather than an empty success, a one-slot range serving exactly one tick, and a snapshot still allowed immediately after a range. `make check-lora-rules` byte-identical. NOT built, named: persistence across host restarts.

- **feat(protocol): a global airtime gap under the per-client snapshot budget.** The per-client gate alone still let eight clients pull eight full-state frames back to back; on LoRa that is the whole evening's air spent on resync. `SNAPSHOT` is now gated twice: per client in the 8-slot table, and **globally** by `lora-snapshot-global-ms` from PARENA (4000 at LONG_FAST, 8000 at LONG_SLOW, 2000 at SHORT_TURBO) -- inside that gap *nobody* gets a snapshot, and a blocked global check **spends nothing** from the asker's own allowance, so a client that had to wait for the air is not charged twice. `TICK` resends remain outside both gates. Verified: `make test-lora-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan -- a second client asking at the same instant as the first is refused by the global gap, served once the gap has passed, then refused inside its own per-client budget; six more clients at spaced times fill the table, a ninth distinct asker still gets -1, and the global-gap numbers match the PARENA table. `make check-lora-rules` byte-identical after regeneration. NOT built, named: a ranged "everything since tick N" request, and persistence across host restarts.

- **feat(protocol): the snapshot budget is per client, in an 8-slot table that never evicts.** The gap the doc named: a host-wide budget let one client spend everybody's snapshot. `LoraSnapshotPump` is now a bounded table keyed by asking entity -- allowed (its own clock moves), wait (inside its own interval), or refused when the table is full -- and it deliberately **never evicts**, because eviction would let a rotating client reset everyone else's clock and spend the whole air. The honest consequence is written into the doc rather than hidden: the worst case is **8 snapshots per interval**, one every 3.75 s at LONG_FAST, and a **global airtime cap** on top is still not built. `TICK` resends remain outside the budget entirely. Verified: `make test-lora-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan -- a second client is served while the first is still inside its budget, that second client is then refused inside its own, six more fill the table (8 total), a ninth distinct asker gets -1, and every earlier case still passes (same-client waiting, budget expiry, tick resends unaffected, null args and zero slots). `make check-lora-rules` unchanged. NOT built, named: a global airtime cap above the per-client gate, a ranged "since tick N" request, and persistence across host restarts.

- **feat(protocol): a lost tick can be resent from an 8-tick history ring -- the cheap half of catch-up.** A full snapshot costs 2-6 frames and is budgeted; a single missed tick costs one. The request frame therefore grew to **8 bytes** (still marked 3; a delta is 2) and carries a tick, with two kinds: `SNAPSHOT` (budgeted host-wide, answers `0` for "wait") and **`TICK`** -- "resend tick N" -- served straight out of `LoraHistory`, a ring of the last 8 ticks holding each tick's records, and deliberately **not** charged against the snapshot budget, because turning a lost tick into a lost world is what the ring exists to prevent. A tick older than the ring is a hard -1, so the client asks for a snapshot instead of receiving a partial replay that would silently diverge. Verified: `make test-lora-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan -- an 8-byte request round-trips with its tick, a 7-byte one is refused, a delta frame is still refused as a request, an unknown kind is refused, `SNAPSHOT` still budgets (refused at budget-1ms, served at the budget), a stored tick resends as exactly one frame whose records are byte-identical to the ones recorded, a **second** tick resend is served immediately (proving it is outside the snapshot budget), a tick outside the ring returns -1, the newest tick survives, the oldest does not, a negative capacity is refused, and null request/world/history plus zero frame slots all return -1. `make check-lora-rules` unchanged (no rules this slice). NOT built, named: per-client snapshot fairness, a ranged "since tick N" request (the client walks the ring a tick at a time), and persistence across host restarts.

- **feat(protocol): a client can ask for the world -- a 6-byte request, served under a host-wide snapshot budget.** The last gap the LoRa doc named. A request frame is its own shape, not a delta: first byte **3** where a delta's is **2**, so a receiver tells them apart before parsing; then kind, the asking entity and the asker's sequence number -- 6 bytes, because a request is the cheapest thing on the air. `lora_host_request` serves it through `lora_world_snapshot`, chunked like any tick, and gates it on `lora-snapshot-interval-ms` from PARENA (30000 at LONG_FAST, 60000 at LONG_SLOW, 15000 at SHORT_TURBO), answering **0 = wait** rather than silence. The budget is deliberately **host-wide**: the air is shared, and pretending to a per-client fairness table that does not exist would be worse than naming it. Verified: `make test-lora-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan -- request round trip, a 5-byte request refused, a delta frame refused as a request (marker check), an unknown kind refused, the first request producing 2 frames for 40 actors, a second request refused *one millisecond* before the budget and served exactly at it, and null request / null world / zero frame slots all returning -1. `make check-lora-rules` byte-identical after regeneration. NOT built, named: the budget is per host rather than per client, and there is no "what did I miss since tick N" request -- only "give me the world".

- **feat(protocol): a full-state snapshot and a resend policy with a retry budget -- the loss half of the LoRa slice.** The doc's own first open question, now code. **Snapshot frames** reuse the v2 delta framing but every record is `STATE`: x and y packed into one uint16 and hp in `extra`, so a whole actor costs **one** record -- 64 actors is 2 frames, reassembled by the same chunk code, and the snapshot does **not** advance the tick because it resolves nothing. **`lora_client_send/ack/due`** are the resend policy: an intent is queued, cleared only by an ACK matching `(entity, seq)` (two clients may share sequence numbers, so entity is part of the key), resent after `lora_resend_ms(preset)`, and **dropped after `lora_max_sends(preset)`** so a dead link is reported on the next tick instead of retried forever into congestion. Both numbers are PARENA's (`lora-resend-ms`, `lora-max-sends`: 8000ms/3 sends at LONG_FAST, 20000ms/4 at LONG_SLOW, 5000ms/3 at SHORT_TURBO), so the radio's airtime owns the policy and the client owns no clock -- `due()` takes `now_ms` as an argument. Verified: `make test-lora-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan -- a 64-actor snapshot splits into 2 frames with counts 38/26, reassembles to 64 records whose packed x/y/hp match the world, an empty world yields one empty frame, a null world and a zero-slot call return -1; the client does not resend before the window, resends exactly at it, ignores an ACK for another entity, clears on its own ACK, never resends a cleared intent, drops after the retry budget with the queue empty afterwards, refuses a 17th outstanding intent, and returns nothing for an unknown seq or a zero-capacity call. `make check-lora-rules` byte-identical after regeneration; `test-macros`, `test-stream-filters`, `test-garage-rules`, `lobby` and `server` still green. NOT built, named: the snapshot REQUEST side (a client cannot yet ask for a full state at a chosen tick), the Meshtastic transport, client pathing/interpolation, assets and UI.

- **feat(protocol): the LoRa tick can span frames, and every intent comes back acknowledged -- delta v2.** Continuing the same day's LoRa slice, with the two questions its own doc named. A 64-actor world resolves up to 192 records, which is 6 frames of 38, so the delta header grew to **v2**: version, per-frame count, tick, **chunk index, chunk count** (6 bytes; 38 records = 234 bytes, still inside the 237-byte Meshtastic payload). `lora_frames_build` splits one resolved record list into whole frames and **writes nothing when the caller's frame slots are too few**; `lora_reassembly_put` receives them and stays incomplete (0) until every chunk is in, rejecting a duplicate chunk, a foreign tick or a contradictory chunk count (-1) rather than letting a receiver act on half a tick. **ACK records** (kind 5) carry the intent's sequence number for every ACCEPTED action -- a swing that missed and a move to the tile you are already on are acknowledged too, and an entity that is not in the world is not -- so a client resends only what was never acknowledged. The 16-actor cap became **64**, because the old cap made multi-frame machinery unreachable and a camp world is bigger than a duel. All-or-nothing got stronger: both `lora_world_tick` and `lora_world_tick_frames` resolve into a **copy** of the world and commit only after the frames exist, proven by memcmp on a refused 40-record tick. Verified: `make test-lora-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan -- v1 frames refused by a v2 decoder, chunk 3-of-1 refused, 80 records split into 3 frames with counts 38/38/4 and reassembled in order, duplicate/missing/foreign-tick chunks refused, a two-frame build refused when given one slot, move+ack and no-op+ack and hit (damage+xp+ack) and miss (ack only) all observed, a two-tile attack acknowledged without damage, 64 actors accepted and the 65th refused, determinism, and a 40-record tick refused in one frame with the world byte-identical then accepted as 2 frames. `make check-lora-rules` still byte-identical (the rules did not change). NOT built, named: the resend policy itself (how long, how many, and whether a full-state frame exists to resync a client that lost everything), the Meshtastic transport, client pathing/interpolation, assets, UI, and any radio in the loop.

- **feat(protocol): the LoRa RPG's wire and tick -- a 12-byte intent, a 232-byte delta frame, and a deterministic resolver that refuses a tick it cannot fit.** Founder (2026-11-03): *"how do we evolve the engine for a low bandwidth runescape classic style rpg that can run over meshtastic"*. Meshtastic carries ~237 bytes per frame with airtime in seconds, so 60Hz replication does not degrade into it -- it stops existing. This slice is the part that is code: `packages/protocol/lora_wire.h` takes **intents** (sequence, entity, action, arg, target tile, target entity -- 12 bytes, hand-packed little-endian because C bitfields are implementation-defined and gcc/msvc would put different bytes on the air) and resolves them **once per tick** into a **delta** frame (4-byte header + 6-byte records, 38 records = 232 bytes, inside the ceiling). The design paste's "checksum/salt" is dropped: LoRa already carries a CRC and a second one is a wasted byte. Melee uses Chebyshev adjacency and the swing is an FNV-style hash of `(attacker, target, tick)` -- no `rand()`, no clock -- so a replay grades identically and two peers can check each other; the test proves two identical ticks produce identical bytes. A tick that would exceed one frame returns **-1 and changes nothing** (proven by memcmp of the world), so the caller splits or defers instead of shipping half a tick. `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/lora_rules.prn` -> `packages/protocol/lora_rules.c` (`scripts/gen_lora_rules.sh`, `make regen-/check-lora-rules`) holds the numbers as total I32 functions over the Meshtastic modem preset: LONG_FAST 5000ms/600‰/3dmg/10xp, LONG_SLOW 12000ms/600‰/4dmg/12xp, SHORT_TURBO 3000ms/550‰/3dmg, unknown preset falls back to LONG_FAST. Verified: `make test-lora-wire` plain + ASan/UBSan -- round trip for every action, 11-byte frame / action 9 / x=64 all refused, delta header+records read back, kind 0 and kind COUNT refused, the 38-record ceiling and the exact 232-byte filled frame, truncated and version-2 and over-capacity frames refused, move/no-op/miss/hit all observed with damage and xp matching PARENA, a two-tile attack refused, determinism, oversized tick refused with the world unchanged, and every generated number checked against the header's expectations. `make check-lora-rules` is byte-identical. NOT built, named: the Meshtastic transport itself (serial/BLE, portnum, the retransmission and replay window a lossy link needs), client A* pathing and interpolation, assets, UI, and any radio in the loop -- none of that was invented here, and the doc lists the three questions the next slice has to answer.

- **feat(macros): every setting is now a command, bindable to a key -- `shankpit_macros.cfg`, WoW-style, no Lua.** Founder (2026-11-03): *"the settings need to be macroable ... like when streamers and speedrunners jump into the nether they jump into settings to adjust brightness this needs to be macroable ... everything needs to be macroable via WOW like lua macros"*. Audit: there was no keybind system, no command parser and no macro of any kind in the lobby, so this is new and deliberately small. `packages/ui/macro.h` (header-only, allocation-free, no SDL) is the **format**: `bind SPEC command; command`, `#` comments, 32 binds, 192-byte bodies, one bind per key (a rebind replaces, never races), and a malformed line is reported rather than reinterpreted. The **commands** live in the lobby and cover everything a menu can do: `stream.grade off|crisp|cinematic|gritty|retro|+|-`, `stream.detail full|light|toggle`, `stream on|off`, `ambient 0..10|+|-`, `bullet.holes`, `brick.debris`, `fullscreen`, `broadcast off|auto|manual`, plus `say`, `bind` (writes the file), `macro <KEY>` and `help`. **The same text runs from chat with a leading `/`**, so a macro is tested by typing it before it is bound; a bind is checked **before** the built-in shortcuts, so a player who binds F6 gets F6. First run writes `shankpit_macros.cfg` (gitignored) with live `ALT+1`/`ALT+2` binds, because a macro feature that ships invisible is not a feature. Verified: `make test-macros` passes headless -- comments and blank lines, malformed lines refused, a spec with no body refused, case-insensitive lookup and rebind replacement, `;` splitting with empty pieces dropped, save/load roundtrip including a multi-command body, a 33rd bind refused rather than overwriting, an overlong body truncated to the budget, and a too-small save buffer refused; `make lobby` and `make server` build; a headless lobby run creates the cfg then reloads it on the next run and logs the bind count; `make test-stream-filters` and `make check-filter-rules` still green. NOT verified, named: key dispatch and the chat path are not exercised headless (no synthetic key events), so a real keypress has not been observed here; `say` is local chat-pane text, not a network send, because the chat submit path itself is local-only; and there are no variables, conditionals or waits -- a macro is a list, not a script.

- **feat(settings): the program-feed grade is a player setting -- OFF by default -- in the lobby SETTINGS page and the pause menu, with a LIGHT detail level for low-end machines.** Founder (2026-11-03): *"wire all of this into player settings so speedrunners and low end specs can adjust this stuff"*. The audit found the surface already existed and did not need inventing: `shankpit_display.cfg` plus the SETTINGS page (`SettingsAction`) and the pause menu. So the chain is now `STREAM GRADE: OFF/CRISP/CINEMATIC/GRITTY/RETRO` and `STREAM DETAIL: FULL/LIGHT` in **both** menus -- the pause menu is where a speedrunner actually is mid-run. **OFF is the default and costs nothing**: `g_stream_filters_ready` stays 0, `filter_apply` is not called and the capture path is byte-for-byte what it was before the post stack. **LIGHT** keeps levels/gamma/saturation/vignette/grain and drops the 3x3 kernel -- the only pass that reads neighbours -- so a weak CPU can still have a look. Both persist as two appended lines in `shankpit_display.cfg`; an older 5-line file loads unchanged and keeps OFF/FULL, and `SHANKPIT_STREAM_FILTER` is now only the startup default, because a streamer should not need an env var. The chain is rebuilt only when the setting changes (`stream_grade_apply`), never per frame. Verified: `make test-stream-filters` passes plain + ASan/UBSan with a new check that dropping the kernel leaves a frame's bytes identical while the sharpened version changes them, `make check-filter-rules` is byte-identical, `make lobby` and `make server` build, and a headless lobby run loads the cfg and logs `stream grade: ...` without touching a frame when OFF. NOT verified, named: nobody has watched a graded stream (no display; ffmpeg still not installed); the LIGHT path's actual frame-time saving is unmeasured; the pause-menu layout shifted to 10 items at 30px (400 -> 130) to fit the new row and that spacing has not been eyeballed.

- **feat(render): the program feed gets a post chain -- levels, gamma, a real 3x3 kernel, vignette and deterministic grain -- before it reaches the encoder.** Founder real-time (2026-11-03), moving off Unity/Unreal/OBS: *"in OBS they have the different effects for color correction sharpness etc thats really useful ... i want to build out those filters"*. Audit first: `packages/render/stream_out.h` already sends the clean program feed out of F2 as raw rgb24, and there was **no post stack of any kind** -- not the paste's `stream_filters.h`/`.c`, no curves, no kernels -- so every stream looked exactly like the viewport and grading meant an external OBS. `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/filter_rules.prn` -> `packages/render/filter_rules.c` (`scripts/gen_filter_rules.sh`, `make regen-/check-filter-rules`) is five presets in fixed-point I32: **NEUTRAL, CRISP, CINEMATIC, GRITTY, RETRO**, each a 3x3 kernel + divisor + levels black/white + gamma permille + saturation permille + vignette + grain. `packages/render/stream_filters.h` is the chain, applied **between glReadPixels and `stream_out_push`** in the lobby's capture block: levels+gamma through a 256-byte LUT built once per preset change (never per pixel), then saturation around integer luma, then the convolution, then vignette, then grain. The convolution keeps **two stack scanline copies and allocates nothing per frame** (the paste's own "harder" note is right: a malloc in the capture path is a frame-time bug), never writes a border byte so a sharpened frame has no dark rim, and **refuses** a feed wider than 2048 rather than overrunning. Grain is a hash of `(x, y, seed)`, never a clock or `rand()`, so a recording replays identically. Neutral is structural, not a preset number: a hand-built neutral config is also free and bit-identical. Verified: `make test-stream-filters` (plain + ASan/UBSan) -- neutral leaves a frame byte-identical and reports no work; levels 16..235 send 16->0, 235->255, 128->130; gamma 2000 of 128 is 64/65 and 500 is ~180 with both endpoints pinned; the OBS sharpen matrix on a hand-built image puts the bright pixel at 255 and its neighbour at 0 while the border stays untouched; a flat image survives the 1-2-1 blur unchanged; an over-wide frame is refused; saturation 0 greys (10,200,60) to 126 (the exact integer luma); vignette leaves a real centre pixel at 200 and darkens the corner; two same-seed grain passes match, a different seed does not; and the generated PARENA table equals the header defaults for **every** preset and parameter. `make check-filter-rules` is byte-identical, `make lobby` builds. NOT verified, named: nobody has watched a graded stream (no display, and ffmpeg is not installed here, so the encoder path is still unrun); the chain runs on the CPU over ~2.7 MB/frame, fine for a 30 fps 1280x720 feed but not timed here; authorable curve control points and a real .cube 3D LUT are NOT built -- levels+gamma is the honest subset, and the LUT struct is where a curve table goes next.

- **test(salvage): the vehicle packet observed on a real wire — and the garage bay can see a car at all.** Two named gaps closed, one of them a genuine blocker. **The bay:** plates were fed only from bodies on FOOT, so a car parked on a garage `PLATE` read as EMPTY — no garage could exist no matter what the rules said. `lobby_widgets_tick` now puts the scene's buggies into the same actor array (half players, half cars), and `garage.h` gained the three-state answer a host needs, `garage_bay_verdict(occupied, origin, objective)` -> empty / takes it / refuses it, plus `garage_bay_value` which returns **0 for a refused car** rather than quietly pricing it. Verified in `make test-garage-rules`. **The wire, for real:** `scripts/vehicle_state_e2e.py` starts `bin/shank_server`, connects over UDP exactly as a client does, asks the server for a buggy through `PACKET_KIT_CALL`, and asserts what arrives: a `PACKET_VEHICLE_STATE` exists, every entry's mask fits its kind (4 parts for a buggy, 5 for a rocket car), a part marked OFF holds 0 hull points, the parts sum to at most the 300 hull, and across two packets the same car keeps its slot and kind while **no part's hull ever grows** — the monotonic property that makes a late or reordered packet harmless. sizeof/offsetof come from a compiled probe of the real `protocol.h`, never a hand-copied number. The first run failed and taught me the harness was wrong, not the packet: it stopped after three datagrams and `WELCOME` + snapshots filled that before the vehicle packet could appear. The binary also RAN for the first time (`SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`, survived the 8 s cap, loaded a level, spawned the author-placed roster, and degraded every GL pass to a fallback instead of dying). `make test-vehicle-state-e2e` wired into CI. NOT verified, named: the e2e proves the packet on a deathmatch server whose match scene has cars only because the test asks for one — it does not play a match; loot is still client-local; and the garage still has no objective to serve, so the verdict is computed and shown nowhere in the UI.

- **feat(garage): a car remembers where it came from, a bay decides what that is worth — and the lobby finally builds on this box.** S4 of the salvage northstar, plus the two host-build gaps it exposed. **Provenance:** `LevelVehicleSpawn` gains `origin` (civilian / stolen / military, a word or a number in the level JSON, clamped so a typo becomes a clean car rather than a rare-parts payout) and it is written back **only when it is not civilian**, so every level authored before this round-trips byte-identically; it travels through `physics.h`'s own setter (its own signature, so the existing spawn callers were untouched) and lands in `BuggyState.origin` at `scene_load`, beside `rocket`/`tank`. **The decision is PARENA's:** `stdlib/shankpit/garage_rules.prn` -> `packages/simulation/garage_rules.c` (`scripts/gen_garage_rules.sh`, `make regen-/check-garage-rules`): `garage-accepts` over ANY / REBUILD / DISAPPEAR / FENCE and `garage-pays` in permille — civilian 1000, stolen 700 (no papers to sell with it), military 1300 (the parts are worth more than the car), anything undefined 0. `packages/simulation/garage.h` owns the defaults and `garage_value(hull, origin)`; both hosts install the table at startup. The garage takes the OBJECTIVE AS AN ARGUMENT and never invents one, because `mission.h` still has no vehicle-aware objective to hand it. Verified: `make test-garage-rules` — every (origin, objective) pair hand-checked, then the **generated PARENA table compared with the header's defaults for every pair, invalid ones included** (a drift would mean two different cars being sold); `make check-garage-rules` byte-identical. **And the lobby compiles here for the first time.** It had never built on this box — no SDL2 at all. With the SDL2/GL/mbedtls dev packages, an `EDITOR.GAME` checkout and PELLEGRINO's own vendored mbedtls (the system package lacks `mbedtls_memory_buffer_alloc_*`, which the phone browser needs), **two real repo bugs** surfaced: `dw_play.c` was missing from `LOBBY_SRC` although `main.c` calls eleven `dw_play_*` functions, and the link had no route to mbedtls at all when PELLEGRINO's fetched tree is absent (now `PGB_MBED_LIBS`, used only in that case). So every lobby line from the last three entries — the shard hook, the loot hook, the walk-over pickup, the `PACKET_VEHICLE_STATE` apply, the garage install — is now **compiled**, not merely reviewed. `make server` and `make lobby` both build; `make test-vehicle-salvage`, `make test-buggy-wall`, `make test-vehicle-burn`, `make check-vehicle-salvage-rules` all still green. NOT verified, named: nobody has LAUNCHED the binary (it needs a display); the garage's bay-side trigger (a car standing on a `PLATE` calling `garage_accepts`) is not wired, because there is no vehicle-aware objective in the mission layer to pass it; loot is still client-local.
- **feat(salvage): the parts reach other players, and what comes off them lands on the ground.** S3 + S5 of the salvage northstar, the wiring that makes the component table something a match can actually see. **`PACKET_VEHICLE_STATE` (type 20, the id after `PACKET_KIT_CALL`'s 19)** carries `(slot, kind, alive mask, detached mask, per-component hp, seed)` for every car in the recipient's scene, absolute and idempotent exactly like `PACKET_BRICK_STATE`: applying one twice, late or out of order cannot desync, and a late joiner heals on the next send with no acknowledgement traffic. Why a packet at all, given `NetBuggy` already sends the hull: **the component split is not a function of the hull** — a 240-hull car can be intact-but-worn or missing an engine depending on the order its hits landed in — so a client deriving parts from replicated health would be guessing and would show a part off a tick before the server agreed. hp crosses as **absolute points in a byte, never a fraction**: every component is at most 120, and a fraction would round, making the client's component sum differ from the hull it was sent, which the client's own reconcile would read as damage and invent. Masks are untrusted and clipped by `vehicle_salvage_set_state`, which also forces hp and mask to agree, and returns the hull the state holds so the client writes back the same number the server has. **The ground half** (`packages/simulation/vehicle_salvage_loot.h`): a part that comes off drops where it fell, and the four tiers map onto four **existing** consumables — the overclocked logic board is the KEY item, composite mesh a ration pack, rebar an energy bar, scrap a coffee — the placeholder mapping the spec asked for rather than a new inventory, with the drop still carrying the tier so a real salvage economy can reinterpret it without touching the wire. Walk over it to pick it up (the arcade convention `food_pickup.h` already uses), it rots after three minutes, and a full pool discards the OLDEST drop rather than the one it just produced. Client-local, the same honest contract `bioslurry.h` states. Verified: `make test-vehicle-salvage` (plain + ASan/UBSan) — the wire round trip returns the exact hull and every part byte (no quantization loss), a hostile 0xFF mask on a four-part car is clipped to 0xF, hp over a part's max is clamped to it, a part the wire says is off holds 0 even when its hp byte lies, `sizeof(NetVehicleEntry) == 16`, `PACKET_VEHICLE_STATE == 20`, the protocol's component/entry caps still match the table and `MAX_BUGGIES`, loot maps to the four items with strictly rising value, a drop is refused from the wrong scene or 30 units away and picked up at 1 unit, and the full pool keeps its newest part. **`make server` builds clean** with the new broadcast and packet — the authority really sends it. `make test-buggy-wall`, `make test-vehicle-burn` and `make check-vehicle-salvage-rules` still green. NOT verified, named: `apps/lobby/src/main.c` still cannot be compiled on this box (no `SDL2/SDL.h`), so the client's apply handler, its loot install and its pickup loop are **reviewed, not compiled**, and no one has seen the packet arrive or a part be picked up; loot is not authoritative (a networked player's drops are their own); and the garage/provenance slice (S4) is untouched.
- **feat(salvage): the parts now own the car — a sheared part falls off as a real body, and a car with no engine drives like one.** S2 of the salvage northstar (`docs2/specs/SALVAGE_AND_COMPONENT_DESTRUCTION_NORTHSTAR.md`), the slice that stops the component table from being a description. `vehicle_salvage.h` gains `vehicle_salvage_sync(v, hull, &fresh)`: the authority's hull is not this layer's private property (a shot, a repair and `vehicle_burn.h`'s own 0.25/tick drain all move it), so once per tick the real hull is handed in and the parts are reconciled **in both directions** — down is the proportional split; up refills only what is still ATTACHED, so a car welded back from a wreck cannot grow a second engine and a repaired wreck is capped at what it still has. `sync` returns the hull the car can actually represent and the tick writes **that** back, which is what makes `sum(parts) == hull` true on every tick instead of only while the number falls. `physics.h` gains `phys_set_buggy_salvage_permille` — four channels beside `g_phys_buggy_rules`, not inside it, because the rules are one mod for every car while a missing engine is per-car state — multiplied into top/reverse speed, drive force, turn rate, steering authority and lateral grip, with **exact passthrough at 1000** so an intact car and every existing physics test are bit-identical. `local_game.h`'s `buggy_tick_all` adopts each car's parts on first sight (by identity, not by a signal), sets the fold before that car's own simulate call, and after the fire ladder throws every part that came off on **this** tick through a host hook; the lobby binds that hook to the brick-body world (`packages/simulation/vehicle_salvage_shards.h`: one body per part, the component's `BF_KIND_*` material, nominal mass, seeded tumble), the dedicated server leaves it NULL because it deliberately keeps no broken-brick bodies. A car first seen already damaged adopts its parts **without** throwing shards — nobody saw them go. Verified: `make test-vehicle-salvage` (ASan+UBSan, now linking the real `brick_bodies`/`grb` path) — sync conserves both ways; a 240-point hit wears all four parts to 18/24/12/6 and detaches none, because the split is proportional and a part leaves only when the hit is more than the car can absorb; hull 0 parts the whole car exactly once; a repaired wreck is capped at its attached hull; a chassis shard is one 320 kg concrete body spawned at the right offset, and an unbound host spawns nothing. `make test-buggy-wall` asserts the fold (300/600 really reach the drive force and top speed, the engine costs no grip, the wheels do) and the exact 1000 passthrough. `make check-vehicle-salvage-rules` is byte-identical. **`make server` builds clean** with the new tick and install — the server compiles `local_game.h`, so the wiring is genuinely compiled, not just reviewed. NOT verified, named: `apps/lobby/src/main.c` cannot be compiled on this box (no `SDL2/SDL.h`), so its three lines (rules install, brick-world bind, hook) are **reviewed, not compiled**, and nobody has watched a part fall; and since component state is not on the wire yet, a networked client derives parts from the replicated hull, so it can show a part off a tick before the server agrees — S5's `PACKET_VEHICLE_STATE` is what removes that guess.
- **feat(salvage): a car is five parts that add up to its hull — and the sum is an invariant, not a hope.** Founder: garage of "vehicles of questionable origin", cars *"converted down to components and its a bit variable like wow disenchanting"*. The premise first: a car is ONE `int health` (`BuggyState.health`) and `vehicle_burn.h` already turns that into smoke -> fire -> detonation, so "the engine is gone" was a story the damage number told rather than a fact. `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/vehicle_salvage_rules.prn` is the missing table, fixed-point `I32` and total like its neighbours: five components per car (engine, chassis, wheels, logic, tank — `TANK` last so a four-part car still has a valid bitmask), hp summing to **exactly 300** (buggy 90/120/60/30, rocket 80/100/50/30/40), `BF_KIND_*` materials so a broken logic board shatters as glass, integer local offsets, `handling_permille` per channel (engine off = 300 drive force / 600 top speed; wheels = 400 grip; the logic board costs handling nothing), and the disenchant table: four tiers, only the engine and the logic board able to roll tier 3, **quantity scaled by the part's remaining condition** rather than by luck. `packages/simulation/vehicle_salvage.h` owns the two things the rules cannot: a **hull-conserving split** (proportional integer shares, then the leftovers of an already-gone part land on the next live part in authoring order — never dropped, never duplicated) and a roll hashed from `(entity, tick, component)` reusing `vehicle_burn_hash`, so two clients watching the same wreck compute the same loot and the server has no private randomness to leak. Verified: `make check-vehicle-salvage-rules` (regen byte-identical) and `make test-vehicle-salvage` — parts sum to the hull, 120 damage lands 36/48/24/12, a part comes off once and never absorbs damage again, **400 random hits conserve the hull exactly**, the handling stack multiplies (engine+wheels = 240 drive), the tier table is hand-checked at every boundary, and the same car rolls identically while still spreading across 40 ticks; clean at `-Werror` and under ASan+UBSan. NOT done, named: nothing is wired into `BuggyState`, no shards, no loot entities, no `origin_tag`, no wire — the next slice, not this one.
- **feat(roblox): a guest account becomes a real one — LINK EMAIL, on IDUNA's `guest-upgrade`, keeping the same player.** Continue of the same day's sign-in work. A guest is deliberately throwaway (no email, no recovery), so the lobby now has a LINK EMAIL row: `login460.link_email` calls `POST /api/v1/games/shankpit/guest-upgrade` with the player's own token as the Bearer, and IDUNA adds credentials to the SAME `players` row — `player_id` unchanged, so seats and any history stay theirs — answering with a fresh player token whose subject is `email:<uuid>`. New `login460.email_sign_in` is the returning half (`email-login`), which is what makes the account portable: signed in from a token this place never saw issued. The password is sent once, used by IDUNA, and never stored here or by the place; `to_save` carries `player_id` + guest secret + email and is asserted to contain **no token and no password**. Client: two TextBoxes and a LINK EMAIL button (panel grew to 470 px), with the linked address shown in the identity line. Verified: `make test-roblox` — **net460 27, queue460 50, ticket460 32, login460 58 checks, 0 failures** (login460 36 -> 58 with the link and email checks), `luac -p` clean, model 9 instances. **Live, against the booted IDUNA**: guest-register -> guest-login -> ticket (MAC verified) -> `guest-upgrade` landing on the **same** player_id -> `email-login` -> a ticket minted with THAT token, MAC verified again — so the whole portable path is proven, not described. The harness's inline MAC check also became a real script (`roblox/tests/verify_ticket.py`) used by every step. NOT done, named in `roblox/README.md`: there is no sign-in-by-email FORM on this screen, so a returning player without their published secret still starts as a new guest; still no Studio; still no rating linkage (the world's ladder keys by seat slot — `dw_ladder_add_citizen(&g_dw_ladder, slot, ...)` — which is not a person, so a Roblox<->pid binding would be a made-up number rather than a real one).
- **feat(roblox): the 460 layer gets a REAL sign-in — and the endpoint it needs was refusing the only login a Roblox player can perform.** Founder: *"make it real."* A Roblox place cannot run Google OAuth and must never ask for an IDUNA password, so the place now uses IDUNA's generic game accounts, and SHANKPIT gains its own `shankpit` registry row for them: `POST /api/v1/games/shankpit/guest-register` creates a real `players` row (UUID), an ES256 player token and a `guest_secret`; `guest-login` signs that same account back in. New `roblox/src/login460.lua` (injected HTTP + injected clock, so it is testable off-game) reads those answers, refuses one without a `player_id`/`token`/`expiry` or one already expired, treats a token inside a 300 s margin as needing re-login, and persists **player_id + guest_secret only** — never the token, which lives 24 h and is cheaper to re-earn than to store. `lobby.server.lua` signs each Roblox `UserId` in (DataStore-backed, pcall-guarded, so a place without API access still runs and merely forgets), mints with that player's own token, refuses a seat whose ticket names a different player, and `lobby.client.lua` grew a SIGN IN button and an identity line. **The real bug this surfaced, fixed in IDUNA:** a game player token's subject is `guest:<uuid>` with the identity in its `player_id` claim, and `/api/v1/shankpit/ticket` did `uuid.Parse(sub)` — so this exact login minted a token that endpoint answered `400 token subject is not a player id`; it now resolves `player_id` first and falls back to a UUID (bare or provider-prefixed) subject, still refusing `local:<uid>` and agent names. Verified: `make test-roblox` — **net460 27, queue460 50, ticket460 32, login460 36 checks, 0 failures**, `luac -p` clean, model **9 instances / 45,921 bytes of Luau**; IDUNA `go build ./...` and `go test ./internal/http/handlers -run ShankpitTicket` green (6 existing + 3 new). **And the whole chain runs live on this box**: new `roblox/scripts/live_ticket_check.sh` boots the real IDUNA binary on a throwaway SQLite file and a private port (`IDUNA_ADDR`), guest-registers, signs back in with the secret, mints a ticket, verifies the MAC the way the C server does, and feeds IDUNA's own JSON to `live_mint_test.lua` — `player_id=c6150b63… name=RobloxCheck`, `MAC verified: 16-byte truncated HMAC-SHA256 over 20 payload bytes, 300 s left`, and the place's own module read that ticket's expiry correctly. Three of my own mistakes were caught by these checks rather than shipped: `SERVER_PORT` is not IDUNA's variable (`IDUNA_ADDR` is), the ticket MAC is keyed by the RAW BYTES of `SHANKPIT_TICKET_SECRET` rather than a hex-decoded copy (the checker hex-decoded it and failed a valid ticket), and the live Lua reader was missing its `package.path`. NOT done, named in `roblox/README.md`: never opened in Studio (no Studio here), URLs are production hostnames (Roblox cannot reach localhost), no Roblox↔world-pid binding so everyone queues at 1000 and the board stays read-only, identity does not follow a player between places, and the C verifier itself lives in the separate shankpit-460 checkout (the harness reimplements the documented algorithm).
- **fix(roblox): the ticket was INVENTED — it is now IDUNA's real 72-hex wire format, with its arithmetic in PARENA, and the lobby has a screen.** Continuing the 460 layer the same day, against the real contract instead of a guess. `IDUNA/internal/http/handlers/shankpit_ticket.go` mints `POST /api/v1/shankpit/ticket` (Bearer JWT) -> `{ticket, expires_at, player_id}`, where the ticket is 36 raw bytes hex-encoded: **uuid (16) || expiry (4, LITTLE-endian u32) || hmac_sha256[:16]**, TTL 5 minutes because it is presented once at PACKET_CONNECT. The previous pass shipped a made-up `s460:<pid>:<hex>` string with an integer pid; there is no such prefix anywhere in IDUNA and the pid is a UUID. `ticket460.parse` now reads the UUID and the little-endian expiry out of the bytes (pinned by hand in the test: 2000000000 is `0x77359400`, so the bytes are `00 94 35 77`), `usable` fixes expiry at the exact second, and **`from_mint` refuses an answer whose JSON `player_id`/`expires_at` DISAGREE with the bytes IDUNA signed** — two sources of truth for one ticket that disagree means one of them is not the ticket. The shape/expiry rules are PARENA's now too: `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/ticket_rules.prn` (`ticket-shape-ok`, `ticket-expired`, `ticket-remaining-s`) emitted beside the queue rules by `roblox/scripts/gen_460_rules.sh` (`make regen-roblox-rules`). **The lobby mints for real**: `lobby.server.lua` POSTs with the player's token as a Bearer, seats by Roblox `UserId`, and hands each side ITS OWN ticket in `SHANKPIT460_MatchFound` with the time actually left. **And there is now a screen**: `src/lobby.client.lua` (LocalScript) draws the board (standing, name, rating, record, meta, STALE), the queue state, CASUAL/RANKED/LEAVE, and the match prompt with the ticket and a countdown. Verified: `make test-roblox` — **net460 27, queue460 50, ticket460 32 checks, 0 failures**, `luac -p` over both scripts, `make roblox-model` -> **8 instances** (XML round-tripped). Two syntax bugs of mine were caught by the compiler and one by the test: a `mint(...):upper()` method call Luau/Lua reject, a stray JS-style comment in a heredoc, and a grep for `hmac` that matched the module's own COMMENT saying it does not verify one (the check now strips comments first). NOT done, named in `roblox/README.md`: no Studio here so nothing has been seen; there is no login screen (the token has to arrive from somewhere); the mint has never run against a live IDUNA; there is **no Roblox UserId <-> SHANKPIT pid binding**, so players queue at the default 1000 and the board stays read-only; and Roblox cannot reach `127.0.0.1`, so both URLs must be public.
