- **feat(roblox): SHANKPIT on Roblox gets its own 460 layer — narrow, and the rules are PARENA's.** Founder, after pushing a base place: *"do a rebase sync just pased a base rbxl for roblox for shankpit roblox (not noobtopia narrower scope it should have shankpit 460 dna)"*, then *"write it in upstream PARENA compiled into LUA so we get types"*. `shankpit_roblox.rbxl` is a real BINARY place (checked: `<roblox!` magic, 128 classes, 3059 instances) and it stays the base — there is no rbx-dom/rojo toolchain here, so hand-writing INST/PROP/PRNT chunks to rewrite it in place would be a good way to corrupt the one file hardest to reconstruct. What ships instead is the layer beside it, in `roblox/`: **`src/queue460.lua`** (casual vs ranked, closest-pair search, seat bookkeeping), **`src/ticket460.lua`** (a ticket's SHAPE only — the verdict is IDUNA's; there is deliberately no signing secret or digest routine in a place any client can read, and a test proves that by stripping comments before it looks), **`src/net460.lua`** (the world's own published ladder: schema support, the ladder's own rating/wins/pid order, staleness) and **`src/lobby.server.lua`** (the glue that needs a running server). **The thresholds are typed once, in PARENA**: `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/queue_rules.prn` holds the window (100 at join, +50 per whole second, capped 600), the strict shorter-waiting comparison, the two-minute expiry and the casual-only 30 s fill as `I32`-in/`I32`-out rules, emitted by `roblox/scripts/gen_queue460.sh` (`make regen-roblox-rules`) to `roblox/src/queue460_rules.lua` — the same one-source-two-targets rule NOOBTOPIA's `rating.prn`/`stage.prn` follow. The policy that came with it: **standing in line buys a wider match, never a lopsided one nobody agreed to** (the STRICTER of the two windows governs, so a newcomer cannot drag a patient body into a mismatch it did not wait for), **ranked never bot-fills** while casual may fill with a citizen after 30 s and the body STAYS queued, and **two minutes releases the seat**. Verified: `make test-roblox` — **net460 27 checks, queue460 50, ticket460 25**, plus `luac -p` over the glue, under plain Lua 5.4, with every boundary hand-derived (100/550/600 windows, the 999/1000 ms and 3999/4000 ms edges, 120000 vs 120001 expiry, ranked-refuses-fill). Three of my own expectations were WRONG and the tests caught all three rather than the code: a 300-point gap does not fit waits of (0, 4000) because the strict window is the fresh body's 100; a newcomer that has waited 2 s has a 200 window, not 300; and the ticket module's own comment ("there is no local HMAC here") tripped a grep aimed at code. `make roblox-model` emits `roblox/SHANKPIT_ROBLOX_460.rbxmx` (5 instances, XML round-tripped) for **Insert from file** into the base place. NOT done, named in `roblox/README.md`: nothing has been opened in Studio (none here), the IDUNA ticket round trip is an opaque `POST` never exercised against a live IDUNA, Roblox cannot reach `127.0.0.1` so `NetUrl` must be the public netd endpoint, and there is **no gameplay** in the place — a pairing fires a RemoteEvent and the arena stays the C server, which is what a 460 ticket is for.
- **feat(zombies): the OG train-mode net drives the horde — and the merge is a split of AUTHORITY, because fed raw it walks a zombie backwards.** Founder: *"FOR ZOMBIES MODE CAN WE MERGE IN THE OG TRAIN MODE NEURAL NET OPPONENTS SO THE ZOMBIES ARE MORE DYNAMIC?"* Checked rather than assumed first: the OG opponent is `packages/simulation/brain_weights.h`, exported from `shank_brain_horde.pth` — 8 -> 256 -> 128 -> 4, run by `neural_net.h`'s `bot_brain_forward`, fed the 8 features `bot_ai.h` builds (self x/z /500, yaw as sin/cos, target x/z /500, distance /500, own health /100) and emitting [fwd, strafe, yaw-rate, shoot]. Its own checkpoint name is why this is a *merge*: it was trained on a HORDE. So `packages/simulation/zombie_brain.{h,c}` is a shim, and it is the ONE translation unit in the C tree that includes `neural_net.h`, so the forward pass has exactly one definition per binary. **`PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/zombie_brain_rules.prn` is why one net is not five identical brains**: each class reads its own body and its own range differently — distance-bias (BRUTE 1200 permille, believes the hero is farther, so it commits late and hard; RUNNER 900, closes early), health-bias (RUNNER 1150, reckless; BRUTE 850, knows it is a big target), weave cap (BRUTE 350 millidegrees, RUNNER 1500), lunge threshold (BRUTE 300 permille of the net's shoot channel, SHAMBLER 700), and a per-body phase derived from (class, level) — deterministic, never RNG, so a horde of one class does not strafe in lockstep. Emitted to `zombie_brain_rules.c` by `make regen-zombie-brain`, the same discipline `ai_brain_rules.c` already follows. **Then it was MEASURED, and the naive wiring is wrong**: applied the way `bot_ai.h` applies it, every class walks a zombie steadily AWAY from a hero standing 100 units ahead — fwd -0.80..-0.85, 86 units of retreat in 600 ticks, zero lunges — in the absolute world frame, in the zombie's own frame, and in its own frame with the true yaw (in the last one it simply spins, yaw -360 to -900). A rifle duelist is trained to kite; a zombie has no ranged attack to kite with, so obeying that channel as a direction produces a monster that moonwalks out of its own fight. **The split, which is the actual merge: the BEARING decides WHERE (the hand-tuned heading, which converges), the NET decides HOW** — its yaw arrives as a class-capped weave on top of the bearing, its strafe as a jink, its shoot channel as the commit that can lunge from AGITATED before the mood arc catches up, and its forward channel as a 0.55..1.0 COMMITMENT rather than a direction (there is no retreat affordance on a zombie, and `ZOMBIE_BRAIN_MIN_COMMIT` is the number that keeps one from acquiring it). Verified: convergence under the shipped mapping measured directly — all five classes close 100 units to 0.00 in 600 ticks; `make test-zombie-brain` (new) — **76 checks, 0 failures**: determinism, the off path, the hand-derived weave caps (600/900/1500/350/1200) both signed, the lunge thresholds measured through one shared 0.6 output (commits a WALKER/BRUTE/ALPHA, not a SHAMBLER/RUNNER), five classes producing five distinct intents from one observation, NaN refused at the door, phase unique across all 60 real bodies, and no class ever walking backwards. `make test-witness-ai-zombies` (ASan/UBSan) — **all pass**, including horde/nav/claw/pheromone/REFLUX; `make server` links; `make test-deadweight`, `test-dw-parity`, `test-dw-ladder-parity` unaffected. **OFF IS OFF**: enabled only in MODE_ZOMBIES and behind `SHANKPIT_ZOMBIE_BRAIN=0`, and `used_net == 0` leaves the old chase byte-identical — one implementation of the old behaviour, not two that drift. Two real bugs found on the way: the phase formula CLAMPED at 899, which collapsed every high-level body onto one drift (precisely the late-horde case it exists to prevent) — it wraps now, with a regression test; and `dw_stage.c` could read an uninitialised `winners[0]` on a degenerate bracket. NOT done, named: nothing is drawn or eyeballed (no GL driver here); the net is not retrained (these are the shipped checkpoint's weights, and nothing in here learns or explores — a forward pass only), so the absolute-coordinate inputs still make it map-specific, and the real fix is a checkpoint trained on HORDE observations rather than duel observations; no per-body variation in the net itself beyond the class rules.
- **feat(deadweight): the world holds its own tournament stage, and the stage is PUBLISHED.** Founder: *"publish the stage"*. The Roblox stage only existed inside a Roblox server, and WOTAN reads the game's own snapshot — so a bracket that lived on Roblox could never appear on the site. `packages/simulation/dw_stage.{h,c}` is the bracket in the world that owns the ladder: entrants are the top four by RATING (then wins, then pid — the published page's own order), the fold and the bracket size come from `PARENA/stdlib/deadweight/stage.prn` via `dw_stage_rules.c` (the same file NOOBTOPIA emits to Luau), and **every match is a real ladder match** through `dw_ladder_challenge`/`dw_ladder_accept`, so records, ratings, the meta, the saved ladder and the ticker all move — a stage result is a ladder result, not a parallel universe. A DRAW advances the higher seed while the match stays an honest draw on the ladder. **A person's match is not played behind their back**: `dw_stage_play` refuses with -2 when either entrant `is_player`, exactly as the Roblox room waits for a prompt. `dw_net` schema **3** publishes it — `"stage":{name, round, rounds, entrants, byes, over, champion, round_name, match, results[]}`, or `"stage":null` when nobody is running one, because "no stage" and "a stage with nobody in it" are different facts. The world opens a bracket from `witness_ai.c`'s tick once the ladder seats four bodies, plays one match per 6 s, and leaves the champion up for a minute. Verified: `make test-deadweight` — **ALL PASS**, new block: four hand-rated citizens seed 1-v-4 / 2-v-3, the first played match is **zero-sum** on the sum of ratings, the bracket reaches its final and crowns a champion, a finished stage plays nothing more, the export carries the stage and its results (and no `"played":0` from a full field), `"stage":null` for an unopened AND a detached stage, the person's match returns -2, and a three-entrant field records exactly one bye. `make test-dw-parity` and `make test-dw-ladder-parity` still pass, and WOTAN's own test drives the real daemon against this snapshot — **ALL PASS (68 checks)**. NOT done, named: no best-of-three, no losers' bracket, the bracket is not on the in-game phone, and a stage involving the player waits forever unless something presses on their behalf (the phone's end of it is the next piece).

- **feat(deadweight): the published ladder carries the rating, so WOTAN's Citizens page is a real leaderboard.** The rating landed on the C ladder in the previous bullet and then stopped at the process edge: `dw_net.c` did not export it, so the page could only rank by wins. Schema **2** adds `rating` to every record, read straight off `DwLadderMember` rather than recalculated — if the exporter recomputed it, the page and the game could rank the same population differently. Deliberately NOT accompanied by a pre-sorted ranking array: the order is (rating desc, wins desc, pid asc), a one-line sort a reader can do, and shipping the same fact twice is how two pages start disagreeing about who is first. Verified: `make test-deadweight` — **ALL PASS**, with the shim block now asserting the schema is 2, that the export's `"rating":<n>` string is the LADDER's own field (built from `l.members[i].rating`, so a divergent calculation fails), that a joining citizen starts at 1000, and that the post-match export follows the ladder's new value. `make test-dw-parity` and `make test-dw-ladder-parity` still pass. WOTAN's own `scripts/shankpit-net-test.js` drives the real daemon against the snapshot this test writes — **ALL PASS (58 checks)**, now including the rating field, an "unknown rating" flag for a schema-1-era record, and an assertion that the model's records are ordered by rating rather than by JSON arrival. NOT done, named: the phone's roster still lists records rather than ranking by rating, and nothing in the game's own UI shows a table.

- **feat(deadweight): the ladder's members carry a RATING, and it is the same number on the Roblox side — a leaderboard with something to climb.** Founder, closing the gap the last bullet named: *"byukd the roblox ui and rating in the c ladder"*. `packages/simulation/dw_rating.c` is now emitted from `PARENA/stdlib/deadweight/rating.prn` by `make regen-dw-engine`, beside `dw_rules.c` and `dw_citizen_rules.c` — one `.prn`, two targets, the same convention as everything else DEADWEIGHT does. `DwLadderMember` gained `rating`, set to `rating_start()` (1000) on join, and a finished match applies **exactly one zero-sum delta** in `dw_ladder_record` — the single recorder an instant match and a watched duel both go through, so the two ways of playing one game cannot price it differently. Beating somebody 200 stronger pays 20, beating somebody 200 weaker pays 12, a draw moves both by a tenth of the gap (±4, clamped), nothing drops below 100, and `rating_gain(w, l) == rating_loss(l, w)` for every pair — the property `rating.prn` failed before its own fix, which is why the number lives in one file rather than in each host. **The save carries it**, format bumped to 2, and a format-1 file written before the rating existed still LOADS (every member starts at 1000) rather than being refused: yesterday's ladder is still a real ladder, and the header's two-version acceptance is deliberate, not laxity. The ladder parity fixture now prints the rating in every member line, so `make test-dw-ladder-parity` pins the pricing across worlds too. Verified: `make regen-dw-engine` (emits `{dw_rules,dw_citizen_rules,dw_rating}.c`), `make test-deadweight` — **ALL PASS**, `make test-dw-parity` — **5 matches, 117 rounds, ALL PASS**, `make test-dw-ladder-parity` — **2 scripts, 12 outcome lines, ALL PASS** against the regenerated fixture (`member 0 pid 6 ... rating 984`, i.e. the loser of one equal-rating game, which is the hand-derived -16), and NOOBTOPIA's four suites against the same numbers (`lua tests/deadweight_ladder_test.lua` **107 checks**, `lua tests/deadweight_board_test.lua` **65**). Named, honest: the rating is adjusted but nothing yet RANKS on it inside the game — the phone's roster still lists records, and the sorted board is the Roblox consumer; the published snapshot (`dw_net.c`) does not carry the field, so WOTAN's page cannot show it yet.

- **test(deadweight): the LADDER is pinned across worlds too — the fixture carries the SCRIPT, not just the answers.** Follow-on from the sequencer's parity vectors: the ladder is the other piece still hand-written per world (NOOBTOPIA has its own Luau port for Roblox), so `PARENA/stdlib/deadweight/ladder_parity.txt` now pins it. `packages/simulation/dw_ladder_parity_test.c` writes AND reads that file (`make test-dw-ladder-parity` / `make regen-dw-ladder-parity`), and the fixture contains a sequence of ladder OPERATIONS — reset/hero/add/challenge/accept/decline/tick/remove — followed by the outcomes; both this test and NOOBTOPIA's `tests/deadweight_ladder_test.lua` execute the script out of the FILE, so the two cannot drift by one of them re-typing the scenario. `accept`/`decline` address "the challenge opened most recently", so the script never names an internal slot. What the two scripts cover: all four challenge directions, a decline, citizen-initiated challenges from the tick's own RNG draws, a challenge that expires past its 45-second TTL, and a challenge to a pid that has left the world. The member DECKS are printed as proof that re-crafting from the record and the meta reproduces them — they are deliberately absent from the save format for exactly that reason. Verified: `make test-dw-ladder-parity` — **2 scripts, 12 outcome lines, ALL PASS**; the same file against NOOBTOPIA's port — **107 checks, 0 failed** (which also found that a member who has not played since the meta moved keeps its old deck; the C has the same behaviour and the test now asserts it explicitly instead of assuming otherwise). `make test-dw-parity` (sequencer) and `make test-deadweight` still pass. NOT done, named: the C ladder carries no rating yet — `PARENA/stdlib/deadweight/rating.prn` exists and is emitted to Luau for the Roblox leaderboard, but wiring `dw_rating.c` into `DwLadder` is a separate change.
- **fix(deadweight): the policy now chooses from the hand it is actually dealt — round 1 used to play a card neither seat held.** Found by DATA, not by reading: NOOBTOPIA's Luau port of the sequencer is now pinned against this one through `PARENA/stdlib/deadweight/match_parity.txt`, and the two disagreed on the first card-vs-card exchange of the first match. `dw_match_step_policy` computed both seats' picks BEFORE calling `dw_match_round`, so it was selecting from the hand dealt the round BEFORE — and on a fresh match, before any deal at all, the hands were still `memset` zeroes, which is a valid card id (the cheapest Offense card), so round 1 always "played" card 0 and every later round played a card from the wrong hand. `dw_match_init` now deals `-1` into every slot (":no hand yet" is -1 everywhere, never 0), and a round is split into `dw_round_begin` (clock, roll, economy, deal, DISABLE) and `dw_round_finish` (every effect channel, the end of the match), with `dw_match_step_policy` choosing in between; `dw_match_round` is still both halves in one call for a scripted caller, so a scripted round and a policy round agree. The same shape and the same bug existed in NOOBTOPIA's port, where it surfaced as a first-round PASS instead (its hands start at -1) — fixed there too. **Also fixes this test suite's own blind spot:** `make test-deadweight` passed over the bug because every scripted round names its own cards, and the policy blocks only asserted properties (a winner, records that move), never which card was played. Verified: `make test-dw-parity` — **5 matches, 117 rounds pinned, ALL PASS**; `make test-deadweight` **ALL PASS**; `make test-witness-ai-zombies` **ALL PASS**; NOOBTOPIA's `lua tests/deadweight_match_test.lua` **903 checks, 0 failed** against the same fixture. Named: the fixture is generated by the same binary that reads it (`--dump`), so a garbled column would be "parity" with itself — a couple of rows were checked by hand (round 1 of match 0: Offense tier 0 into Operations tier 0 deals 3 and takes 0, energy 4-1 = 3, credits 3+1 = 4).
- **feat(deadweight): the player's deck is a choice — five playstyles, built by the same function that builds a citizen's.** Item B5. Players were handed `DW_PLAYER_PATH` and a crafted deck and told it was a placeholder. `dw_play_set_path` lets them pick a playstyle (CRAFTER / TRADER / DUELIST / ENDGAME / DRIFTER, the five `CILIFE_*` paths), and the deck is then built by `cp_deck_card` — the SAME function that builds a citizen's — from the player's record and the population's meta, so a player's deck reacts to the meta exactly as the population's do instead of being a fixed hand-in. `dw_play_path` reads it back. **Refused mid-duel (`-5`):** a deck swapped while a match is live would make the result a fact about two decks, and the deck a match was seeded with is the deck it finishes with. The phone gets five `DECK:` rows after the roster with the live one marked, plus a line that counts the built deck by kind (`DECK 16/16 OFF 7 OPS 6 DEF 3`) so choosing a playstyle has a visible answer rather than being a word on a row. Select here is another `BP_FX_DW_DECK` the host carries out — the phone still never re-crafts anything itself. Verified: `make test-deadweight` — **ALL PASS**: every one of the five playstyles is accepted, sticks, rebuilds sixteen ids that are all inside `num_cards()`, and moves no record; at least two of the five decks are genuinely different (measured, not assumed — if the policy ever collapsed them onto one deck the test says so); `-1` and `5` are refused; and the swap is refused during a watched duel and accepted after it. `make test-witness-ai-zombies` ALL PASS, `main.c` type-checks at 0 errors, `phone_test` passes. NOT done, named: this is playstyle-level choice, not card-by-card — picking individual cards needs the full-catalogue deck walk (B3), which is still blocked on the emitter's cross-module call typing (B10).
- **feat(deadweight): a watched duel shows what was PLAYED, not just the score.** Follow-on to the round clock: the scoreboard moved but the game did not read as a game. `DwWatch` now keeps a bounded play log (`DW_WATCH_LOG` = 6 rounds, newest last) of each seat's card per round, filled in `dw_ladder_watch_step` from the match's own `last_card`, and `dw_play_watch_board` hands it out seat-aware. **Card IDS, not names, deliberately:** the catalogue (`PARENA/stdlib/deadweight/card_rules.prn`) carries no name strings at all — grepping it for "name" returns nothing — so the phone describes each card from the numbers the engine already owns (`card_kind` / `card_tier` / `card_power`) as `OPS T2 P6` instead of this repo inventing a second catalogue that would drift from the real one the moment a card is retuned. A pass renders as `PASS`, which is what `last_card == -1` actually means. Verified: `make test-deadweight` — **ALL PASS**, with the log asserted against the board it came from (one entry after the first round, that entry's round and BOTH seats' cards equal to the live match's own `last_card`), then after the match: the log is exactly `min(rounds, 6)`, its round numbers ascend, and **every logged card is either a pass or an id inside `num_cards()`** — so a stale or invented id cannot reach a screen. `make test-witness-ai-zombies` ALL PASS, `main.c` type-checks at 0 errors, `phone_test` passes. NOT done: no names (there are none to show), no replay, no card art.
- **feat(deadweight): a match you accept is READ round by round — the board exists, and watching does not change the game.** Closing the honest headline of the whole feature ("the game plays headlessly and the citizens play it — nobody has watched a match"), item B6. The sequencer was already round-shaped, so this is SCHEDULING: `dw_match_step_policy` is one policy-chosen round, `dw_match_play_out` is now that in a loop, and `DwWatch` holds one live `DwMatch` in the ladder with its own clock. `dw_ladder_accept_watched(l, slot, now, round_ms)` sets a duel up and returns immediately; `dw_ladder_watch_step` plays exactly one round when it is due and, when the last one lands, records it through the SAME recorder an instant match uses — which is now a function (`dw_ladder_record`: meta tally, both records, the last-match record, the published ticker, both re-crafted decks, the save file) rather than a block copied into two callers, because two recorders is how a watched game would start disagreeing with a real one. Driven from the world's own tick (`witness_ai.c`), not from a phone's frames, so a duel advances with the world. `dw_play_accept_watched` / `dw_play_watch_poll` / `dw_play_watch_board` are the player's end of it, and **accepting a challenge on the phone now starts a watched duel** (1.2 s a round) instead of resolving in the same frame as the question: the page shows ROUND / HULL / ENERGY while it plays and the result lands when it ends. Two guard subtleties are decided rather than incidental: accepting an already-answered slot is `-3` (the invitation is gone) even while a duel runs, and a DIFFERENT invitation inside a live duel is `-5` (one board at a time). Verified: `make test-deadweight` — **ALL PASS**, with the watched/unwatched block building two identical ladders, the same challenge id, one match resolved in a single call and the other read a round per second, then asserting **identical winner, round count, both hulls, meta total, every member's record and every re-crafted deck**; plus "not due yet plays no round", "one round does not finish a match", the live board showing a played round with hulls still inside 20, and `-1` from stepping with no watch or after it ended. `make test-witness-ai-zombies` **ALL PASS** on the changed tick; `apps/lobby/src/main.c` type-checks at 0 errors; `packages/common/phone_test.c` still passes. NOT done, named: **citizens' own matches still resolve instantly** (nobody is watching those, and pacing the whole population would be simulation load for no screen); the page has still never been LOOKED at (no GL driver, `make -B lobby` still cannot link — A7); no card art, no per-round log line, no replay.
- **feat(deadweight): the ladder's records survive a restart — and the save file proves the deck-crafting is deterministic.** Working the report's own item B7. `static DwLadder g_dw_ladder` meant every record, the meta tally and the player's own history reset to an empty table on boot, which quietly undercut the one rule the ladder exists for ("citizens craft their own decks based on their win rate and the current meta"). `dw_ladder_save` / `dw_ladder_load` write a line-oriented text file (members with pid / is-person / path / aggression / wins / games, the meta tally, the per-direction results, the hero) — **not JSON**, because it is written and read by the same C module and a format nobody else parses does not earn a parser; write-then-rename, so a crash mid-save cannot leave a truncated ladder. **The decks are deliberately NOT saved**: a deck is a FUNCTION of the record and the meta, so loading re-crafts every one, which both restores it exactly and PROVES the function is deterministic — the test compares all sixteen cards per member against the pre-save ladder. Ordering is the whole trick: the meta is restored FIRST and only then are members re-crafted, because crafting against an empty meta would hand everyone the wrong deck while looking like a successful load. Challenges are not persisted either (a 45-second TTL that did not run is a lie, not a record), and an unknown or malformed line refuses the WHOLE file rather than half-loading a corrupt record — a wrong win count is worse than an empty one. Off by default: `witness_ai.c` only reads/writes when `SHANKPIT_LADDER_FILE` names a file, the same discipline the published snapshot follows, and every finished match autosaves (the one place a record moves). Verified: `make test-deadweight` — **ALL PASS**, new block: two real matches leave records, save succeeds, a genuinely EMPTY ladder loads them back (count, hero, meta kinds, meta plays, all four result counters, every member's pid/species/path/record), **every deck comes back byte-identical though no deck was ever written**, a missing file reports `0` and changes nothing, and both corrupt fixtures (a bad line, and no header at all) return `-2` while leaving the live ladder untouched. `make test-witness-ai-zombies` still **ALL PASS** on the changed `witness_ai.c`. NOT done, named: still no board, records are per-PID so a citizen keeps its record across a respawn (worth a design decision, not a bug), and the file is only written by a world that names one.
- **feat(deadweight): the player's end of the ladder — the human half of "citizens can challenge players", as a phone app.** Working the report's own item B2. The citizens had been playing each other for a while; the person had no way in at all. `packages/simulation/dw_play.{h,c}` is the one layer between the ladder's vocabulary (pids and member indices) and a screen's (rows): what is waiting on you, who is asking, yes/no, who you could ask, ask them. It is a separate module, and tested directly, precisely because the bugs here are SCREEN bugs and no renderer on this box can catch them — showing a challenge that already expired, offering a citizen you already asked, reading a result from the wrong seat, or printing "you lost" after a decline. `DwLadder` gained a `DwLadderLast` (kind, both pids in the CHALLENGE's order, winner, rounds, both hulls, timestamp) filled by `dw_ladder_accept` at the one place a match stops being live, so a result is a fact about a match rather than something inferred from a closed challenge; `dw_play_answer` translates it to the reader's seat ONCE, so "3-1" always means your hull first whichever end opened it. `BP_APP_DEADWEIGHT` on the phone shows your record, the population's meta kind and share, the waiting challenge as its own PLAY / DECLINE row, and the roster with each citizen's record — host-fed like EMPIRE, because the header must not invent a name or a record that the world could disagree with; `witness_ai_citizen_name()` is the one place a pid becomes a person. SELECT only NAMES the choice (`BP_FX_DW_ACCEPT` / `_DECLINE` / `_CHALLENGE` with a ROSTER index), and the host re-derives the roster before acting, so a challenge can never land on a different person than the row that carried the cursor. A pending challenge also marks the tile on the phone's home grid, the same affordance unread mail already uses — a citizen asking you to play is a notification, not something to find by opening apps. Verified: `make test-deadweight` — **ALL PASS**, with the new block asserting that answering nothing reports nothing (`-3`), that the roster is exactly the two citizens and does not offer you yourself, that asking someone removes them from it while the ask is open, that a decline closes the challenge and moves NO record (games still 1, `declined` 1), that accepting plays the match and counts it (games 1 → 2), that the last result's hulls are the ladder's own values read from the PLAYER's seat (asserted against `l.last.hull[my_seat]`, not against a hull-order guess — a match can be lost on bankruptcy with more hull left than the winner, so the naive ordering assertion would have been wrong), and that the recorded direction is `citizen-vs-player` when the citizen opened it. `apps/lobby/src/main.c` **type-checks at 0 errors** (`-fsyntax-only`, GLU prototypes stubbed locally — this box has no `libglu` headers, nothing linked); `packages/common/phone_test.c` still passes over every app including the new one. NOT done, named: still no BOARD to watch a match on (a match resolves instantly and the phone reports the result — a watched game is a round clock, item B6); `make -B lobby` still cannot LINK here (pre-existing missing `../EDITOR.GAME/gen/editor_stdlib_gen.c`), and there is no GL driver, so nobody has seen the page — it is type-checked, not looked at.
- **feat(deadweight): the citizens' ladder goes on the internet — an "internet of SHANKPIT" shim (producer in the game, a real HTTP daemon, a real WOTAN page).** Founder real-time: *"use real wotan we need an internet of shankpit shim"* then *"use real iduna and real wotan the local ones but shim me up skotti"*. The ladder lived inside the game process and had no outside world; WOTAN is a static site that can only read HTTP. `packages/simulation/dw_net.{h,c}` is the **producer**: `dw_net_export` is a PURE function of `DwLadder` (schema 1 — population, the meta and its tallied share, every seat's record + the sixteen cards it built, open challenges with time left, results by direction, and a live ticker of finished matches), `dw_net_write` does write-then-rename so a reader never sees half a snapshot, and `dw_net_publish` writes at most every 2 s (promptly, bounded to 500 ms, after a match). **Off by default**: publishing happens only when `SHANKPIT_NET_SNAPSHOT` (or `dw_net_set_path`) names a file, so a normal run writes nothing and pays nothing. `dw_ladder_accept` calls `dw_net_note_match` at the one place a match stops being live, so nothing can finish a match and forget to publish it; `witness_ai.c` registers the citizen-handle lookup and publishes from its own tick, with `dw_net_reset` on a new world so a session never inherits a previous ticker. `tools/shankpit_netd.c` is the **consumer**: no dependencies, serves `GET /api/v1/shankpit-net/{deadweight,health}`, a real ETag (mtime+size) with 304 on `If-None-Match`, CORS + OPTIONS for a dev origin, 405 with `Allow` for a write method, an honest 404 that names the path it did not find, and an honest 404 for "no world is publishing here yet". POSIX only, named: it is a deployment daemon behind nginx, never something a player runs. Verified: `make test-deadweight` — **ALL PASS**, the shim block asserts the schema, the population count, a name with a quote and a backslash really arriving JSON-escaped (`a\"b\\c`), the path name, the challenge direction, that an unplayed challenge is NOT in the ticker and a played one is (with the challenge's own seat order preserved through `dw_net_recent`), that a 32-byte buffer is REFUSED rather than truncated, and that publish writes once and then not again 1 ms later. Then against the real binary: `bin/shankpit_netd` served the 843-byte snapshot the test wrote at **200** with `ETag`, `304` on a matching `If-None-Match`, `204` on OPTIONS, `404` on an unknown route and `405` on POST. `make netd` / `make net-demo` added; WOTAN's own `scripts/shankpit-net-test.js` drives the real daemon end to end (**53 checks**) — that test found one real mistake of mine, an assertion that a match finished at t=2500 read as "just now" when `now` was 5200; the age is now asserted as the hand-derived 3 s. NOT done, named: nothing is drawn (the feed is not on the in-game phone or HUD yet), the daemon is not deployed to the cluster (nginx proxies to `127.0.0.1:8791`, which is the world's box, not a pod), and results still do not reach IDUNA as rated matches.
- **feat(deadweight): every effect channel the catalogue carries is now sequenced — the six hand-lifecycle mechanics landed, and the test found that one card does two of them at once.** Follow-up to the DEADWEIGHT-in-SHANKPIT landing above, working the report's own item B1. `dw_match.c` now sequences **08 COPY, 34 DISABLE, 35 BLIND, 36 SWAP hands, 37 REDRAW and 38 DISCARD**, so `unsupported_fx` is no longer a list of known gaps but a GUARD that a channel the sequencer does not recognise sets a bit for instead of being silently ignored. `DwSeat` gained `disabled_next` and `blind_rounds`; DISABLE is applied AFTER the hand is dealt, because the mechanic is precisely "you were dealt a hand you cannot fully use", and BLIND ticks down at each round start (it is state a UI reads — in a perfect-information headless sim it has no mechanical outcome, which the header says rather than pretending otherwise). COPY resolves after the cancels and before the exchange, which is the ordering that matters: **the catalogue's only COPY carrier is Hostile Takeover (36), and its first word is CANCEL** — so the card cancels the opponent's declaration, then copies the pass it just created, and the round is a 0-damage stalemate with both `last_card`s at -1. I had asserted the naive "copy their Attack and mirror it" first and the test failed at 20/20 hulls; a probe against the real engine (`fx_ch(card_fx_a(36))=7`, `fx_ch(card_fx_b(36))=8`) showed why, so the assertion is now the interaction and a real property of the card, not a guess about it. Verified: `make test-deadweight` — **ALL PASS**, with each channel exercised through its own real card: Glacial Prison disables exactly 2 of the opponent's NEXT hand slots (and no more, and clears), Merkle Blindness shows 3 rounds then 2, Fire Sale draws a fresh hand (deck cursor 8 vs a control match's 4), Realm Warp really swaps the two hands (seat 0 ends holding seat 1's cards), and Hostile Takeover is the cancel-then-copy stalemate — each also asserting `unsupported_fx == 0`, so a sequenced channel cannot silently be reported as missing. `make test-citizen-avatar`, `make test-witness-ai-zombies` and `make -B server` remain green. NOT done, named: no catalogue card carries DISCARD (38), so that branch is implemented and unexercised by a real card — it wants a card of its own; nothing is drawn still (no board, no challenge prompt); and the rest of the report's list (player UI, full 105-card decks, a running wasm module, persistence, networking, pacing) is unchanged.

- **feat(deadweight): DEADWEIGHT itself, running inside SHANKPIT — the PARENA engine behind a wasm-ready shim, a real sequencer, and a citizens' ladder where every challenge direction exists and decks build themselves.** Founder: *"implement the game DEADWEIGHT INSIDE OF SHANKPIT BY ADDING THE PARENA WASM ENGINE SHIM WHATEVER IT NEEDS TO BE I WANT CITIZENS TO BE ABLE TO CHALLENGE EACHOTHER TO GAMES OF DEADWEIGHT AND CITIZENS CAN CHALLENGE PLAYERS AND PLAYERS CAN CHALLENGE CITIZENS AND PLAYERS CAN CHALLENGE PLAYERS CITIZENS WILL CRAFT THEIR OWN DECKS BASED ON THEIR WIN RATE AND THE CURRENT META."* **`dw_engine`** is the shim: DEADWEIGHT's rules (`PARENA/stdlib/deadweight/card_rules.prn` — 105 cards, the kind triangle, every cost/power/credit, the packed fx condition-and-amount engine) and the new citizen policy sit behind ONE flat integer function table, native by default and swappable for a wasm module through `dw_engine_attach_wasm`, which rejects a stale ABI or a table with a hole rather than silently answering zeroes, and `dw_engine_selftest` asks whichever backend is live for the numbers it claims to own. Honestly: SHANKPIT has no wasm interpreter in-tree, so the active backend is always native — the seam is what makes the wasm path a drop-in (the same PARENA source is what `build_d2_wasm.sh` compiles) instead of a fork, and that is stated in the header rather than implied. **`dw_match`** is the sequencer DEADWEIGHT's own `core/match.c` would be and which is not in this tree: 20 hull, 2 energy (+2 a round, capped 6, +1 for passing), 3 credits (+1 a round), a 4-card hand each round, the triangle, armour, burn/regen, both fx phases for 26 of the 32 channels (bonus/pierce/bypass/immune/lifeline/reflect/cancel/halve, heal/selfdamage/true/energy/next-round energy/armor/vault/opponent vault/burn/regen/equalize/chronobreak/convert/cleanse/debtfree), bankruptcy below -6 credits, and a 100-round draw on hull then credits. The six it does NOT sequence (COPY, DISABLE, BLIND, SWAP, REDRAW, DISCARD — all hand-lifecycle mechanics) are counted in `unsupported_fx` so a deck leaning on them is REPORTED instead of quietly playing a different game. **`dw_ladder`** answers the founder's four directions through ONE entry point, classified by who the two pids are — citizen↔citizen, citizen→player, player→citizen, player→player — with a pending list per pid, TTL expiry, and citizens opening their own games every few seconds (against each other, and at the player 40% of the time when one is present). **Deck crafting is the founder's own rule and it is a function, not a preference file:** `cp_deck_card` reads the citizen's win rate (Laplace-smoothed, so no record is an unknown 500 and 1-from-1 is 666) against the population's meta (the kind mix of every card actually played, tallied by the ladder), so a citizen losing to the current top deck rebuilds toward the answer while a winning one keeps its identity — and both sides re-craft after every match, win or lose. Wired into the population: `witness_ai.c` resets the ladder with the world, seats each citizen as it spawns (aggression from its own bravery/ambition), ticks it, and exposes it as `witness_ai_deadweight()`. Verified: `make test-deadweight` (new) — **ALL PASS**: the shim's backend swap plus its two rejections, the base line's real tiers (id 3 is Operations TIER 0 — cost 1, power 3 — which corrected two of my own assumptions), the triangle both ways, mirrors (Offense full, Operations half of 3 = 1, Defense nothing), a scripted round's exact hull/energy/credit arithmetic (20/17 hull, 3/5 energy, 4/4 credits) and the pass economy next round (6/6, 5/5), an unsequenced card reported rather than faked, a full match replayed byte-for-byte from one seed, the policy's hand-derived scores (610 legal-unread, -1 masked, 510 vs a Defense read, 820 with the triangle), and the ladder: all four directions classified, records and meta moving, expiry, citizens starting their own games, and a deck that rebuilds from Operations into Defense when a losing crafter meets an Offense meta then back to Operations once it starts winning. `make test-citizen-avatar`, `make test-witness-ai-zombies` and `make -B server` are green, and `apps/lobby/src/main.c` still compiles at 0 errors. NOT done, named in `docs/DEADWEIGHT_IN_SHANKPIT_NEXT_STEPS.md`: nothing is DRAWN (no challenge prompt, no board, no card art) — the player's end is one call away and unreached, `make -B lobby` still cannot link on a clean box (pre-existing missing `../EDITOR.GAME/gen/editor_stdlib_gen.c`), decks come from the base nine rather than all 105, no wasm module is actually attached (no interpreter in-tree), records and meta are RAM-only, and players get a placeholder deck because players cannot choose one yet. One real compiler limit shaped the design and is now documented: `parena build` types an imported call's result as `void *`, so `citizen_policy.prn` is import-free and takes card facts as arguments — the emitter fix is named as its own next step.

- **feat(citizens): every citizen gets its own body and wardrobe — generated from the persona it already has, so it looks the way it plays.** Founder: *"work towards programatic generation of the avatars for the citizens in shankpit zombies with the new tech"*. The population had grown personas, paths and secrets (`citizen_life.h`) and was still drawn with one shared box mannequin. The body is now DERIVED, authored in PARENA — `PARENA/stdlib/forgedinface/citizen.prn`, FORGEDINFACE's proportion rig flattened to what a low-poly box body can express — and emitted to `packages/simulation/citizen_avatar_rules.c` by `make regen-citizen-avatar`. **One rule, everywhere: the avatar is a total function of (seed, slot, path, the five traits)** — never a second roll, so a citizen looks the same in the lobby, on the server, in a replay and in a test, and no character sheet ever has to be kept in sync with a personality sheet. What that buys, concretely: a CRAFT player is heavier across the chest and shorter in the leg because it stands at a bench (1120 permille bulk vs a raider's 880), a DUEL player has the longest reach (1060 vs an idler's 960) because its persona is walking toward the zombie, an ENDGAME player has the longest legs (1120) because its whole life is a long trip to one far post, and, crucially, the masc/fem continuum is ONE shared body with a 0..1000 scalar (300 permille of shoulder width, 120 mm of height, 120 permille of head) — never two rigs, the same rule `rig.prn` applies to the mesh. Proportions are permille of the reference body (`apps/lobby/src/player_model.h`'s Ronin Shell), so 1000 is exactly the mannequin that already exists and a neutral citizen regresses nothing; `citizen_avatar_scale()` hands the renderer a multiplier per part. Clothes are a real integer HSV→RGB conversion, not three hashed bytes: a kit owns a hue anchor, coat and pants sit 14 degrees apart (a different dye lot, not a different outfit), pants are always the darker material, and the PATH owns the accent — teal bench, amber market, red duelist, violet endgame, slate nobody — with SOCIABILITY setting how loud the accent is and AMBITION how showy, which are the two traits a stranger can actually see. Wired at the source: `witness_ai.c` builds the avatar from the same `CitizenLife` it just rolled and exposes it as `witness_ai_citizen_avatar(player_id)`, beside the existing `witness_ai_citizen_life`. Verified: `make test-citizen-avatar` (new) — **ALL PASS**: hand-derived values at both clamps (height 1830 for a bold masculine body, 1680 for a timid feminine one, clamped at 1500/1980; bulk clamped at 780; shoulders clamped at 1300; legs at 1150), the continuum genuinely reached from both ends across 256 citizens (lowest below 200, highest above 800 — a continuum you cannot reach both ends of is a toggle with extra steps), the HSV wheel's hand-derived anchors (hue 0 → 255,0,0; 120 → 0,255,0; 240 → 0,0,255; 350 → 255,0,43; −30 wrapped to 330 and val 300 clamped), the composition checked against the rules themselves rather than by eye, a citizen rebuilt byte-identically from the same seed, a citizen keeping its coat when its playstyle changes while the accent changes, and **all 256 of 256 citizens distinct** (measured, not assumed — the test asserts the 200 bar, the population clears it entirely) (not a uniqueness guarantee — a city is allowed near-twins — but 256 must not read as six people). `make test-witness-ai-zombies` still **ALL PASS** on the new bodies (that is the whole pheromone/navigation/REFLUX suite through the real `witness_ai.c`), and `make -B server` links. The draw path is wired the same day — next bullet. Also not done: per-citizen animation variation, faces (this is proportions + palette, not a face), the zombie bodies (the ask was citizens), and the mesh side of FORGEDINFACE — the territory `stdlib/forgedlevel/mesh.prn` proves out but has not been applied to a body.

## 2026-11-01

- **feat(weapons): the magnum's right click is a spray and pray mag dump — held, full auto, wide open.** Founder: *"can we have it so that the right click for the magnum (pistol) does rapid fire high jitter full auto? it should basically turn into a spray and pray empty the mag ril quick"*. `BTN_ABILITY_2` (right mouse / controller LT) now does two different things depending on the weapon, and deliberately stays one bit: the AR's grenade is an **ability** (260-tick cooldown), the magnum's spray is a **firing mode**, so it spends no ability cooldown and grants no storm charges — which is what keeps right-click scoping on the sniper free. The bit is held rather than edge-detected (`net_sim.h` copies the button state every tick), so `update_weapons` fires the magnum every tick it is off cooldown with **no left mouse at all**: 4 ticks a round, 15 rounds/s, the 8-round cylinder gone in 32 ticks (~0.53 s), and the normal `RELOAD_TIME_FULL` starts on the tick the last round leaves. Damage per round is untouched at 45 — the price is the whole magazine and the reload, not a hidden nerf — and the accuracy is the other half of the trade: `MAGNUM_SPRAY_SPREAD` 0.16 is the widest spread in the roster (4x the AR's 0.04, past the shotgun's 0.15), applied through the same `phys_rand_f()` jitter every spread weapon already uses. The spray interval goes through `g_phys_stats_rof_hook` like any other ROF, so job haste speeds it up instead of the two numbers disagreeing; the stock 25-tick left-click cadence is unchanged. Verified: `make test-magnum-spray` (new) — **ALL PASS** through the real `update_weapons`: tuning asserts the interval is below half the revolver's own ROF and the spread is at least the shotgun's; the first held tick fires one round with no LMB and leaves `ability_cooldown`/`storm_charges` at 0; 37 held ticks empty the mag and start exactly `RELOAD_TIME_FULL`, which then counts down; 60 ticks of left mouse fire only 3 of the 8 rounds versus the spray's 8; a single left-click still sets the authored 25-tick cooldown; and the jitter is real, not just a constant — aimed dead down a line at targets 5 and 200 units away, a held spray hits the near one and **never** the far one while one aimed shot does reach 200. `make test-ar-grenade` still passes after its "non-secondary weapon" case moved from the magnum (which has a secondary now) to the knife, and `make -B server` links. NOT verified, named: no window, muzzle flash or audio exists on this box, so the *feel* is unproven — the rate, the drain and the scatter are what was measured — and networked clients still perceive it only as the shooter's `is_shooting` animation.

- **feat(zombies): zombies are a population with classes and levels, their bodies carry the DNA the lab reads, and the citizens are living their own lives — a few of them secretly somebody else.** Founder: *"as per big_o when you kill zombies they may drop bioslury which can be taken to the lab to possibly harvest dna; dna should depend on the stats of the zombie slain zombies should have different stats different levels and different classes as per GFD - citizens to for that matter - tcitizens could be spies they could secrety be another player like think of all of the citizens are all living their life and they play the game in different ways ... some just do crafting some just the econemy some just pvp some just endgame ... in that same way the citicitizens will all have their own lives and priorites and perosonalities like dwarf fortress think of the citizens like a mix between the citizens in gta and the sims and dwarf fortress"*. **`zombie_taxonomy`** is the body: five classes (SHAMBLER / WALKER / RUNNER / BRUTE / ALPHA) x levels 1..12, and every stat is DERIVED from those two numbers rather than rolled separately — which is what lets a lab grade mean something and a test exist. `WALKER` at level 1 is exactly the zombie that was already there (100 hp, 14 damage, the old pace), so nothing that worked changes meaning; RUNNER closes at 1.45x, BRUTE hits for nearly double and lumbers, ALPHA is the night-only one worth a hammer. Spawns roll class and level from the slot and the clock, weighted by day/night (no BRUTE or ALPHA by day), and the live spawn now reads its health off that profile — a level 9 brute is a 200+ hp fight, not a rename. The melee damage and the chase pace read the same profile, so they cannot silently disagree with the table. **`bioslurry`** is what a kill may leave: the chance is the body's own (`slurry_permille` — an alpha 800/1000, a shambler 250), you pick it up by walking over it, carry up to six, and carrying one into the lab delivers it — and delivery is a **roll against that body's grade**, not a promise. A shambler strand is under the usable threshold and comes back empty every time; an alpha level 8 yields a 100-grade SCENT strand (the trait is the class's own identity: RUNNER=SPEED, BRUTE=ARMOR, ALPHA=SCENT, matching the phone's own BP_TRAITS vocabulary). Uncollected canisters rot after two minutes. **`citizen_life`** is the founder's GTA/Sims/Dwarf-Fortress citizen: five traits (industry, commerce, ambition, bravery, sociability) choose one of five WAYS TO PLAY — CRAFT, TRADE, DUEL, ENDGAME, IDLE — and that path sets how far it travels, how fast, and how long it stays, so a crafter works its own corner while an endgame player takes a long trip to the one place worth being. Roughly one citizen in twelve is a **spy**: it has a player handle instead of a persona, and its goals bias to vantage points it holds. Deterministic per body (same slot+seed, same person, same handle), so a spy can be recognised later in the session. The sandbox's old 6-second random heading is replaced by this, and fleeing still wins: a citizen in a fight runs before it lives its afternoon. Verified: `make test-zombie-ecology` — **ALL PASS**: the walker is byte-for-byte the old zombie, classes differ in the directions their names promise, levels scale monotonically and both clamps are total, DNA traits are the class's own and cap at 100, 800 spawn rolls are deterministic and the night table contains all five classes while day contains no brute or alpha, an alpha leaves 161 canisters in 200 kills with identical answers on repeat, an alpha strand reads at quality 100 while six shambler canisters read as nothing, rot/scene-scoping/the pool cap all hold, and 600 citizens cover all five paths with 52 spies. `make test-witness-ai-zombies` still **ALL PASS** (the whole pheromone/navigation/REFLUX suite, on the new bodies), and `make test-hammer-tower`, `make test-hammer` and `make -B server` are green. NOT done, named: nothing draws any of it (no class silhouettes, no canister prop, no citizen name/secret reveal) because `apps/lobby/src/main.c` cannot be compiled in this sandbox; the strand list is not handed to the phone's LAB app yet — that seam is `bioslurry_dna_at`, and the LAB UI is in that same uncompilable file; delivery only happens inside the one hardcoded VOXWORLD lab circle (there is no floor-item interaction system for a lab station yet); and a spy is a persona with a handle, not an actual second human — it does not yet take orders from, or report to, a real account.

- **feat(weapons): weapon 8's E power builds a mini tower — a 3 s cast you have to stand still for, and a gatling that only shoots what is already fighting.** Founder: *"want to build in the E power for 8 the hammer it should build a mini tower there should be like a 3 second cast time you have to stay still for 2.6 seconds or it doesnt cast you caan start to run a bit early like ffxi but when it casts you stop when you first start casting you stop running if you are auto running make it like a torb gattling gun but not that strong for now have it auto target zombies and the men if the men are in combat or citizens if the citizens are in combat"*. **The cast lives in `update_weapons` (physics.h), not in the simulation layer**, so the offline match and the dedicated server run one copy of the rule: 180 ticks (3.0 s), still required for the first 156 (2.6 s), the last 24 (0.4 s) the FFXI release window where you may start running and it still lands. Movement inside the still window breaks it — input **or** real velocity, so sliding off a ledge counts as walking — and pay for it with a shortened 90-tick cooldown instead of the full 600, because a fizzle should be retryable. Starting the cast zeroes your horizontal velocity ("when you first start casting you stop running if you are auto running") and holds you rooted through the still window; reload and swings are refused until it lands or breaks. New `packages/simulation/hammer_tower.c` is the tower itself, because physics.h cannot see the NPC population: it is called from `local_update`, the tick both a local match and apps/server run. **Targeting is the founder's rule in tiers** — zombies (and Giant Zombie Bugs, the feral giant variant of the same population) always, then The Men, then citizens, and those last two only when `witness_ai_npc_in_combat` says they are actually in a fight (a live hunt at SILENCING/ENGAGE/PANIC, a Man with a dispatch target, somebody shooting, or somebody hurt). It never shoots the player, a role-less bot or a teammate, so a PvP match is unchanged. **Deliberately weak, named as such:** 4 damage a round at ~6 rounds/s is ~24 dps, roughly four seconds to put one 100 hp zombie down, with a 500 ms gatling windup, 40 units of range, a 25 s life, and a 6-tower world cap that refuses rather than stealing an existing tower. Verified: `make test-hammer-tower` — **ALL PASS, 15 blocks** through the REAL `update_weapons` and the REAL `witness_ai.c`: the 180/156/24 split, E plants your feet and spends the cooldown, standing still lands it, walking at 2.8 s remaining breaks it (cooldown 90), walking in the last 0.4 s still lands, respawn clears both fields, a zombie is shot down to 84/100 in one second, a calm citizen is untouched for three seconds, a hurt citizen is shot, The Men in combat are shot past a closer bystander, a zombie outranks a closer citizen in combat, the player and a role-less bot are never targeted, out-of-range is untouched, expiry and scene change clear it, the pool caps at 6, and the placement lands 6 units in front on the caster's feet. `make test-hammer` still passes and `make -B server` links. NOT done, named plainly: **nothing draws it** — there is no tower model, no muzzle flash and no cast bar, because apps/lobby/src/main.c cannot be compiled in this sandbox (no SDL2 headers), so a tower is currently an invisible body that shoots; and towers are not on the wire, the same real boundary projectiles already have here, so in a networked match a tower is server-side only.

- **fix(android): shankpit-460 stops waiting for a click it can never get in the WebView, so the APK is actually playable and FIRE works.** Founder: *"it almost works but it thinks i need to click on the screen its like paused but i cant click on it on the phone its like a quirk of the web view or something i can move around but not shoot"*. **The cause is the lock gate, not touch handling.** The generated host defines `lockedP()` as `document.pointerLockElement != null`, and `onLockChange` — the `pointerlockchange` listener — is the only caller of `wasm.sp_lock()`. Android WebView implements no Pointer Lock API at all, so that listener never fires, the wasm is never told play has begun, and the game stays behind its click-to-play overlay: the synthetic pad keeps moving you because `sp_pad` axes are polled unconditionally every frame, while shooting is gated on the lock the tap can never acquire. Fixed entirely in `apps/shankpit-460/android-controls.js` (the Android-only layer — `sp-host.js` is generated and still untouched): it now presents a locked canvas (`pointerLockElement`/`webkitPointerLockElement` resolve to the canvas, `requestPointerLock` returns a resolved promise, `exitPointerLock` is a no-op so the lock stays sticky since there is no cursor to hand back), and dispatches `pointerlockchange` so the host hands the wasm `sp_lock(1)` — once before the module runs, and again at load, at +250/+1000/+2500 ms and on the first tap, because the host's listener does not exist yet when this classic script executes. **A second, real bug was found while reading the path:** when `requestPointerLock` is absent the host's `mousedown` handler throws, and `startOnce()`'s synthetic `mouseup` was on the next line — so the release never ran and `sp_button(0,1)` stayed latched down for the rest of the match. The mouseup now runs in a `finally`. Verified: `node android/tools/verify-web-bundle.mjs` **PASS, 41 checks**, four of them new — `claimLock()` is exposed, `pointerLockElement` reports the canvas (i.e. the host's own `lockedP()` reads true), a `pointerlockchange` actually reaches the host, and `claimLock()` is safe to repeat — with all 37 existing checks (virtual pad shape, tap-starts-a-match, the sim advancing, no socket offline, status rewording, button map) still green. Measured headlessly against the real module: `sp_wants_lock()` is **1 for the whole match** (0 only on the title screen), so the game really is asking for a lock the WebView cannot give it — that is the gate, not a guess. NOT verified, named: there is still no emulator or device on this box, so nothing was seen on glass, and an end-to-end shot could not be measured either — `p_ammo0`, `p_fire_pending` and `g_locked` did not move under a held pad RT **or** `sp_button(0,1)`, with `sp_lock` set to 0 and to 1, so those exports do not expose the fire path the way their names suggest and the shot itself stays unproven. Touch feel and the WebView's own touch-to-mouse synthesis are equally untested.

## 2026-10-31

- **fix(airplane): the C-110's attitude is rate-commanded like Bedrock Racers' cold-gas jets, and it flies where its nose points instead of skidding and ballooning.** Founder: *"continue to debug the prop plane it should handle more like the rocket car with w and s pointing the nose up and down IMPORTANT the OG bedrock racers wasm thats in parena/wotan that one has the good physics the rocket car in shankpit isnt quite there"*. The reference is `PARENA/stdlib/bedrockracers/web/br_phys.prn` `fly-sub!`, where the **rate** chases the stick (`wp += (tp - wp) * 0.27` per substep) and stops dead when the stick is released. This header added degrees to an ANGLE every tick instead, so a tap banked the aeroplane and it kept circling, and bank fed yaw which fed bank — a loop that rocked the airframe. **(1) Rate-commanded attitude.** `AirplaneState` gained `pitch_rate`/`yaw_rate` (deg/tick, pure sim state, never serialized). W/S commands a pitch rate of 2.2 deg/tick, A/D a yaw rate of 1.4, both through a first-order lag at 0.45/tick — BR's own 0.27 per 8 ms substep is ~0.54/tick, so this is deliberately a shade heavier. Letting go stops the turn: measured **40.3 deg** held, **1.7 deg** of coast after release. **(2) No bank feedback.** Roll is no longer integrated from the rudder; it follows the yaw rate (`AF_BANK_PER_YAW`), a coordinated turn, so there is nothing left to oscillate. J/L and the arrows are still a commanded 360 barrel roll, untouched. **(3) Lateral grip.** Thrust only ever added velocity along the nose, so after a turn the airframe kept travelling along its OLD heading for seconds — the other half of "floppy". Velocity is now decomposed into forward and lateral and the lateral part thrown away (0.30/tick on the wheels, 0.06 in the air), the aeroplane's version of the rocket car's tyre grip: **0.5 deg** of slip after a 40-tick turn. **(4) It no longer balloons.** Vertical speed chases a target from the lift surplus, and lift is capped at 1.25x weight: ten seconds of full throttle now climbs **119 units at a steady 0.20 units/tick**, where the old integrator went from y=6 to **y=2203** and kept accelerating. **(5) A taxi you can actually start.** On the wheels W/S is the throttle the way the rocket car's is (S brakes to idle, release falls back to idle), so holding W rolls it off; in the air the same keys pitch the nose, and SPACE/CTRL still trims the lever and holds it. Also fixed: a phantom -0.12 of downward velocity was being built while the gear carried the airframe and then zeroed at the clamp, so the cached airspeed read a twitch every tick (`idle stays parked` now reads 0.000, not 0.020). Verified: `make test-airplane-flight` **ALL PASS, 16 checks**, six of them new — W-only taxi (0 throttle after coast, 919 units rolled, coasts 4.15 -> 0.08 and stays grounded), bounded climb, trim to level (pitch 3.3), turn-stops-on-release, nose-tracking (0.5 deg slip), and W/S pitching the nose up to 32 then down to -32 — with the original ten (bridge deck, takeoff roll 637 units at tick 247, idle, flaps, stall, the v^2 lift law, inertia, taxi traction, ground rudder, barrel roll) still green. `make server` compiles clean. NOT verified, named: the lobby cannot be compiled in this sandbox (no SDL2 headers), so no hand-flown confirmation — every number above is the headless sim. The W/S-throttle-on-the-ground mapping is a deliberate split (W drives on the wheels, pitches in the air) so the plane can be taxied without a lever ritual while W/S still points the nose once flying; SPACE/CTRL remains the only throttle in the air.

- **feat(android): shankpit-460 ships as an APK in every release — the PARENA wasm in a WebView, playable with no backend at all.** Founder: *"lets ship a version of shankpit for android have it take the shankpit 460 route and use the wasm parena work we already have ... it defaults to local demo with bots when there is no matchmaking ... we dont have a backend right now thats ok have it fail gracefully"* → *"we can literally ship wasm with a web view as v1 i dont care"* → *"we just need the controller affordances added to the screen when on android"* → *"it needs to build in the main SHANKPIT repo and auto release an apk with the rest of the releases"*. The bundle is now SHANKPIT's own (`apps/shankpit-460/`, byte-identical wasm + generated host vendored from `WOTAN/shankpit-460`, which becomes downstream), with the marketing shell stripped: a self-contained page, no fonts, no nav, no server CSS. `android/` is a Gradle module with **no AndroidX and no dependencies** — `MainActivity` serves the assets from a virtual `https://shankpit.local` origin (so `fetch()` and `WebAssembly.instantiate()` behave as in a browser; `file://` would fail CORS) and `shouldInterceptRequest` never touches the network. **Controls without touching the generated host:** the wasm takes a standard gamepad, so `android-controls.js` overrides `navigator.getGamepads()` with a synthetic pad and drives the same `sp_pad` path a physical controller uses — which is why aiming needs no pointer lock (Android WebView has none). Left stick moves, dragging aims, FIRE/JUMP/CROUCH/RELOAD/ABILITY/WEAPON map to RT/A/B/X/LT/RB, and the first tap starts the match (a pad button leaves the title) and unlocks audio. **Graceful failure is the default path, not an error state:** with no backend configured an inert `readyState === 3` WebSocket replaces the constructor, so the host never retries and the wasm runs the bot match it already has, with `Bot match (no matchmaking)` rewording the misleading `Searching for a match` pill via a `MutationObserver`; with `SHANKPIT_ANDROID_BACKEND` set, relative `/api/v1/shankpit460/*` calls and the socket are rewritten to it (`wss://`). `release.yml` gained the bundle verifier, JDK 17, `android-actions/setup-android`, Gradle 8.7 and `assembleRelease`, and the APK is uploaded and attached to the same real (non pre-release) release as `ShankPit_Android_<build>.apk`; signing uses a keystore from secrets when present, otherwise the debug key (installs by sideload; CI mints a new one per run, so updating over an installed copy needs an uninstall). Verified on this box: `node android/tools/verify-web-bundle.mjs` **PASS, 31 checks** — the real `.wasm` and the real controls file in a fake DOM, asserting zero wasm imports, seven bots, tap→match phase 0→1, live ticks advancing, no socket constructed offline, status rewording, the button map, and `releaseAll()` clearing stuck input — plus a real `gradle assembleRelease` against a real SDK (**BUILD SUCCESSFUL**) whose APK is 93 KB, contains all four assets, is signed, and badging reports `industrial.einhorn.shankpit` / minSdk 24 / targetSdk 34. One real build failure was found and fixed by building it: `(int) new Date().format(...)` cannot cast a String, so the local `versionCode` fallback now uses `.toInteger()`. NOT verified, named: no emulator/device was available, so the WebView on glass — touch feel, letterboxing, audio unlock, and the `shouldInterceptRequest` main-frame path — is **unproven**; the APK has never been installed. Unification is started, not finished: no zombies (the 460 arena is deathmatch-only), no map editing (NOCK is desktop-only), no accounts.

- **fix(nock): `local_nock.bat` finds the bundled server and writes `var\logs`, instead of `ShankPitClientvar\logs` and a Python fallback that could not start.** Founder: *"we are getting a failure in local nock it says python fallback ; system can not find the path specified; also our logging has a bug its writing to ShankPitClientvar/logs instead of ShankPitClient/var/logs"*. **One root cause for both symptoms:** `ROOT` is built with `for %%I in ("%~dp0.") do set "ROOT=%%~fI"` — deliberately *without* a trailing separator, because `--root "C:\dir\"` hands the C runtime an escaped quote (the 500-blank-page bug fixed the same day) — but every join below it still assumed one: `set "LOGDIR=%ROOT%var\logs"` produced `...\ShankPitClientvar\logs`, and the three `if exist "%ROOT%bundled\dw_nock.exe"` checks pointed at `...\ShankPitClientbundled\dw_nock.exe`, which never exists, so `SERVER` was never set and every double-click fell through to the Python fallback regardless of what shipped in the zip. The joins are now explicit (`%ROOT%\var\logs`, `%ROOT%\bundled\...`, `%ROOT%\bin\...`), including the "Looked for:" lines in the failure message, so what it prints is what it checked. **Second fault, the reported error text:** the fallback was `start ... cmd /c "cd /d "%ROOT%" ^&^& python ..."` — quotes nested inside quotes, which cmd resolves by stripping the outer pair and leaving a mangled `cd /d "C:\path`, i.e. *The system cannot find the path specified*. It now uses `start /d "%ROOT%"` for the working directory and a **relative** log target (`>> var\logs\nock.log`), so there are no nested quotes and no spaces to quote; the native launch got the same `/d` and a relative redirect. Verified: the .bat cannot be executed here (Linux, no cmd), so this is a path-composition and quoting review — every `%ROOT%<char>` join in the file was enumerated and each now has its separator, and no other `.bat` in the repo has the same pattern. The first real double-click on Windows is still the confirmation; the C side was checked and is not implicated (`nock_log_open` composes `"%s/var/logs"` with its own separator, and `nock_mkdir` creates both levels).

- **feat(hud): the rocket car says how much air it has left and which of its two nozzles is lit.** Founder: *"hud and sync work"*. The car had just gained LIFT and BOOST and the tank that pays for them, and the HUD still called it `BUGGY ONLINE` and printed `F6 STYLE / F7 GLOW / F8 LIGHT` — three keys that only touch the buggy's own model, which a rocket car no longer draws. It now reads `ROCKET CAR ONLINE`, and the line under it says `SPACE LIFT  SHIFT BOOST  ALT:<height>`, the two keys the car actually answers and the height the climb is measured against. Beside it is the tank: a bar in cold-gas blue when full shading to amber as it empties — the same shift the pods' own plumes make — with `AIR n%`, plus `LIFT` and `BOOST` labels that light while those keys are held **and** there is air to answer with (an empty tank lights nothing, which is physics.h's own rule, so the HUD cannot promise a burn the car will refuse). The car is found by `occupant_player_id`, not by garage slot, because a called car lands wherever was free. `in_lift`/`in_boost` moved from the tick block to the frame-keyboard block that already reads W/A/S/D, written to the player being **controlled** rather than always slot 0 — so in a networked match the HUD's lights match the car actually being flown. Verified: the two new functions extracted verbatim from `main.c` and compiled standalone under `-std=c99 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` with stubbed GL and a real `BuggyState`, finding the car by occupant id and drawing every branch (that is a syntax and logic check of the new code, not of its integration). NOT verified, named: `main.c` cannot be compiled here (no `SDL2/SDL.h` on this box), so the two wiring edits — the call site inside the vehicle branch and the key read beside the W/S sampling — are **reviewed, not compiled**, and nobody has seen the bar; the layout numbers (bar 300..530 at y 112..128, labels at y 148) are chosen to clear the `ONLINE` line, which is 7 × its scale tall and grows upward from y 112, but that clearance is arithmetic rather than a screenshot.

- **feat(rocket-car): the Bedrock Racers car can finally fly and boost, and it is drawn as that car — its zombies-level spawner was already there.** Founder: *"where is my rocket car we may have already added it just need to add a spawner near the other vehicles in the zombies level it should have the same geometry and vehicle physics in terms of boost and flyability as bedrock racers wasm"*. **The spawner already exists**, in the level and in its baked copy: `var/zombie/nextown_zombies.json` (and `packages/world/baked_levels_gen.h`) author `vehicle_spawns` as buggy 620 / **rocket 675** / heli 730 / recon 785 / fly 835 / plane 1050, all at y 12.5561, z −227.9 — the rocket car is the second bay, between the buggy and the helicopter, and `level_boxes.h`'s kind list already resolves `"rocket"`. What was **not** the BR car was the driving and the shape. **(1) FLYABILITY.** The profile had ported the tank's *economy* and none of its *commands*, so nothing could take the car off the hover height. Space is now LIFT — the four nozzles (`br_phys.prn fly-sub!`), 0.23 units/tick against this profile's 0.0825 effective gravity, which is BR's 215 against 77 (the same 2.8×) rescaled into per-tick units; the hover spring is off while it burns so the car genuinely leaves the surface, 26/s comes out of the tank, and the climb rate is capped at 1.4/tick so it is a car and not a missile. Released, the **free** fans catch it and settle it back to the hover height — in BR the fans cost nothing, so hovering no longer drains air either. **(2) BOOST.** Shift is BOOST: the rear nozzle on its own key, 0.10/tick on top of the throttle (BR's 285 against a 175 drive), its own 22/s, and it lifts the ceiling by 35% (BR's 340 against 190) instead of only pushing inside the old top speed. An empty tank cannot **start** a burn (`air-tick!`), judged on the tank as the tick found it rather than after the idle refill has trickled something back in. Both numbers are a PARENA mod again — `rocket-lift-accel-milli` / `rocket-boost-accel-milli` added to `stdlib/shankpit/rocket_car_rules.prn` and regenerated into `rocket_car_rules.c` with the real bootstrap — so the server and every client fly the same car off one table. **A real bug only the test could catch:** appending the two new function pointers at the END of `PhysRocketRules`' initializer while inserting them mid-struct bound `tank_step` and `tank_fill` to the wrong functions, so refuelling *set* the tank to 100 and burning *set* it to 230 — believable-looking constants, and four checks failed with "idle air did not refill". **(3) GEOMETRY.** `draw_rocket_car_model` is `br_mesh.prn build-car!` section for section, not an approximation: seven cross-sections nose to tail, six points round each (bottom, beltline, roof edge and their mirrors), the same body-pal mapping (paint, a darker shade for the sills, smoked glass, carbon underside, warm headlights, tail lights), the same four nozzle pods where the wheels would be with their mouths underneath — scaled by 0.45 into the buggy's own 7.0×4.0 footprint and flat-shaded under a fixed key light the way the drone and the C-110 are. A rocket car no longer draws the buggy body and its neon; the existing plumes moved onto the new pods' mouths. Input is `PlayerState.in_lift` / `in_boost`, **local only, exactly like `in_roll`** (what replicates is the attitude and the air, not the keypress), read by `local_game.h`'s buggy tick through a new `simulate_buggy_state_ex` — the old five-argument `simulate_buggy_state` still exists and passes zeroes, so the server's cars and every existing test are untouched. Verified: `make test-vehicle-call` **ALL PASS** with two new blocks — lift climbs **2.57 → 37.78**, comes back down, and the fans settle it at **2.57** again with the tank paying for the climb; boost **12.42 vs 9.11** units/tick over the throttle alone, burning measurably more air over a burn short enough that neither car runs dry, and an empty tank boosting nothing on the tick that found it empty. `make test-heli-flight` all checks passed and `make test-kit-call` all checks passed (both link the regenerated rules and the wrapper). NOT verified, named: the lobby cannot be compiled here (no SDL2 headers), so the two key reads and `draw_rocket_car_model` are **reviewed, not compiled**, and nobody has looked at the car; there is still no vertical collision on any vehicle, so a long climb passes through a floor above it; the strafe and attitude jets remain unported (the chassis has no axes for them, and that was already named in the rules file); and the profile is the buggy's chassis with BR's numbers, not BR's wall-and-ceiling driving — the fans here hold a hover height over a heightfield, they do not turn a wall into a road.

- **feat(levels): a subtract cube punches clean holes through level geometry — elevator shafts through floors, stairwells through decks.** Founder: *"we need affordances for punching holes in level geometry in a sane way where it punches a hole cleanly with the faces being nice a use case for this is elevators for example we build a building and then put a big cube with a subtract modifier on it and it subtracts from the geometry of the level wherever it intersects it it would look the same as if we removed that geometry with the shotgun that way we can thread an elevator through the floors and also punch holes through floors for the stairs to actually work"*. A level can now carry a `subtract` array — cubes in the same shorthand a wall already uses (centre `x/y/z`, full extents `sx/sy/sz`) — and `level_boxes_apply_subtract` resolves each one at **load time** into ordinary boxes by exact **AABB-minus-AABB**: one box minus one cube emits at most six axis-aligned pieces (below, above, and four around the shaft), each face either an original outer face or a plane of the cut. That is deliberate, not a shortcut: the cell grid (`brick_fracture.h`) is ~3 units and rounded, so an authored stairwell routed through it would come out **ragged and cell-sized**, and runtime CSG on a server-authoritative tick is a per-frame cost for a hole that only ever needs computing once. It is also why this works on **floors**, which weapon damage deliberately never touches (`brick_world_box_is_slab` excludes slabs from being fracture parents) — the two systems are now complementary rather than overlapping: weapons carve what you shoot, the modifier authors what the level was built with. Wiring is one call at the end of `level_boxes_parse_json`, so **every** consumer (client, server, `leveltool`, NOCK's round-trip) sees geometry that is already carved and none of them can forget; zero new runtime state, nothing on the wire, no per-frame work. Index stability was the real design constraint: a carved box keeps its own index (its first piece stays there) and extra pieces are appended, so doors, buttons and widgets that reference a box **by index** keep pointing at real geometry; a box wholly inside a cut is removed and the index-bearing lists are remapped once, with `-1` meaning "that box is gone" (a state every consumer already reads as *not attached*), and saved brick damage on a removed box is dropped rather than re-pointed, because it was damage **to** that geometry. An over-budget split refuses **whole** (that box stays solid) instead of half-applying, matching the existing brick carve convention. NOCK's `validate_level` now validates the array too — centre plus positive extents — so a malformed modifier is a refused save rather than a hole that silently never appears. Verified: `make test-level-subtract` (new, `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`, ASan+UBSan, no GL) — **ALL PASS, 9 checks**: a stair shaft through a floor slab gives exactly the 4-piece ring with **volume 1504.0/1504.0** (exactness is the assertion a fudge factor cannot pass); one cube threads an elevator shaft through **4 stacked floors → 16 boxes, 6000.0** volume; a cut that misses changes nothing and reports 0; an interior cut gives **6** axis-aligned pieces, every one flush with the shell or the cut plane, volume **26000/26000**; two disjoint holes are order-independent to 0.001; a swallowed box is removed with its door re-pointed to `-1`, its button untouched and its stale damage dropped; degenerate (zero-width, negative) cubes are ignored and the call is total on NULL; an over-budget split refuses whole; and the modifier survives a JSON round trip **and is already applied when the level is parsed back** (4 boxes, no extra call). Plus `make test-level-save`, `make test-lofts` and `make test-baked-levels` still green on the changed loader. NOT built here, named: the lobby and server binaries are not compiled in this sandbox (no SDL2), and the level JSON has no NOCK **UI** yet — a modifier is authored as a `subtract` entry or exported by IDUNA, with no drag-a-cube-in-the-editor affordance, which is the same named gap the lofts' own objects panel already carries. Rotated/ramp boxes are deliberately left whole (their pieces would not be axis-aligned), and nothing has been looked at on screen.

- **feat(render): weather fog now pools on the GROUND, and it is lit by the environment matrix instead of being a grey screen.** Founder: *"can we get some realistic fog effects? when the weather is stormy or foggy etc can we get some realistic level fog actually close to the ground in the level? it should obey the global volumetric lighting"*. The fog that already existed was `GL_EXP2` in **distance** — everything faded evenly from your boots to the sky, so a "foggy" level was a uniform wash with no bottom to it. Real fog has a **height** term: radiation fog, dawn mist and storm scud pool in the bottom few metres, which is why you can stand in pea soup and still see a tower against the sky.
