NOT done, named: **`make lobby` still cannot link on this box** because `../EDITOR.GAME/gen/` is not checked out, so the check is a compile, not a run — no screenshot, no menu clicked, no body seen; an imported skin plays **four of the thirteen clips** (idle, walk, melee-as-greet, ability-as-dance) through the NPC kit path, not the full state machine; **a client's own imported choice does not replicate** — a coop peer sees the server's `forced_kit` or the usual heuristic, because there is no wire field for "this player's selected set"; and the NOCK **editor dropdown does not yet list imported sets** for authoring, so an NPC's kit number is still typed by hand from the startup log.

- **feat(nock): importing a Mixamo clip set and committing it is now a thing you do in the editor, not a command you remember.** `tools/local_nock.py` gained `GET/POST /api/clipsets` and `tools/nock_editor.html` gained a **Character clips** panel: one dropdown per contract state, auto-map by clip name (`UAL1_Standard_Walk_Loop`, `mixamo_run`), and **Import + commit** — which writes `assets/goldenband/clipsets/<name>/` and commits it with a message naming the set. It is the *same* `tools/mixamo_clipset.py` binder the CLI runs, imported rather than reimplemented, so the editor and the command cannot disagree about what a valid bundle is; refusals arrive as the binder's own sentences ("no clip for: anim_run, anim_ability", "content_hash mismatch…") because the artist is who has to act on them. Only clips with **both** `.gband` and `.gband.json` are offered: the binary is channel-blind, so a lone binary would only produce a failed import. The **licensing boundary is a design decision, not a footnote**: Adobe's terms allow baking Mixamo into a game but not redistributing the editable source, so nothing here ingests or stores a raw `.fbx` — the import path starts from baked `.gband` clips this repo already owns, and a staging folder for raw downloads stays personal and gitignored. `docs/MIXAMO_PIPELINE.md` records the boundary, the three steps (convert → bind → play) and the IDUNA gap. Verified: `make test-clipset-import` — **22 checks, 0 failed** against a real HTTP server over a throwaway git repo holding copies of Mike's real clips: the contract and package path come back as the panel needs them, a lone binary is not offered, an incomplete rig is **refused until allow-partial**, partial binds exactly the two states the names match and **commits them** (with the set named in `git log`), an explicit 13-state mapping binds all thirteen and warns that reusing a clip is reusing a clip, a path-shaped name is refused, an empty import is refused, a clip whose data no longer matches its hash is refused **with no half-bundle left behind**, and re-importing identical content reports "no changes to commit" instead of an empty commit. Plus the existing `make test-local-nock` — still **16/16**, so the level/live-session contract it shares the server with is untouched. NOT done, named: **IDUNA's cloud endpoint is not built** — there is no Go toolchain on the box this was developed on, so instead of shipping an unverified handler into a large service, `docs/MIXAMO_PIPELINE.md` specifies the exact `POST /admin/nock/api/shankpit-clipsets` contract the local endpoint already implements (validation, `{bound, missing, warnings}`, store-not-git in the cloud path); IDUNA's glTF → GOLDENBAND conversion itself already exists. And nothing *draws* from these bundles yet — that is still the renderer slice.

- **feat(vehicles): a burning car now actually burns — the fire ladder moved onto the authoritative hull, so it destroys the car and hurts the people standing next to it.** The previous vehicle-burn entry shipped the ladder and named this exact gap: *"the hull and the blast are the local/client sim's — there is no wire field for a burning car yet, so a networked match does not replicate the fire state"*. The honest reading turned out better than a new wire field: the fire is a pure function of **hull**, the hull is already replicated (`NetBuggy.health`), and both halves of the ladder are deterministic — so the missing piece was never a packet, it was that nothing on the server ever ran it. `buggy_tick_all` (`packages/simulation/local_game.h`, the same function both the server and the lobby's local sim use) now ticks `vehicle_burn_tick` against `b->health` **in place**, so smoke at 150 and fire at 75 happen to the authoritative car: the hull genuinely drains 0.25/tick once alight, the fuse reaches the tank at 300 burning ticks, and the **detonation is `explode_splash` on the authority** with PARENA's own `blast_damage`/`blast_radius_milli`, which means allies see a car that is dying rather than one that merely looks like it is, and a driver who parks a burning car next to a teammate damages the teammate. The lobby keeps its own ladder for the *look* (BR's fx-fire-rise/fall flame, the flickering light, the smoke), still driven from the replicated health — now falling on its own, because the server is the one taking it down. Seeded from the car's own id so fuse and flicker are that car's on every host, and reset when a car is replaced rather than inheriting a dead one's burn. `buggy_tick_all` gained the clock (`now_ms`: `get_server_time()` on the server, `cmd_time` in `local_update`) because a blast needs a timestamp for hit feedback; the header's forward prototype moved with it — the first build failed with a real `conflicting types for 'buggy_tick_all'`, which is exactly what that prototype block exists to catch. Verified: `make -B server` links (the authority compiles the new path), `make test-vehicle-burn` still passes every check, the whole client `main.c` cross-compiles for mingw (so the lobby's own `local_game.h` path is unchanged in shape), and a live headless `./bin/shank_server --dungeon` boot generated its zone and ran without the burn tick faulting. NOT verified, named: **no live two-client burn was driven** — there is no scripted way here to crash a car to 75 hp on a server with an occupant, so the claim that allies see it is reasoning from the replicated health field plus the shared deterministic ladder, not an observed session; smoke is still modelled and not drawn as its own plume; and the C-110's cargo ramp, airborne spawn and cargo drops remain unbuilt.

- **feat(anim): the Mixamo half is finished — a binder that produces the thirteen-clip bundle, and a selector that loads and plays it.** The previous anim entry ended by naming exactly this gap: *"no .gband-from-Mixamo importer in this repo, the kit loader still takes four clip names, and nothing is drawn"*. The shipped assets are real Mixamo-derived clips named after their source (`UAL1_Standard_Idle_Loop`, `Mike_Idle`, ...), and the engine loads by contract name, so the missing piece was never a DCC importer — it was the two ends. **Binder** (`tools/mixamo_clipset.py`): given a directory of real `.gband` + `.gband.json` pairs and either a `--map` JSON or `--auto` keyword resolution, it writes `<out>/anim_idle.gband(+.json)` … `anim_climb_loop`, preserving the channel order verbatim (it *is* the data layout), the content hash, the tick rate and the duration, and owning only the contract fields: state name, index, source clip, and loop metadata that mirrors `anim_rules.prn` (postures loop over their full duration; actions are one-shot with `end_tick` 0). It refuses, by name, the failures that would otherwise be a silently wrong animation: a missing state, a binary that is not `GBND`/v1 or whose length disagrees with its header, a manifest whose `tick_rate`/`duration_ticks`/`channels` disagree with the binary, **channel data that no longer matches its own `content_hash`** (a clip edited on disk without rehashing), and **clips from two different `skeleton_hash`es** — the one error the engine could never see, because channels are matched by name and a shoulder from another rig is still spelled `Shoulder.L.qx`. Ambiguous `--auto` candidates are an error listing them, never a coin flip. **Selector** (`packages/simulation/anim_clipset.h`, header-only, no GL/SDL): loads all thirteen through `gseq_clip_load`, ticks the chosen clip at its own `tick_rate`, wraps looping states by duration, **holds a one-shot's last tick** (a reload snapping back to frame 0 reads as a glitch), starts the rules' own crossfade on entry, and falls back — run → idle → whatever loaded — so a rig with four clips animates instead of drawing nothing, while `anim_clipset_tick` returns -1 when nothing loaded so a caller knows not to draw a pose at all. Verified: `make test-mixamo-clipset` — **17 checks** over real Mike/Leela assets: auto-mapping a 13-clip rig, all outputs contract-named, channels and hashes preserved, posture/action loop metadata, a missing state refused by name, `--allow-partial`, tampered data caught by hash, two rigs refused, a truncated binary refused, ambiguity refused, `--dry-run` writing nothing, and a shared clip warning instead of failing. `make test-anim-clipset` — **34 checks** through GOLDENBAND's real loader: thirteen clips load, channels and rates come from the manifest, a posture wraps rather than holds, RUN/FIRE/MELEE are selected from real `PlayerState` fields with FIRE marked additive and MELEE outranking it, entering a state restarts at tick 0 and begins the crossfade, a one-shot holds `duration-1`, a crouch owns the pose, a one-clip bundle plays idle for everything and still yields a pose, and an empty bundle returns -1/NULL rather than garbage. Two real problems that run caught: the selector resolved the **previous** `state` instead of the wanted one (so every test stayed IDLE — a genuine bug, not a test artifact), and my first assertion used a 16 ms step against 30 Hz clips, which is zero whole ticks. NOT done, named: **nothing draws from the clip set yet** — the player body is still `compute_player_anim_pose`, the NPC kit loader still takes idle/walk/greet/dance, and the sampled channels have not been run through forward kinematics into a skinned draw; that is a renderer slice and this box has no GPU or display to verify one.

- **feat(stats): GFD's 23 jobs finally reach this engine as numbers — table copied from its own source, mapping ours, in PARENA.** Phase 4 of `docs/GFD_UNIFICATION.md`. The founder: *"the stat progression stuff can all be tied together"*. Read before writing: GFD is checked out at `../GoblinFoxDragon`, and its job model is `server/job/job.go` — 23 real jobs in `AllJobs` order (**including GFD's own non-canonical 23rd, ASN**), each with base HP/MP, per-level curves, and seven primaries. That table is copied **verbatim** into `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/stats_bridge.prn` (generated to `packages/simulation/stats_bridge.c` by `scripts/gen_stats_bridge.sh` / `make regen-stats-bridge`), because retuning a job is GFD's decision and this file must not quietly invent a different Warrior. Everything **derived** is this engine's, in I32 permille, because GFD resolves damage against a mob registry and a dice model that do not exist here: haste as a **multiplier on a weapon's own ROF ticks** (never a rewrite of it — a knife stays a knife), a damage **scalar** on `WPN_STATS`, movement narrow enough (850..1150) that the vehicles and terrain tuning still read right, crit off DEX, evade off AGI+level capped at 35% because a 60 Hz shooter cannot sell invisible hits, and health clamped to **255** because `NetPlayer.health` is an `unsigned char` and capping beats wrapping to zero. The boundary the plan asked for is kept: `packages/simulation/stats_bridge.h` is a flat `StatsBridge` of function pointers over plain ints, so **no C file learns about 23 jobs**, and it carries identical C defaults so any binary or test can use the bridge without linking generated code (the same contract `zone_rules.h`/`widget_rules.h`/`slide_rules.h` keep). Two behaviours are total on purpose: a negative job id reads as WAR, an id past the table as ASN (different cases, different meaning), and levels clamp to 1..75. Verified: `make test-stats-bridge` — **40 checks, 0 failed**: every one of the 23 jobs' eight base values matches an **independent transcription of `job.go`** (so a slip in either place fails instead of shipping a nerfed Warrior); the id order is GFD's own; `NIN` resolves to 12 while `nin` and `QQQ` do not; the generated rules and the header agree on **every derived number for all 23 jobs at levels −2..80** and on cooldown across ROF 0..60 x haste 600..1400; GFD's level curve is exact (`WAR` 90 at level 1, 105 at 2, linear to 75, flat past it); melee jobs have **no MP at any level** (job.go's own rule) while casters grow per level; a level-1 BLM is squishier than a WAR and a level-75 anything caps at 255 rather than wrapping; `NIN` has more haste and evade than `WAR` without exceeding the caps; a 20-tick knife ROF never becomes 0 or a 999-tick monster; and an out-of-range id is a real lookup, not a read past the table. That run also caught a genuine inconsistency in **my own** contract: the name helper clamped an unknown id to ASN while its doc promised WAR — fixed in the helper, and the rules' comment now states the real (and deliberately asymmetric) fallback. NOT done, named: **nothing applies a job to a live match yet** — no call site reads the bridge, so a player is still 100 HP and the stock ROF whatever a save says; equipment and elemental affinities are untouched (they need GFD's item model); nothing persists; and the dungeon's `difficulty` tier is generated but not yet fed into a stat curve.

- **feat(zones): a world announces itself on the LAN, so an ally runs `--coop-find` instead of being told an IP.** The last named gap in phase 3: `--coop-host` made hosting one command, but the joiner still needed an address typed at them. New `packages/simulation/zone_beacon.{h,c}`: a **fixed 48-byte** beacon (magic, version, game port, players, seed, NUL-padded zone id), announced by the server on the **same 1 Hz beat as `PACKET_ZONE_STATE`** to the broadcast address **and to loopback** — the broadcast is the LAN's copy, and the loopback copy is what lets the host's own finder see its world and what makes the discovery test run on a box with no broadcast route. It carries **identity only**, exactly like the zone packet: a joiner still generates the dungeon locally from the seed, so nothing about the geometry is transferred and the beacon stays 48 bytes forever. `zone_beacon_find` binds the beacon port, waits with a 100 ms `select` loop, and drops anything that is not a valid beacon (that port is open to every process on the LAN) — and the address it returns is the **datagram's source**, never a field inside the packet, so a forged beacon can only point a player at a host that will not answer. The id rule is shared with `--coop-host` (`coop_host_zone_valid`) rather than restated, because the decodable id is still a filename on the receiving side (`var/maps/proc_<id>.json`). Server: `--beacon-port N` (0 disables advertising), announced only while the zone is live, and it advertises `g_server_game_port` — the port this process actually bound, not the constant 6969, since a beacon pointing at 6969 while the world runs elsewhere sends allies to nothing. Client: `--coop-find [beacon-port]` listens, then joins the same `MODE_QUEUE` path the host uses. Verified: `make test-zone-beacon` — **33 checks, 0 failed**: encode/decode round-trips zone, seed, players and port; reserved bytes stay zero; and the refusals are the real work — a short, over-long, NULL or wrong-magic datagram, a future version, an empty id, an **unterminated** id (no NUL inside its field), an id that is a path (`../etc`), an id that looks like a flag (`-rf`), game port 0 and a privileged port are all refused; a too-small encode buffer and an invalid zone/port are never advertised; a negative player count clamps to 0 instead of wrapping into the packet. Then the live half: the test starts the actual server on `--port 7351 --beacon-port 7352`, waits for it to write its zone, calls the same `zone_beacon_find` the client calls, and asserts it heard the world **named the one that is running**, with **the real game port**, a sane player count, the seed, and a source address that parses as IPv4. `make test-coop-host` still 40/40 (the server source list gained `zone_beacon.c`/`coop_host.c`), and the whole client `main.c` (with `--coop-find` wired) cross-compiles for mingw with no errors. NOT verified, named: **the client's own find-and-connect was compiled, not run** — `make lobby` cannot link here (`../EDITOR.GAME` absent, no native SDL2 headers) and there is no display, so the live proof is the server-side beacon plus the finder, not a joiner's window. Discovery is also **same-broadcast-domain only** (no relay, no internet lobby), and the beacon says nothing about map identity beyond the zone id and seed — a host running a hand-authored `--level` advertises only its zone.

- **feat(zones): hosting a LAN world is one command — `--coop-host` starts the server and joins it.** Phase 3 of `docs/GFD_UNIFICATION.md`, and the piece the previous entry named as missing. `./bin/shank_lobby --coop-host [zone]` now spawns the real `bin/shank_server` for that zone as a child process (`packages/simulation/coop_host.c`, same fork/exec and `CreateProcessA(CREATE_NO_WINDOW)` pattern `lobby_launch_nock` already uses at `--port 8787`), points `SERVER_HOST` at loopback, and enters **`MODE_QUEUE`** through the exact `reset_client_render_state_for_net(); net_connect();` path the menu's TDM tile uses — so the host is a networked player, not a local special case, and an ally on the LAN joins the host's world with `--host <host-lan-ip>` and the same netcode. Deliberately not the existing `--dungeon` flag: that builds the zone in-process for solo play and has no server anyone can reach, which is why it could never be co-op. Two decisions in there are load-bearing rather than convenience. **The UDP port probe**: a UDP port has no `accept`, so `coop_host_port_busy` binds it briefly and lets go — which is what makes relaunching **attach** to a world that is still up instead of spawning a second server that cannot bind and dies, and what makes quitting-and-rejoining the same dungeon work at all. **Zone ids are validated at the flag** (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, never starting with `-`) because the id is a real filename component on both sides (`var/maps/proc_<zone>.json`) *and* an argv element, which is two injection surfaces for one unchecked string. A failure never writes `SERVER_HOST`/`SERVER_PORT`, so a missing binary or a refused bind leaves the player in the lobby with the reason on screen (`app_launch_status`) instead of silently connecting to the internet default. Verified: `make test-coop-host` — **40 checks, 0 failed**: defaults are the server's own defaults; a zone with a slash, `..`, a leading `-`, a space, a dot or a backslash is refused, as are a privileged and an out-of-range port; the child argv is exactly `{ bin, --port, 6969, --dungeon, <zone>, NULL }` with the port as a decimal string; the port probe reads free on an unused port, **busy against a socket the test really binds**, and free again after close; and then the live part — the test calls the same `coop_host_start` the lobby calls against the actual `bin/shank_server`, gets a pid, **waits for `var/maps/proc_coop_selftest.json` to appear** (the server's own output, not the fork's return code), confirms the port is held, confirms a **second start attaches instead of spawning a twin**, stops it, and confirms the port is released. `coop_host.c` also cross-compiles clean for mingw (`x86_64-w64-mingw32-gcc -Wall -Wextra -Werror`), which is the branch that runs on Windows. NOT done, named: **no LAN broadcast/discovery** — an ally still types the host's IP, nothing announces the world; **the server outlives the lobby** (a child process, so quitting the host leaves the world up until it is killed — deliberate for now, but a policy that needs a decision); **no in-process host**; and the client's own `--coop-host` wiring is **reviewed, not compiled on this box** — `make lobby` cannot link here at all because `../EDITOR.GAME` is not checked out and there are no native SDL2 headers, so `main.c` could only be compiled for mingw, which already fails on pre-existing `empire_*` implicit declarations unrelated to this change.

- **feat(zones): a LAN client joins the SAME procedural dungeon from its seed, not from a level transfer.** Founder: *"the first person to enter it it generates and then everyone else enters the same one"*. The server now hosts a zone: `./bin/shank_server --dungeon [zone]` generates it once, applies it through the ordinary custom-level path, and broadcasts **`PACKET_ZONE_STATE`** (`packages/common/protocol.h`, wired at 1 Hz beside the snapshot) carrying the zone id, its seed, occupancy, the registry's state and how long it has been empty. No boxes cross the wire: a client decodes the packet with the new `packages/simulation/zone_sync.h` and **rebuilds the identical layout from the seed** with the same `proc_dungeon.h` the host used, which is only sound because generation is deterministic (`make test-proc-dungeon` proves one seed writes byte-identical JSON). The reload rule is the real work: `zone_sync_should_reload` returns true only when the zone id or the seed changed, so a repeated 1 Hz packet is a no-op instead of rebuilding the world — and with it every spray, decal and cached shot — every second. Occupancy on the server is the connected player count: the first client in generates, the last one out starts the fifteen-minute countdown, expiry deletes the layout file and clears the seed, and the next arrival regenerates from a **new** seed, which is exactly what makes the clients reload. An idle zone with the same seed does not reload (it is still the same dungeon). Verified: `make test-zone-sync` (20 checks) — the packet has its own wire type, round-trips every field, and **refuses** a short datagram, the wrong type, an empty id and an unterminated id (the id becomes `var/maps/proc_<id>.json`, so that refusal is a path-safety boundary, not pedantry); the same zone at 1 Hz does not reload while a new seed does; a missing packet and an uninitialized zone never reload; an idle zone's countdown survives the wire; an absurd player count clamps instead of wrapping. Plus a **live headless server run**: `./bin/shank_server --dungeon dungeon_1` generated `PROC dungeon_1 <seed>` (41 boxes, 4 spawners, 3 materials, 3 widgets) and wrote `var/maps/proc_dungeon_1.json`, which the real parser then loaded back. That run caught a real bug this fixes: the zone id was copied into itself with `snprintf` (overlapping buffers are undefined, and glibc emptied it), so the server generated a zone and then advertised `''` and stopped broadcasting. Also `make test-proc-dungeon` (46), `test-widget-circuit` (51), `test-widget-runtime` (48), `make check-windows` (101 sources) and `make -B server`. NOT built, named: **no `--coop-host`** that starts the server in-process and advertises on the LAN (phase 3), no LAN discovery, and **no explicit "enter zone" action** — on the server, occupancy is presence, so the zone generates when the first client connects rather than when someone walks through a door.

- **feat(zones): shared, non-instanced procedural dungeons — one seed, one layout, fifteen empty minutes and then a new one.** Founder: *"we need to start unifying gfd and shankpit archipelligo ... like diablo where we can travel to a map and do it as a dungeon it can be procedurally generated ... the first person to enter it it generates and then everyone else enters the same one ... there should be a grace period of 15 minutes ... dungeons are not instanced they are generated and then they exist until everyone leaves + 15 minutes ... all mods based PARENA based"*, then *"including bringing in the GFD exclusive levels"*. GFD was **cloned and read before anything was written** (it is not in this tree, and its real contents are not what the pasted specs claimed: `apps/mud` is the text state engine with no spatial data, Town is 17 building seeds plus expansion blocks in `packages/world/town_map.c`, and Meadow is a 44×44 goblin *simulation* seed with no geometry at all — `docs/GFD_UNIFICATION.md` records this and phases the merge). Phase 1 is the deterministic zone core, in PARENA: `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/zone_rules.prn` (generated to `packages/simulation/zone_rules.c` by `scripts/gen_zone_rules.sh` / `make regen-zone-rules`) owns the 900-second grace, 8 live zones, and every layout number — room count 3..5, sizes 24/32/40, wall height, 8-unit doors, 52-unit ring, spawners, material kind and difficulty 1..5. `packages/simulation/zone_registry.h` is the shared-zone table: **the first entrant CREATES and gets the seed, every later entrant JOINS with the same seed**, a re-entry inside the countdown cancels it and keeps the layout, an occupied zone never expires, and expiry (only at `players == 0 && idle >= 900`) clears the slot so the next entrant generates a **new** dungeon. `packages/simulation/proc_dungeon.h` builds one: a hub plus 2–4 cardinal rooms joined by corridors, axis-aligned, enclosed, ground plane, spawners, and a gate authored as a **real circuit** (lever → 45-tick relay → machine pointing at the gate's own box) rather than a bespoke door type. Determinism is the point and is tested as such: no `rand()`, no float trig, no time — two generations from one seed save **byte-identical JSON**, and a different seed does not. `scripts/import_gfd_levels.py` converts GFD's Town into `var/maps/gfd_town.json` (17 named buildings + expansion blocks at scale 3.0, a perimeter wall, materials, spawners) and says plainly that Meadow has no geometry to import. Reachable in game: **F7** descends into `dungeon_N` and `--dungeon [zone]` starts straight into one, both through the ordinary custom-level loader, so lighting, destructible brick, the widget circuit and zombies population all apply. Verified: `make test-proc-dungeon` — PARENA's rules equal the compiled-in defaults across 100+ samples (including the expiry predicate at every 50 s); the same seed builds the same box count and **identical saved bytes**; the saved level reloads through the real parser with its gate box index, circuit and spawners intact; the gate opens **on the relay's own 45-tick delay** (not on the press) through `widget_runtime`; the second entrant gets the same seed; one leave is not idle and the last leave starts the countdown; 899,999 ms is not enough and an occupied zone never expires at 9,000,000 ms; expiry calls back with the zone id, clears the slot, and the next entrant gets a different seed which produces a different dungeon; a full table refuses a ninth entry rather than sharing a stranger's world; two zones expire independently. Plus `make test-widget-circuit` (51), `make test-widget-runtime` (48), `make check-windows` (101 sources now that `widget_rules.c` and `zone_rules.c` are in `LOBBY_SRC` — a real latent link break this caught, since `main.c` had been calling `widget_rules_install()` without the generated file in the source list) and `make -B server`. NOT built, named: **no zone packet**, so a LAN friend cannot join a zone yet (the seed is host-local; phase 2), no menu tile (F7 and the CLI are the paths), no GFD state server / stat bridge (phase 4), and no Meadow level, because there is no geometry in GFD to convert.

- **feat(widgets): the circuit now RUNS in a level — F at a lever, a panel on a terminal, and a lift that actually waits.** Founder: *"general interface like F to interact and then after that it needs to be able to go into custom programmed affordances ... the main thing we are trying to do is bring the lab equipment to life ... an elevator is a widget of widgets"*, then *"focusing on delivering value and integrating it all into game modes and affordances the player actually has access to like zombies mode"*. The circuit existed and nothing called it — a lever, delay, transistor and motor were a table of numbers a test drove. New `packages/simulation/widget_runtime.h` is the host half: **plates are fed from the bodies actually standing on them** (authored radius with a vertical window, so a body six units up is not on the mat), **machines move their authored `box_index`** through the same `phys_set_custom_level_box_y` a story door uses, and **every register is mirrored into EduVM** (`eduvm_host_widget_set`), so a script's `read_sensor(reg)` and the physical switch read one number. It is deliberately free of GL, SDL, physics and the education package — the caller passes two sinks — which is what lets `make test-widget-runtime` drive the whole thing headlessly. Wired into `apps/lobby` from `lobby_edu_widgets_init`, **the same loader every custom level uses, so zombies mode, LAB and deathmatch all get it** (zombies loads through that loader, not a special path). `F` is the general interface: a lever toggles in place and **consumes the press** (so it cannot also board a vehicle on the same key), anything with a register opens its **panel**. A panel freezes movement, dials UP/DOWN in 500s, and owns ESC — leaving a panel never drops you into the pause menu. `draw_widget_affordances` draws the prompt and the panel in the existing stroke font, and the per-widget legacy box path is skipped for any box the circuit owns, so a relay's delay is real instead of two systems writing one lift. Two real bugs this run caught and fixed: the first tick of a level pushed every machine's box as a *change* (so a lift reported moving before anyone touched it — the placement now happens once, deliberately, and the test asserts it), and a terminal's dialled setpoint was **clobbered by the same tick that mirrored circuit power** (0..15) a keystroke later. A third was in my own test, not the engine: a level with no walls silently detaches `box_index` (the parser refuses a box that does not exist), which is correct behaviour the fixture had to respect. Verified: `make test-widget-runtime` (48 checks) — the first tick places both boxes once, a lever -> relay -> lift does not move for four ticks and opens on the fifth, **and does not drop early on the falling edge either**; a plate alone opens nothing while its transistor's gate lever is off, the same plate opens the door once the gate is on, stepping off closes it, a body six units above the plate never presses it, and the lift is undisturbed by plate traffic; F on a terminal opens its panel, three UP presses make 1500, five ticks later `read_sensor`'s register still holds 1500 and the mirror fired **once, on the change**; BACK returns the world, a closed panel swallows nothing, and closing it does not reset the machine; busy suppresses the prompt and walking away releases it after the hold window; a level with no widgets ticks, focuses and uses as a no-op. Plus `make test-widget-circuit` (51 checks, still green after the setpoint change), `make check-windows` and `make -B server`. NOT done, named: **no lever/centrifuge/terminal mesh** (a node is still a box coloured by its power), no editor wiring UI beyond the pin fields, a panel setpoint is display-only to a script because EduVM's widget register is boolean by contract, and there is **no widget packet** — the circuit runs in the lobby's local simulation, so a server-authoritative match would draw the boxes without the interactions.

- **feat(widgets): composable world widgets — a real circuit, an F interface, and panels that hand over to a script.** Founder: *"we already have a widget primative ... general interface like F to interact and then after that it needs to be able to go into custom programmed affordances ... the main thing we are trying to do is bring the lab equipment to life ... the widgets need to be composible ... think light switches, basic circuits, an elevator is a widget of widgets ... bring in edu vm script it all needs to play nice with that interpreter"*. The audit: the schema, the EduVM widget host and the F/BTN_USE path already existed (`LevelEduWidget`, `edu_bindings.h`, `story_buttons.h`), so this adds the missing middle — **the circuit**. `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/widget_rules.prn` (generated to `packages/simulation/widget_rules.c` by `scripts/gen_widget_rules.sh` / `make regen-widget-rules`) defines five node kinds on one **0..15 power integer** (DEADWEIGHT's own cable tiers): LEVER (stays where you put it), PLATE (host-written sensor, a script can never forge one), DELAY (a relay — output follows input N ticks late, **both edges**), TRANSISTOR (a real AND: passes input only while its gate is powered) and MACHINE (a motor: gate, bridge, portal, crate, lift). `packages/simulation/widget_circuit.h` is the host: nodes loaded from the level, a two-phase tick in authoring order, every node's live value mirrored into its EduVM register, the proximity F prompt with hysteresis and a busy flag, and a PANEL mode that takes the keys so a script-driven affordance can own the screen. The level schema grew three keys (`input_pin`, `gate_pin`, `delay_ticks`) with `-1` defaults, and `machine_type` now accepts 6 LEVER / 7 DELAY / 8 TRANSISTOR alongside the original education machines 1..5 — a mis-wired pin is dropped at load, so a bad level degrades to "that switch does nothing", never a feedback loop. New `docs/WIDGET_AFFORDANCES.md` is the **agent-facing contract**: the JSON keys, the five kinds, and recipes for a light switch, a security door, an elevator (lever -> delay -> machine) and a lab centrifuge — which is the founder's own "a way to tell our agent to generate rich affordances". Verified: `make test-widget-circuit` — lever -> delay -> motor only runs after the relay closes **and stops late on the falling edge**; plate -> transistor -> door is a real AND (gate off closes the door with someone still standing on the plate); registers mirror live power and an unowned register reads **-1, not a fake zero**; the F prompt respects busy, picks the nearest in reach, and holds then releases; USE on a lever stays in the world while USE on a machine opens its panel; panel UP/DOWN clamp at 8000 and at 0 and BACK releases it; keys in the world are not swallowed; the PARENA curve equals the compiled-in defaults sample for sample; and bad wiring (pin past the end, empty id, unknown machine type) is dropped. Plus `make test-edu-widgets` and `make test-level-save` (the schema still round-trips). Two real problems the run caught: the first delay design only delayed the **rising** edge, which made a delay useless as a relay (both edges now, and the test asserts it), and the test target needed `gnu99` because `level_boxes.h` uses POSIX `popen`. NOT done, named: **nothing is drawn** (no prompt quad, no panel, no lever mesh), `widget_circuit_tick` is **not in the game loop yet** (the test drives it), there is **no editor wiring UI** beyond the pin fields, and DEADWEIGHT's back-EMF/overload is not modelled.

- **feat(render): metal, glass and a mirror material reflect the environment — sphere mapping, one extra draw, no assets.** Founder: *"we want to implement basic reflection in metal and glass materials i guess make a mirror material? it needs to be lightweight"*. The audit first: this renderer is fixed-function GL 1.x plus a couple of hand-loaded GL 2.0 passes (`material_shaders.h` already had specular, IPS/HPS emissive and a PARENA-tinted glass pass), so a **sphere map** is the cheap real option — the driver derives the lookup from the vertex normal and the camera basis, which means no render target, no second scene pass, no cube map, no shader and no per-frame CPU work. New `packages/render/reflection_map.h` is the environment as pixels (a pure-C, deterministic sphere map: bright sky above a warm horizon over dark ground, two cold "reflected sign" streaks, and a hot spot so a highlight visibly **travels** as you turn), and `packages/render/reflection.h` is the GL half: lazy texture creation at first use, `glTexGeni(GL_S/GL_T, GL_SPHERE_MAP)`, additive blend with depth-write off (a sheen, never an opaque coat), and a begin/end pair that **saves and restores the texture binding, texgen, blend and depth mask** — refusing a nested begin rather than corrupting its saved state. Strengths are PARENA's, not the renderer's: `mat-reflection-strength-permille(kind)` in `material_rules.prn` (**mirror 900, metal 380, glass 320, everything else 0**), so brick/concrete/wood cost exactly nothing. Wired inside `material_shaders.h` where the materials already live: `draw_material_specular_box` adds the sheen for anything at or above its own 0.6 metal threshold, `draw_material_glass_box` adds a subtle one after its transparent pass, and a new `draw_material_mirror_box` draws a near-black base so the environment IS the surface. Verified: `make test-reflection` — the map is byte-identical across builds, every texel opaque, **200+ distinct graded colours**, sky much brighter *and* bluer than the ground (so highlights are not upside-down), the hot spot 736 vs 99, the streaks visible, a degenerate size writing nothing, and the shipped strengths ordered mirror > metal > glass > 0 with an out-of-range kind reflecting nothing. Plus `make test-material-rules` (still green), `make check-windows` (99 lobby sources, which is what compiles the GL half through `material_shaders.h`) and `make -B server`. Honest limits, named: a sphere map has **no parallax** — it does not slide as you walk past and cannot reflect the actual level — so a "mirror" reads as polished chrome, not a true mirror; the real alternative costs an extra scene render per frame, which this target cannot pay. The GL state machine itself is **compiled but not run** here (no GPU, no SDL2/GL headers on this box, so the headless test covers the pixels and the numbers only), and nothing in the build yet *selects* a mirror material — `draw_material_mirror_box` exists for the material list that adds one.

- **feat(movement): a crouch-slide down a hillside now speeds you up — the same slope physics a board feels.** Founder, on the Skate spec paste: *"can we also make it so doing a slide after a slide jump in normal shankpit physics where there is a terrain like in voxworld map the slide should use the same kind of physics as the skateboarding where sliding down a hill accelerates you or whatever"*. The engine already had a slide (`apply_friction` drops to `SLIDE_FRICTION` above 0.75 units/tick while crouched) but it treated a 45 degree hillside exactly like a flat car park, because **nothing ever read the terrain normal**: a slide could only ever slow you down. Now it does, in three pieces. `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/slide_rules.prn` (generated to `packages/simulation/slide_rules.c` by `scripts/gen_slide_rules.sh` / `make regen-slide-rules`) owns the curve as permille: how steep a face is (`(1000 - normal.y) x 3`, saturating past ~48 degrees), how much a downhill heading earns, the acceleration (constant in speed, the way gravity is), how much of the existing slide **drag** a slope keeps (`1000` on the flat — unchanged — down to 300 at full steep, because the same 1% scrub that makes a flat slide glide would otherwise cancel the hill's own pull), and the cap at 1.20 units/tick: above `MAX_SPEED`'s 0.95 on purpose, since a hill is the one way to outrun a sprint. `packages/simulation/slide_rules.h` carries numerically identical compiled-in defaults so every existing test that includes `physics.h` still links without a generated file (the test proves the two agree sample for sample), `slide_rules_host.h` installs PARENA's copy at lobby startup, and `physics.h` gains `phys_slide_slope_tick` (gravity along the surface gradient, vector-clamped to the cap) called once per tick from `local_game.h` right after `apply_friction`. Two real bugs the test caught, both mine: the heightfield normal **points against the gradient**, so my "downhill" was upside-down and a slide accelerated *uphill*; and the sub-0.75 crouch-walk scrub (90%/tick) pinned a slide that started from rest at a 0.008 crawl, so a downhill heading now uses the slide branch at any speed. Verified: `make test-slide-slope` — a real 1:1 (45 degree) heightfield sampled through the engine's own `terrain_sample_normal`: **one tick of a flat slide is exactly the old formula to 1e-5, the slope step never fires on flat ground, and a flat slide still comes to rest**; a downhill slide reaches 1.09 after 60 ticks against 0.00 on the flat (and outruns `MAX_SPEED`), pinned at exactly 1.20 after 120 ticks, accelerating along the downhill axis; a slide from rest on a hill starts moving; an uphill slide never gains speed, stalls, and is turned back downhill; airborne/standing/in-vehicle all no-op; and PARENA's curve equals the header's defaults across 3,608 samples. Plus `make test-wall-ride` (the existing airborne wall ride is untouched), `make check-windows` (98 lobby sources) and `make -B server`. NOT done, named: **terrain only** — a rotated box ramp has a face normal per box rather than a field under the player, so a slide on an authored ramp gets nothing yet; the server installs no PARENA copy (its compiled-in defaults are the same numbers, and the test enforces that equality, but a future retune would reach the lobby first); and the rest of the pasted Skate spec (dual-stick flick detection, third-person orbit, grind snapping, manuals) is **not** in this change — this is the slide-on-a-hill half the founder actually asked for.

- **feat(anim): the thirteen-clip animation set gets a decision and a naming contract — the ragdoll half of the spec already existed.** Founder paste: the Mixamo + XPBD ragdoll engineering spec. The audit changed what got built: **§4 (ragdoll) is already done and wired** — `packages/simulation/ragdoll_pool.h` runs 17 Winter/Dempster-mass segments on GOLDENBAND's grb XPBD solver with per-death-kind timings from PARENA, and `apps/lobby/src/main.c` already spawns, steps, evicts and skins them on death (`g_rd_pool`, `ragdoll_pool_get_skin_matrices`). **§2/§3 did not exist at all**: nothing decided *which* clip a tick belongs to (the player's body is drawn procedurally by `compute_player_anim_pose`, and NPC kits only know AUTO/GREET/DANCE), and no clip-naming contract existed for an imported rig to satisfy — so an importer had nothing to conform to and no way to fail loudly. New `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/anim_rules.prn` (generated to `packages/simulation/anim_rules.c` by `scripts/gen_anim_rules.sh` / `make regen-anim-rules`) is the decision, in a priority order the spec leaves out and a player notices immediately: **melee swing > ability > fire > reload > airborne (jump-start for its own 6-tick window, then in-air, plus the reserved climb state) > crouch/slide > walk/run/idle by speed band**. It also owns the metadata a state machine needs and a spec dump usually forgets: which states loop (exactly the six postures — a looping reload is the classic bug), that **only the recoil is upper-body additive** so a player can shoot while running, a per-state crossfade (60 ms for a shot, 150 for a posture), the walk/run bands, the melee windows (**20 knife / 28 katana / 42 hammer**, matching `protocol.h`'s own weapon table so the clip window and the damage window are one number), and the eye height. New `packages/simulation/anim_state.h` is the host: it reads only real `PlayerState` fields (`on_ground`, `crouching`, `vx/vz`, `jump_timer`, `is_shooting`, `reload_timer`, `katana_slash_timer`, `dash_timer`), maps `WPN_*` to the three melee classes, and publishes the **clip-name contract** (`anim_idle` … `anim_climb_loop`, thirteen unique lowercase stems that become `<asset>/<name>.gband`) so an importer renames its Mixamo clips or fails at load instead of silently playing the idle for everything. Two honest corrections to the spec, named rather than copied: its crouch eye heights (4.0 → 2.5) are from a different build — this engine's is `EYE_HEIGHT` 2.59, and the test **asserts the rule equals that constant** (via a one-function probe TU, `anim_engine_constant.c`, because `physics.h` carries unused statics that would trip this repo's `-Werror`) — and its `ANIM_SLIDE` has **no real producer here**: this engine's crouch-slide is a friction change and the wall ride is ctrl-in-air, neither sets a timer, so `slide_ticks` is an explicit caller argument rather than an invented field. Verified: `make test-anim-state` — 90 checks: every state has a name and no two share one, an out-of-range state falls back to idle instead of null, band edges land exactly at their thresholds (59 idle / 60 walk / 299 walk / 300 run), backwards movement is RUN not IDLE, crouch owns locomotion at any speed, jump-start ends exactly at its window and a walk-off falls straight to IN_AIR, **a swing outranks firing and reloading, and firing survives being airborne**, an ability outranks a slide, exactly six postures loop and all seven actions are one-shots, a shot blends faster than a posture, knife/katana/hammer windows match the real weapon ids while a rifle has none, the standing eye height equals the engine's own, and the real `PlayerState` wrapper drives IDLE/RUN/CROUCH/FIRE/RELOAD/MELEE/JUMP from those fields (a diagonal is 0.424 units/tick, not 0.6). Also `make check-windows` (98 lobby sources compile for mingw) and `make -B server`. NOT done, stated plainly: **nothing is drawn from this yet** — no imported skin has these thirteen clips on disk, the kit loader still takes four clip names (idle/walk/greet/dance) rather than thirteen, and the player draw path is still procedural; `anim_state.h` is not included by `main.c` yet, so the mingw check proves `anim_rules.c` compiles, not that the header does on Windows. There is also no `.gband`-from-Mixamo importer in this repo (the spec's Blender script does not exist here), so the mapping is a contract to import *into*, not a converter.

- **feat(bedrockracers): the stadium exists natively, and its mesh is checked against the surface the car is clamped to.** Founder: *"implement bedrock racers in the native shankpit client pixel for pixel using shankpit unified physics ... also the geometry of the level needs to be accurate with the smooth curved walls"* and *"we need to dedupe stuff into parena as much as possible so the bedrock racers wasm stuff leaks into shankpit"*. The shape is **PARENA's**, not a re-derived C copy: `PARENA/stdlib/bedrockracers/web/br_stadium.prn` (generated to `packages/simulation/br_stadium.c` by `scripts/gen_br_stadium.sh` / `make regen-br-stadium`) holds the exact rounded box the web game drives on — 520 × 520 in plan, 240 tall, **a 70-unit fillet on every edge**, the four upside-down goal shafts at ±450, and the rally loop's own scale/width so the road baked into BR's floor texture and the road this client draws come from one pair of constants. New `packages/simulation/br_stadium.h` is the native half: the SDF query (positive = air inside the room), the surface normal as the SDF's gradient by central difference (**continuous**, which is the entire reason wall-riding works: floor → fillet → wall → ceiling has no seam, where a faceted shell would give 12 normals per turn), the fan-grip curve, and an **exact** mesh — six flat faces inset by the radius, twelve quarter-cylinder edges, eight eighth-sphere corners. `packages/render/br_stadium_gl.h` draws it through the same immediate-mode GL path every world box already uses (no textures, no GLSL, no VBO), with a per-vertex tint so the curl reads. Wired into `LOBBY_SRC` and `SERVER_SRC` so the client that draws it and the server that collides with it link the same numbers. Verified: `make test-br-stadium` — the floor/ceiling/walls are on the surface and 5 units inside or outside are open air or solid respectively, a 45-degree point on the fillet returns a clean 45-degree normal, normals are unit length, **all 416 vertices of the quality-4 shell sit within 0.0000 of the SDF and every stored normal matches the gradient to a dot of 1.0000** (the property that stops a car driving through a wall it appears to touch), quality 6 is 936 quads, an absurd quality fills the pool instead of overrunning it, grip is weak when crawling and total from 2.0 units/tick, and the draw path receives exactly four vertices per quad in one batch. Two real bugs the test caught and this fixes: the grip curve *exceeded* 1000 permille between 400 and 1400 units/tick, and flipping the edge normals to point into the air also flipped the component the edge **position** used, pulling every fillet vertex 70 units into the wall. Also learned and named: this repo's PARENA emitter lowers only `I32`/`F64`/`Bool`/`String`, so the shape module is `F64` (`F32` refused to compile), written as small `defn`s rather than one `let` because the emitter's `let` binds typed scalars only. NOT done, and named rather than implied: **the stadium is not in any scene yet** — nothing builds the mesh at load, nothing draws it, no car is clamped to it, and there is no collision response, no goal-tower shafts in the SDF, and no faceted OBB destruction shell; the four goal towers are exported as numbers and nothing more. `docs/` carries no northstar for this yet, and no visual claim is possible here (no GPU or display).

- **feat(render): the Doom/Quake/Half-Life/GTA material vocabulary, as rules instead of a shader rewrite.** Founder paste (a spec dump proposing a `#version 330` PBR/VBO/voxel-lightmap pipeline) then: *"what other materials/shaders do we need for full doom/quake/half life/gta story telling?"* and *"anything missing from source 2 engine?"*. The audit came first and it changed the answer: SHANKPIT is fixed-function GL 1.x plus **one hand-loaded GL 2.0 shader path** (`gl_shader.h`, the flashlight beam and the fireball), so `#version 330 core`, VAOs, `sampler3D`/`glTexImage3D`, `glVertexAttribIPointer` and a deferred G-buffer **cannot compile or run here** — all named in `docs/RENDER_NORTHSTAR.md` §4, along with the pasted shader's fake PBR term and its claim of bit-identical GPU timing. What a story actually needs from a material is *animation*, so that is what was built, in PARENA where it can be deterministic: `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/material_rules.prn` (generated into `packages/simulation/material_rules.c`, `make regen-material-rules`) — five material kinds (plain, scrolling, frame-loop, palette-LUT, emissive-flicker) with **UV scroll speeds that wrap on the second** and a vertical component so ooze reads as thick, **frame-loop sheets** (`mat-frame-index(fps, frames, tick)`) for terminals/cameras/bomb timers, **all eight Quake light styles** (steady, strobe, unstable fluorescent, broken tube, pulse, dying, alarm, candle) as 0..1000 permille of a fixture's brightness, emissive faces floored so a lit terminal still reads in a bright room, the four screen tints (low health, submerged, acid, shield), a 256-entry palette wrap that never goes negative, and `vlm-axis-index-milli` — the clamped integer mapping that is the honest half of Source 2's `M_combined_light_probe_volume` (a baked grid sampled per face, not a 3D texture this build cannot bind). Verified: `make test-material-rules` (every style stays in 0..1000 over 600 ticks; the strobe really goes dark and really lights; the fluorescent drops out 1-19 times in 10 s; the broken tube is dark more than twice as often as lit; snaps and frames wrap on the second; all 8 frames of a 10 fps sheet are reached; health tint is 0 at full and monotonic in damage; submerged saturates; palette -1 wraps to 255 and 300 to 44; probe axes clamp at both ends and mid-grid), plus `make check-windows` (96 lobby sources) and `make -B server`. NOT done, and named in the doc: **nothing of this is drawn yet** — no probe bake, no UV-offset draw path, no palette LUT shader, no tint quad wiring, and no visual verification is possible here (no GPU/display). The doc ranks the build order and says plainly that a real palette shader is the only genuinely new fragment program the list needs.
- **feat(empire): influence is a currency you spend on bodies, and the map is a sphere of districts.** Founder: *"clone down EMPIRE ... clone down ECOWAR ... clone down REDGARDEN ... clone down TRAPX ... we need phone app affordances in shankpit to bring in the mp and to clone your troops in PARENA first mods based it should show the minimap of the nearby area and allow you to spawn in troops near you or near buildings you have enough influence over ... in empire mode the map itself should be a big sphere and it should be procedurally generated"*. All four repos cloned and read before writing anything (`docs/EMPIRE_NORTHSTAR.md` records what each one actually contains, which is not what their names suggest: EMPIRE is a bridge + deck UI with no world, ECOWAR is the arena game with 16 tested cards and **no in-match deck UI**, REDGARDEN holds the team AI and hero kits, TRAPX is the doctrine — alignment and pressure on a grid, no avatar, no direct unit control). Rules first, as asked: `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/empire_rules.prn` (generated into `packages/simulation/empire_rules.c` by `scripts/gen_empire_rules.sh` / `make regen-empire-rules`) defines **one currency** — influence at 2/s capped at 400, four troop kinds (25/35/40/60), a **clone at 130% of a recruit** that needs a veteran to copy, a squad cap of 6, a 14-unit spawn radius, TRAPX's three-way district alignment (yours/neutral/rival at 600‰ pressure) with income attached (full, a third, none), and **the sphere: 6/12/18/18/12/6 = 72 districts**, ring counts rising to the equator so districts keep their area — the reason a sphere beats a lat/long grid, where the phone's minimap would lie at the poles. New phone app **`BP_APP_EMPIRE`** (P → EMPIRE): a nine-cell minimap of the nearby districts (yours blue, rival red, yours-to-stand-on green) and the spawn/clone rows, painted as HTML+CSS by the same PELLEGRINO surface the kit pages use, with a real text fallback so the app is never a blank rectangle. The phone **never spends**: SELECT emits `BP_FX_EMPIRE_SPAWN`/`_CLONE` and the host re-checks the same PARENA gate (`empire_can_spawn`/`empire_clone_can`) before taking the influence and placing a body 2 units beside the player, so a retuned cost cannot let the UI and the world disagree. Verified: `make test-empire-rules` (costs, the clone premium, refusal at one point short, unknown kinds, the squad cap, clone-needs-a-veteran, district flip/income, 72 districts with the equator ring larger than the pole cap, spawn radius local), `make test-vehicle-burn`, `phone_test` (the app list still covers every app), `make check-windows` (96 lobby sources) and `make -B server`. Named, not faked: **the body is a story-AI slot, not a loyal soldier** — SHANKPIT has bodies and REDGARDEN has the friendly/enemy team model, and nothing yet joins them, so the placement is real and the allegiance is not; **no district simulation exists in the world**, so the minimap honestly shows you holding the block you stand on and its neighbours neutral; **the sphere is geometry only** — no procedural city generation and no streaming yet; and there is no wire format, so influence and ownership are single-player state.
- **feat(nock): every vehicle is placeable in a map — IDUNA NOCK, local NOCK, and the native loader.** Founder: *"we need nock affordances for spawning all the new vehicles in a map including iduna nock including local nock we already have a widget for buggy spawnerer"*. The buggy tile was the right idea with one vehicle in it. Its name convention is now the general case: **`vehicle_spawn_<kind>` / `vehicle_spawn_<kind>_<deg>`** with kind `buggy`, `rocket`, `heli`, `recon`, `fly`, `plane` — one thin wall, no schema change, so select/undo/clone/glTF/widget-flattening all keep working, and a **legacy `buggy_spawn` tile still works and is ALSO exported as kind `buggy`**, so nothing authored before this moves. IDUNA (`level_store.go`): `VehicleSpawnExport` + `vehicleSpawnsForExport` (max 24) alongside the untouched buggy list, `rotatedVehicleSpawnName` carries a placed widget's rotation into the spawn's yaw (a legacy buggy tile stays a legacy buggy tile), and the two flatten call sites now go through it. Native (`packages/world/level_boxes.h`): `LevelVehicleSpawn` + `vehicle_spawns` parsed and re-written (so `--edit-map` F6 round-trips it), with `LEVEL_VEHICLE_*` kinds and human aliases. `physics.h` gains one `g_custom_level_vehicle_*` set beside the buggy arrays; both `apps/server` and `apps/lobby` copy it through; and `local_game.h`'s one `scene_load(SCENE_CUSTOM_LEVEL)` places the whole roster where the map says — buggy/rocket into a `BuggyState` (`.rocket = 1` for the rocket car), heli into a hovers-ready `HelicopterState`, recon/fly into `DroneState` at their own kind, plane on the apron. Both editors got the affordance: IDUNA's level editor and widget editor each gain a kind `<select>` + **Add vehicle spawn** (a coloured pad per kind, so a garage bay reads at a glance), and local NOCK's canvas editor gains a **Vehicle** tool (key `v`), draws every placed spawn in its kind's colour, saves them into the level JSON, and mirrors the game's own placements from the new `vehicle_spawn` event (added to local NOCK's event allow-list). Verified: `make test-level-save` (the new list round-trips through the native writer/loader) and `make check-windows` compiling every lobby source. NOT verified: the IDUNA Go/TypeScript builds — this sandbox has no `go` or SDL2 — so those edits are reviewed, not compiled; and no live match was driven, so a placed C-110 is placed on the apron but has not been seen flying from a map yet.
- **feat(vehicles): a car smokes, catches fire, and only then blows up — drawn with Bedrock Racers' fire.** Founder: *"use the shaders from bedrock racers on fire for when a car is about to blow up or whatever"*. The look is not invented: `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/vehicle_burn_rules.prn` (generated to `packages/simulation/vehicle_burn_rules.c` by `scripts/gen_vehicle_burn_rules.sh` / `make regen-vehicle-burn-rules`, installed at startup by `vehicle_burn_rules_host.h`) copies BR's `br_fx.prn` fire curve — **fire-fx swells 25% of the remaining gap per tick (fx-fire-rise) and dies at 6% (fx-fire-fall)**, its light flickers between 1.4 and 2.4 "a car's worth", and its drama scalar jumps then ebbs — and adds the ladder BR does not have: hull ≤ 150 smokes, hull ≤ 75 is on fire, and the 75 points between the fire line and zero take **exactly 300 ticks (5 s)** at 0.25 hp/tick, so the warning window and the burn clock are the same number instead of two guesses. `packages/simulation/vehicle_burn.h` is the per-tick state machine (`vehicle_burn_tick` returns the ONE tick the car detonates; `vehicle_burn_light`/`vehicle_burn_drama` carry BR's flicker deterministically from the car's seed and tick; crash damage is mass × delta-v², staged to stay inside i32, ~22 of 300 hull for a full-speed buggy — reckless a few times, not fatal once). The lobby draws a burning buggy with the **same additive fireball shader the grenade/missile blast already uses** (`fireball_draw`, two billboards, sized by `fire_fx`), and the blast itself is the existing `fx_spawn_explosion` path. Two real bugs the new test caught and this fixes: the burn clock was inconsistent with the fire line when it was whole points per tick, and **the fire latched on `burning_ticks`, so a repaired car burned forever** — it is now a function of the hull, and a repair puts it out and restarts the fuse. Verified: `make test-vehicle-burn` — a healthy car is untouched by 10 s of ticking, 140 hull smokes with no flame and no hull loss, 4 ticks of fire is >0.6 fire-fx, **exactly one blast at ~296 ticks**, the flame is still >60% lit 5 ticks after a repair and out within 200, the flicker stays inside 1.3–2.45, a 900 kg buggy at 5 units/tick costs 22 hull and a scuff costs 0, **and the generated PARENA table must equal the header's defaults (checked field by field) so the game and the test cannot play different cars**. Limitation, named: the hull and the blast are the local/client sim's — there is no wire field for a burning car yet, so a networked match does not replicate the fire state, and smoke is modelled (and tested) but drawn only as the flame's arrival, not as its own dark plume.
- **feat(aircraft): the C-110 — a fixed-wing transport that has to be flown.** Founder: *"ok how about a basic prop plane c-110 or whatever the legendary ones are"*. Deliberately not a helicopter with wings: lift comes from AIRSPEED, so this is the one vehicle in the engine that cannot hover. New `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/airplane_rules.prn` (generated to `packages/simulation/airplane_rules.c` by `scripts/gen_airplane_rules.sh` / `make regen-airplane-rules`, installed by `airplane_rules_host.h`): top speed 12 units/tick, stall 4.0 clean and 3.2 with flaps, liftoff 6.0, thrust 0.06 units/tick² at full throttle against a speed-proportional drag curve (which is what makes the takeoff run a real runway), and **lift as a v² law in permille of weight, exactly 1000 at 6.0 units/tick and exactly 0 at or below the stall however the nose is pointed** — a full back stick in a stall does not save it, it just makes the dive uglier. New `packages/simulation/airplane_flight.h` is the integration: throttle is a **lever** (SPACE up, CTRL down, it holds where you leave it), W/S elevator, A/D aileron, the pilot's heading is a tiller only with the wheels down and slow, and **a bank is what turns it** — every rate is heavy, so it turns like a transport and not like a quad. It lands and taxis instead of exploding, and a wall hit costs hull. `AirplaneState`/`MAX_AIRPLANES`/`VEH_AIRPLANE` join `protocol.h`; it ticks in `local_game.h` beside the drones; `vehicle_call.h` calls it 16 units ahead (an 18-unit wingspan called onto your toes is a collision) on the ground with the lever closed; E boards and leaves it; and it is drawn as a slab-sided transport — fuselage, high wing, tail, twin nacelles with counter-rotating props — so it reads as a plane from underneath, which is where players see it most. New sixth prefab **TRANSPORT** puts the C-110, helicopter, buggy and recon on the same four gadget keys. Deliberately NOT built, named rather than faked: **no cargo bay, no rear ramp, no airborne spawn, no cargo drops** — this is an airframe you can fly, land and roll, not a mobile base, and an interior portal/`Level` inside a moving hull is a much bigger piece of work than the plane. Verified: `make test-airplane-flight` (new) — at full throttle it rolls and then flies, liftoff on tick ~120 at ~6.6 units/tick after a real runway run; an idling aircraft never leaves the ground and builds no airspeed; **flaps lift off measurably earlier** (stall 3.2 vs 4.0); a stalled aircraft sinks with the nose falling even at full back stick; lift is quadratic (250 → 1000 permille for 5.0 → 6.0 units/tick) and zero below the stall; and 10 ticks of full aileron banks it ~40° and turns it only ~11°, i.e. inertia — plus `make test-vehicle-call` (the C-110 calls onto the ground ahead, lever closed, full health), `make test-kit` for the sixth prefab, `phone_test`, `make server` linking and `make check-windows` compiling all 93 lobby sources for mingw.
- **feat(drones): a shard kills the micro, and the recon gets a real gimbal.** Founder: *"wire the Mini Fly Drone to explode instantly upon any impact with a rigid shard"*, then *"and gimbal"*. Both belong in PARENA, not in the renderer. Two new rules in `drone_rules.prn` (regenerated): `drone-shard-kills(kind, mass-grams, rel-speed-milli)` — **any** shard with mass kills the 45 g micro, while the recon's frame needs `mass x relative speed > 40,000,000` (a 15 kg chunk doing ~2.7 units/tick, about what the top of a collapse moves), so dust and pebbles bounce off it — and `drone-gimbal-yaw/pitch-milli` — the recon's head swings **90°** with **60°** of pitch, the micro's 20°/25°. New host side in `drone_flight.h`: `drone_shard_contact()` (point shard vs the airframe's own radius, 1.2 m recon / 0.35 m micro, deciding through the rule), `drone_kill()`, `drone_eject_pilot()` (thrown clear where it died, falling, not riding a wreck), and `drone_gimbal_track()`/`drone_gimbal_view_yaw()` (the offset clamped to the airframe's mechanical limits, because a gimbal is a mechanism and not free look). `DroneState` gains `destroyed`, `gimbal_yaw`, `gimbal_pitch`. Wired in the lobby: `drone_shard_sweep()` runs every frame over the live debris (`g_brick_debris`, the flakes a cracking wall throws — this build has no simulated structural shards, so mass is estimated from each flake's own volume and its per-SECOND velocity converted to the per-TICK flight model), a hit ejects the pilot and fires a real fireball (0.6 units for the micro, 1.6 for the recon); and the mouse now drives the drone's head through `drone_gimbal_clamp()` instead of a free camera. Honest consequence, stated rather than hidden: because the only live debris is cosmetic, **nothing in this build can currently break a recon drone with a shard** — the rule and the sweep are real and tested, the mass-bearing shards are not (BrickBodies/GRB has no live instance in the lobby). Verified: `make test-vehicle-call` — a shard 30 units away does not touch the micro, a 15 kg shard touching it does (and the dead drone cannot be hit twice), a 15 g stationary shard leaves the recon alive while a 15 kg shard at 3 units/tick breaks it, the pilot is ejected in place with `in_vehicle` cleared, the recon's gimbal clamps at its limits and follows exactly inside them, and the micro's head moves far less — plus all earlier traffic/vehicle checks, `make test-kit`, `make server` linking and `make check-windows` compiling all 93 lobby sources for mingw.
- **feat(aircraft): two quadcopters — a recon drone and a mini fly drone — with their own PARENA airframes.** Founder: *"next we want to add more aircraft can we add a basic drone and a mini fly drone?"* A drone sits beside the helicopter as a different airframe rather than a reskin: the heli's collective lever and airframe tilt take real ticks to become motion, while a quadcopter tilts its rotors and goes — **translational thrust hits velocity immediately**, which is exactly why you would fly one into a window the heli overshoots. New `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/drone_rules.prn` (generated to `packages/simulation/drone_rules.c` by `scripts/gen_drone_rules.sh` / `make regen-drone-rules`, installed by `drone_rules_host.h`): two profiles, `DRONE_KIND_RECON` (2.5 kg, response 650 permille, 1.8 units/tick, 60 hull, stops dead on a wall) and `DRONE_KIND_FLY` (45 g micro, response 1850, 3.4 units/tick, 12 hull, **bounces** off the world), plus weather drift where the recon feels a quarter of a storm and the micro feels twice it. New `packages/simulation/drone_flight.h` is the immediate-thrust loop and the spawn/enter/exit helpers; `DroneState`/`MAX_DRONES`/`VEH_DRONE` join `protocol.h`, drones tick in `local_game.h` beside the helicopters, and `vehicle_call.h` calls either kind onto the gadget keys (`KIT_ITEM_DRONE` / `KIT_ITEM_MINI_DRONE`, and a fifth prefab **AERIAL** puts DRONE / MINI DRONE / HELICOPTER / BUGGY on the same four keys RUNNER uses). Both are drawn as a body plus four spinning rotors at their own scale with a red (recon) or green (micro) nav light, so the two read apart at range. Deliberately NOT done and named: no gimbal/broadcast-camera link to a stream (the founder's F3 idea needs a camera route this engine does not have), no per-drone damage from collisions or weapons, and no snapshot replication of drones to remote clients. The storm the airframes feel is `g_sky_weather.storm` read once per frame into `drone_set_storm` -- local, like the environment matrix, not a wire field, so a remote client would have to derive it the same way. Verified: `make test-vehicle-call` — both kinds call, arrive occupied and keep their kind; **both move on tick one** (the property that distinguishes them from the heli); the micro outruns the recon over 180 ticks; the same 900‰ storm deflects the micro >1.5x the recon; and against a real `map_geo` wall the micro bounces while the recon stops, with neither passing through — plus the existing buggy / rocket-car / helicopter checks still pass, `make test-kit` for the fifth prefab, `phone_test`, `make server` linking, and `make check-windows` compiling all 93 lobby sources for mingw.
- **feat(kit): the three VEHICLE gadgets — buggy, rocket car, helicopter — are call items on the gadget keys.** Founder: *"add buggy, rocket car, and hellicopter to the gadgets we already have the buggy and the hellicopter the rocket car implementation is in bedrock racers im not sure if that code is in PELLEGRINO or what but it was at wotan i dunno the graphics for those cars are perfect too for the models"*. The rocket car was not in PELLEGRINO at all — it is **PARENA**, `stdlib/bedrockracers/web/br_phys.prn` ("the rocket car, the ball, and the world they share", the Tesla-Roadster-style car with four lift nozzles and fans). So `KIT_ITEM_BUGGY` / `KIT_ITEM_ROCKET_CAR` / `KIT_ITEM_HELI` were added to `packages/common/kit.h` plus a fourth prefab **RUNNER** (`` ` `` BUGGY, `-` ROCKET CAR, `=` HELICOPTER, `C` canister) so all three are reachable without building a kit. New `packages/simulation/vehicle_call.h` is the verb: spawn the vehicle 7 units in front of the player and seat them in it through the **existing** `buggy_try_enter` / helicopter occupant path, so the camera, chase steering, exit offsets and draw code are the same code and not a second vehicle implementation. Refusals are real: not a call item, already driving, on the vehicle cooldown, or no free slot in this scene's garage — refusing beats silently recycling someone else's car. New `PARENA/stdlib/shankpit/rocket_car_rules.prn` (generated to `packages/simulation/rocket_car_rules.c` by the new `scripts/gen_rocket_car_rules.sh` / `make regen-rocket-car-rules`, installed by `rocket_car_rules_host.h`) ports the part of BR's car that fits this chassis: **one air tank pays for everything** (lift 26/s, boost 22/s, refills 34/s, 48/s on a surface), the rear-nozzle boost scales with what is left in it ("empty, the car still turns at a quarter rate — never helpless"), and a fan suspension holds the belly 2.6 units off the surface instead of riding wheels. `BuggyState` gains `rocket`/`tank` appended after the `snap_*` client-local scratch fields, and `simulate_buggy_state` routes a rocket buggy to the new `simulate_rocket_car_state`. Deliberately NOT ported and named as such: BR's strafe/attitude axes (this chassis has no separate axes — the camera is the steering, like the buggy) and wall/ceiling driving. The look keeps the buggy body (which is already this engine's car) and adds what makes a rocket car readable at distance: four additive lift-jet plumes under it and a rear booster, both brighter with the air left. Verified: `make test-vehicle-call` (new — a called buggy spawns ahead on -Z and seats the player, a second call honours the cooldown, the rocket car arrives hovering with a full tank, the helicopter arrives clear of the ground and occupied, non-vehicle items and full garages are refused, and the profile is measurably different: **the rocket car outran the buggy over 240 ticks, spent air under throttle, refilled at idle, and sat down on its belly with an empty tank without sinking through the floor**), plus `make test-kit` and `phone_test` updated for the fourth prefab. NOT done: no kit-call packet, so the three keys are local/sandbox only and do nothing in `STATE_GAME_NET` (the server owns spawning); no per-call HUD readout of the air tank; and the BR car's own mesh data was not lifted — the models here are this engine's box-built car plus the nozzle plumes.
- **feat(kit): four gadget keys, three kit apps, and the pages are HTML+CSS painted by PELLEGRINO.** Founder: *"we need to add kit affordances for these items have it be so that you can ` and - and = so 3 additional gadgets in additon to 1-9 or -0 ... and we can also set f and c i suppose ... oh wait f is already taken for vehicle entry thats fine we can dont need f same spec"*, then *"give us 2 apps one to map the current kit to keys and another app to select the kit from the kits menenu (we can select a kit that is already pre built) and then another app for building kits keep it simple while including all the affordances use basic html and CSS and PELLEGRINO (clone it down) tech for rendering the ui"*. New `packages/common/kit.h`: **14 bind slots** -- the ten number keys plus `` ` ``, `-`, `=` and `C` (F deliberately excluded, it enters vehicles), and the ten digits are never rebound by a prefab because that is where a player's weapons already live. Items are not a new inventory: each one is an input this engine already acts on (`PHEROMONE` -> `in_pheromone`, `ABILITY`, `SECONDARY`, `RELOAD`), so a kit is a key->real-action map rather than a parallel universe of fake gadgets. Three prefabs (SCOUT / BREACHER / HUNTER, genuinely different shapes), cycling that wraps through every item and back to empty, and a **draft** so the builder never trashes the kit you are carrying. Three phone apps: **KITS** (pick the prefab that goes live), **KIT MAP** (rebind the live kit's keys, ENTER clears), **KIT BUILD** (edit the draft, ENTER saves it over the live kit, with `*` marking any row that differs). Rendering is `kit_page_*` HTML with a small `<style>` block, painted by **PELLEGRINO's own layout engine** through `os_menu_set_page`/`os_menu_draw` (`packages/pellegrino_embed/kit_page_gl.h`) -- deliberately not the in-phone browser, which is a real navigator that can only show URLs; a text fallback draws every row when that surface is unavailable so no app is ever a blank rectangle. PELLEGRINO is cloned at `../PELLEGRINO` per the founder's instruction. Gameplay wiring: `` ` ``, `-`, `=` and `C` arm a ~150 ms pulse of their bound item, consumed by the same input assembly the keyboard already feeds (all four on the net path, the canister on the local path), and the orb keeps ` on any kit where that slot is empty. Verified: `make test-kit` (slot/key mapping incl. the deliberate F hole, prefab shape, full wrap-around cycling, out-of-range slots ignored rather than clamped, select-then-draft isolation, three pages containing real markup, and a too-small buffer refused instead of truncated) and `phone_test` (KITS selects BREACHER, KIT MAP cycles `` ` `` PHEROMONE->ABILITY and clears it, KIT BUILD leaves the live kit alone until SAVE). NOT done: no server-side authority for a kit yet (the effects are the hook point), the kit does not persist across launches, and the two non-canister gadget pulses are net-path only.
- **feat(phone): the tech tree is spendable, and its first line lengthens pheromone immunity.** Founder: *"stat adding tech tree we can have improved pherimone balls it should increase your seconds of immunity by 2 seconds per level upt to 5 points build the ui into the phone app ui that you pull up with P."* `BP_APP_TECHTREE` already existed but was a read-only, host-fed list of names with `[OK]` flags -- nothing could be bought and nothing it displayed did anything. It now has real levels, a real point cost, and one real effect: `PHEROMONE IMMUNITY +2s`, five levels, feeding `witness_ai_set_pheromone_grace_bonus_ms`. The number lives once in `phone.h` (`phone_tech_bonus_ms`), so the UI's readout and the zombie AI's actual window cannot drift apart. The phone stays pure (spends the point, returns `BP_FX_TECH_UPGRADE` with the node index); the lobby owns what a level is worth and pushes it the instant it is bought, so the very next canister carries the longer window. Points are earned from the XP this build already awards -- 1 per 500, re-derived every sync, so there is no second save file to drift and no way to be double-charged by a level load. The app draws `LV x/5` with a five-pip bar, the point balance, the resulting window in seconds with the base called out, and a real "no points" line instead of silently ignoring ENTER. Verified: `phone_test` (a SELECT spends one point and emits the upgrade effect; nine further presses stop exactly at level 5 with 0 points; a capped-and-broke SELECT is a no-op; a negative balance clamps) and `make test-pheromone-grenade` (with the maxed line installed a canister's window measures >17 s against the 9 s base). NOT done: the tree has exactly one node (the other eleven slots are unused), points are awarded at a fixed XP ratio rather than per level-up curve, and the bonus is pushed in-process -- there is no packet, so a remote server-authoritative match would not see it yet.
- **feat(zombies): the lure feeds, the horde navigates, and your pheromone buys you a window that closes.** Founder: *"zombies shouldnt just walk into walls ... sometimes they should but that is when they are trying to break walls down they should have some ability to navigate the terrain i think citizens already do that also the zombies dont seem to respond to ferimone balls it should make them hungry and somewhat aggressive also it should guide them to the spot also they should attack the player ad the citizens when they are hungry or aggressive there should be some threshold where they dont attack you if your pherimone caused their hunger and agression but that should be like a fading memory your zombies wont attack you at first but they forget pretty quick and they will target you for the next snack after your window runs out so you need to be intentional with your phermone balls to move them around the map as there are more of them."* Three separate defects, one cause: the systems were all built and none of them were talking to each other. (1) **Walking into walls.** `witness_ai_tick`'s wall pass called `wai_avoid_walls(..., g_wall_hit_hook != NULL)`, and the real game always installs that hook, so every zombie was `relentless` -- route planning skipped -- and the whole horde ground the straight line into the nearest face and clawed it from 100 m out. It is now a real decision: breach only when the target is within `WITNESS_AI_ZOMBIE_BREACH_TARGET_RANGE` (70 m, i.e. the fight is right there) and the zombie is HUNTING/FRENZIED, otherwise it plans a route exactly as a citizen already did; the existing `g_wai_breach` stop-plant-and-tear path is untouched, which is what keeps wall-breaking working. (2) **Pheromone balls did nothing.** A marker only redirected the steering of a zombie with no hero lock -- it fed nothing, so a lure moved a horde around and left it exactly as harmless as it found it, and the marker's own thrower was never recorded at all (the parallel `g_pheromone_owner1[]` array now does, rather than adding a field to ported BIG_O code). A zombie now gains `WITNESS_AI_PHEROMONE_HUNGER_PER_SEC`/`_AGGRESSION_PER_SEC` (rate-clamped against the real tick delta) while it is steering to a canister, so the lure genuinely makes it dangerous, and it remembers whose canister recruited it. (3) **They never attacked each other or you.** Targeting was gated on `hunger >= STARVING` (0.70) alone, so a fed but furious zombie stood next to a citizen and did nothing -- `zombie_values.h`'s whole aggression meter was computed every tick and never read. New PARENA rule `zombies-pheromone-should-hunt(hunger_milli, aggression_milli)` makes it hungry OR aggressive (0.40 / 0.45, deliberately unequal: hunger is "I need a meal", aggression is "something just happened"), and the fading memory is its sibling `zombies-pheromone-spares-thrower(grace_ms, thrower_alive)` -- `WITNESS_AI_PHEROMONE_GRACE_MS` (9 s) of immunity for the THROWER ONLY, checked before the hero is even sensed, with the owner cleared the moment it lapses so a later thrower is judged fresh. Citizens were never covered, so a lured horde eats the neighbourhood and remembers you late.
