## 2026-09-18
- feat(sim): ragdoll spike now writes a real get-up ORACLE .gband clip, correcting a mis-scope (S497) -- founder real-time: "fuck it play the death animation backwards - let the robot learn how to just pop back up itll still be horiffic but in a different way," then, correcting the first cut of this before any code shipped: "my bro the rag doll doesnt need to stand up if its not rigid body it wont fall over - reversing the death animation was supposed to be the oracle that the RL learns against to be able to stand back up after falling over." tools/ragdoll_spike/main.c now records every tick's joint positions during its forward fall sim and, after the sim completes, walks the buffer backward and writes a real `.gband` binary + manifest (tools/ragdoll_spike/output/getup_oracle.gband(.json), gitignored -- generated, not source) -- an imitation-reward reference clip for HQ-SPEC-SIM-100 §4's Reward Compiler, not a directly-playable get-up animation (this spike has no rigid-body/orientation state, so nothing in it actually "fell over" in the sense that would make literal reverse playback a usable animation on its own). Verified end to end against GOLDENBAND's own real reader, not just the writer's own claim: loaded via gb_init, gb_verify PASSes (content hash matches), duration_ticks=192, num_channels=195 (65 joints x tx/ty/tz), and the arc runs from the fall's settled/lying pose (joint0 y=0.000) at tick 0 up to the original standing rest pose (joint0 y=7.987) at the final tick -- confirms the reversed clip actually shapes a lying-to-standing arc. docs2/RAGDOLL_ORIENTATION_NORTHSTAR.md updated with the corrected framing as a real third recovery path (alongside the existing authored-blend and trained-policy paths), and directly answers the founder's separate "most importantly we need a roll over animation" point: not needed for this specific mechanism, since the reversed clip retraces whatever orientation the character actually fell into, not a fixed assumed starting pose -- a roll-over step is still a real, separate need for any OTHER recovery path that assumes a fixed pose. Compiles clean under -Wall.
- feat(sim): ragdoll spike iteration 3 (ground friction) + real orientation-physics NORTHSTAR (S495/S496) -- founder real-time chain: "we may as well see how far we can push the engine while we are building it it impacts the kinds of stories we can tell" -> "and then what does it look like to take it all the way to robotics? do we have to build cad in?" -> "if the character falls over theres no way its going to look human like when they get up its going to be horiffic and synthetic." Tested one more real, cheap hypothesis before committing to full orientation physics: added GROUND_FRICTION (kills horizontal implied-velocity on ground contact, previously only vertical was killed). Result: real but partial -- Y-flatness completely unchanged, but lateral spread tightened substantially (fingertip spread ~0.6-0.7 -> ~0.3-0.5 units), turning a splayed starfish into a more compact pile. Real, corrected framing reached in the same conversation: lying flat after falling isn't actually the bug (real ragdolls do that) -- the real problem is the symmetric, poker-straight shape, since nothing resists a joint straightening toward full extension. New docs2/RAGDOLL_ORIENTATION_NORTHSTAR.md: real v0 design (swing-axis constraints derived from rest-pose geometry, no new .gskel metadata needed) for the actual next increment, phased (major joints first), with twist limits/full per-bone orientation named honestly as the bigger v1 lift. Directly answers two real, adjacent founder questions in the same doc: confirms "horrific and synthetic" recovery is a well-documented real RL failure mode, HQ-SPEC-SIM-100's own Reward Compiler (imitation reward against a real reference clip) is the actual fix, names two real recovery paths (authored blend now vs. trained policy later); and grounds the robotics/CAD question directly in SIM-100 §3's own real actuator-metadata (torque/velocity/backlash) requirement, sourced from real hardware specs, not full CAD in the simulator.

## 2026-09-17
- feat(sim): ragdoll spike iteration 2 -- real per-bone mass + bend constraints, a sharply-defined Phase 2 finding (S494) -- founder real-time: "just work on ragdoll physics" / "we are going to need to define masses for objects im sure for rigid body physics." Added real per-bone mass to tools/ragdoll_spike/main.c (proportional to each joint's own parent-bone length -- a thigh genuinely outweighs a finger bone now, replacing the original uniform inv_mass=1) and a real bend constraint (BendConstraint, a one-sided minimum-distance constraint between a joint and its own grandparent -- the standard PBD bend-resistance technique). Live-verified the bend constraint works exactly as designed: 0/63 constraints violated at the final settled state, real hyperextension/fold-back genuinely prevented. But the whole body still collapses completely flat (every joint at y=0.000) by t=0.75s, unchanged from iteration 1 -- a real, conclusive, NOT-a-tuning-problem finding: a fully-extended limb lying flat on the ground satisfies every distance AND bend constraint simultaneously, since neither has any concept of orientation relative to gravity, only relative distances between points. No number or combination of point-distance constraints can ever fix this. Real, now sharply-defined Phase 2: a believable ragdoll needs actual per-bone orientation state and real angular/swing-twist joint limits (true rigid-body dynamics with quaternion orientations), a categorically different, larger technique than point-mass PBD -- not an incremental extension of what's built.
- feat(render): real enclosed/indoor lighting + real screen-space bloom (S493) -- founder real-time, from a real in-game screenshot: "theres not much difference between having lights on and not having lights - its still basically illuminated in this totally enclosed level ... there should be more difference between light and dark and we should see like the lighting kind of move a bit with the glow effect of the hps light." Two real, found root causes, both fixed:
  1. **Enclosed/indoor lighting**: `retro_lighting_eval_surface_rgb` (packages/render/retro_lighting.c) adds a real, physically-motivated sun/moon "scattered sky" fill term to every wall face unconditionally -- correct for outdoor scenes, but applied even to windowless interior rooms with zero real sky exposure, swamping the real per-fixture (HPS/IPS) point-light contrast a level author actually placed. `RETRO_LIGHTING_INTERIOR_FLAT` already existed as a defined preset (zero sun/moon contribution) but was never actually selected anywhere in the codebase -- a real, found-live "defined but orphaned" gap. New `CustomLevelData.enclosed` (level_boxes.h, real JSON key, 0/absent default = unchanged outdoor behavior) drives a real switch in `level_boxes_apply_to_physics` (apps/lobby's own shared level-load path, covers local single-player, the --level CLI flag, AND the network multiplayer client's own QUEUE-level fetch, all three call sites in one place) -- `g_world_lighting_preset` now actually changes per level for the first time ever (previously a single global, hardcoded to RETRO_LIGHTING_DAY_STATIC, exactly ONE assignment in the entire codebase). Real safety net added: leaving an enclosed level for a built-in outdoor scene (VOXWORLD etc.) resets the preset back, so the world doesn't stay stuck dim. IDUNA's own new SetEnclosed method + NOCK checkbox let a designer toggle this per level -- see that repo's CHANGELOG. 6/6 new tests green (apps/tests/test_level_enclosed_lighting.c).
  2. **Real screen-space bloom** (new packages/render/bloom.h/.c) -- the "glow" that already existed (draw_light_glow_billboard) was a camera-facing sprite at each fixture's own position, not true bloom: didn't react to what's actually bright on screen, didn't bleed past its own fixed size. Real technique now: render the frame to an off-screen FBO, extract genuinely bright pixels via a real luminance threshold (a real GLSL shader, naturally picks up the already-bright HPS/IPS emissive surfaces AND their existing per-frame flicker with zero extra wiring), blur via a real 2-pass separable Gaussian (quarter-res, a real, deliberate glow-radius/perf tradeoff) using its own second GLSL shader, composite back onto the real frame with additive blending. New FBO/renderbuffer GL extension loading (own p_gl* set, same hand-loaded SDL_GL_GetProcAddress convention gl_shader.c already established, for the same real Windows-cross-compile portability reason). Handles window resize (SDL_WINDOW_RESIZABLE) by reallocating targets when size changes. Real, found-and-fixed bug in an early draft: gl_shader.h has no glUniform2fv-equivalent helper, and the first cut called mismatched-arity setters (gl_uniform1f/gl_uniform3fv) against the blur shader's own vec2 uniform -- a real, silent GL_INVALID_OPERATION no-op that would have left the blur direction at a permanent (0,0), i.e. sampling the same texel 5 times and never actually blurring anything. Fixed by using two real scalar uniforms instead. Deliberately whole-frame (HUD/weapon-viewmodel/overlay elements bloom too, a real accepted tradeoff given this game's own existing neon/retro-CRT aesthetic, not an oversight) -- wraps draw_scene's entire existing call site with zero changes to draw_scene's own body. Real, honest, degrades safely: bloom_init's own failure (old driver/software renderer) makes every other bloom_* call a genuine no-op, confirmed live under Xvfb's own llvmpipe software GL (which lacks the needed shader entry points in this sandbox, a real, pre-existing, already-documented limitation elsewhere in this same codebase) -- the binary ran the full test duration with zero crash, exactly the intended honest degradation. Actual bloom visual quality needs verification on real GPU/driver hardware, a real, named limitation of this sandbox (no display), same as this session's own earlier day/night lighting work.
  make server/make lobby both clean.
- fix(ai): AI_ROLE_WANDERING_BOT no longer silently rejected at level load (S492) -- founder real-time: "how do i stat to have different characters like walking around the city and stuff - even just standing there and having their head turn and look at you and they can say something or whatever." Real, found-live gap: this role already exists natively and already does almost exactly this (patrols, turns to face + waves + dances when the player gets close, never combat) -- but apps/server/src/main.c's and apps/lobby/src/main.c's own character-spawn range checks both stopped one enum value short at AI_ROLE_BLIND_STALKER, so a level author who managed to author role=10 anyway would have had that character silently skipped ("CUSTOM_LEVEL_CHARACTER_SKIPPED reason=invalid_role"), not spawned as the real, working ambient bot it actually is. Both range checks widened to AI_ROLE_WANDERING_BOT. Same real gap fixed on IDUNA's own Go mirror (internal/shankpit.validateCharacters) and, separately, in NOCK's own frontend dropdown (see that repo's CHANGELOG) -- three independent copies of the same off-by-one, all found and fixed together. make server/make lobby both clean.
- feat(sim): level exits can target a specific destination spawner, for seamless GTA-style building interiors (S491) -- founder real-time: "so i figured out we can do buildings like GTA we have an exit point of the level that is pointed to the level that represents the interior of that building - exiting that level you can spawn back into the world but how can i specify which spawner the exit leads to for the seamless experience of exiting the building." Real, found-live gap fixed along the way: LevelSpawner never actually parsed its own author-assigned "id" from JSON at all (silently dropped, since nothing needed it before this) -- fixed (LevelSpawner.id, JSON parsing, threaded through phys_set_custom_level_spawners' now-5-arg signature and both its real call sites in apps/server and apps/lobby). New custom_level_pick_spawner_by_id: a real, minimal, additive sibling to the existing team/FFA custom_level_pick_spawner, honestly returning 0 (never crashing) for a stale/missing target id or the real target_spawner_id<=0 "no target" sentinel. story_check_level_exits (server) and lobby_check_story_level_exits (lobby) both capture which exit actually fired and, after phys_respawn's own normal full reset, surgically override just the resulting position when a target spawner is named and still exists. 10/10 new tests green (apps/tests/test_level_exit_spawner_target.c), make server/make lobby both clean.
- feat(sim): REFLUX cross-mod pub/sub, ported natively into SHANKPIT, plus real buttons and button-subscribing doors (S485) -- founder real-time: "we need buttons - how am i gonna put a button on a wall next to a door to open a door?" -> "we need a reusable button that can be put in different places ... i want to follow the pub sub model ... we need to use the same pub sub mod as ECOWAR" -> "bring in the ecowar pub sub mod" -> "but keep all of the code in shankpit". New packages/reflux/reflux_runtime.h/.c: a real, native port of ECOWAR's own proven REFLUX primitive (EMILY/BACKLOG.md SECTION 380/381) -- same API/ABI (reflux_host_dispatch/log_size/action_*_at), own log, zero cross-repo dependency. New packages/simulation/story_buttons.h: a real, reusable, edge-triggered interact button (reuses the ALREADY-WIRED BTN_USE input path -- an F-key binding that already flowed end to end to p->in_use/p->use_was_down for CTFB flag pickup, just never had a second consumer before) -- pressing one dispatches REFLUX_ACTION_BUTTON_PRESSED(button_id, player_id, 0) and knows nothing else. New LevelDoor.subscribe_button_id (level_boxes.h, -1 default = every existing door unchanged): a door with this set ignores distance-to-player entirely and instead polls the shared REFLUX log (its own persistent cursor, host-tracked since PARENA/VS0 has no closures for real push callbacks) for matching BUTTON_PRESSED actions, toggling open/closed on each one -- real agency ("a door that is open and closable gives you more agency"), not proximity hysteresis. Wired into BOTH the dedicated server (story_doors_tick) and local single-player (lobby_doors_tick, dlfcn-free so it works there too, unlike scripted PARENA doors) -- and the button-press dispatcher itself runs unconditionally, every game mode, per S481c's own resolved "levels are never story-mode-only" policy. Real, deliberate inversion of control, founder's own words: "the bridge listens for a button the button has no idea the bridge exists" -- proven end to end with a genuinely unrelated control door in the same test that a button press never touches. 13/13 new tests green (apps/tests/test_reflux_buttons.c), make server/make lobby both clean. Honest, explicitly named next phases, not built this pass: proximity sensors (REFLUX_ACTION_PROXIMITY_ENTER/EXIT, action types reserved but never dispatched anywhere yet) and gaze/look-at triggers (REFLUX_ACTION_LOOK_AT, same), plus a real PARENA-scripted REFLUX reactor (today's door-subscription toggle is a built-in C default, matching door_tick_builtin_proximity's own precedent -- a scriptable PARENA reactor contract is real future work, not this pass's own scope)
- feat(physics): ragdoll/rigid-body PBD spike (S484), throwaway validation, not shippable -- founder real-time: "can we add ragdoll and rigid body physics including stuff with mass? can we make the models based on the manequin we have for multiplayer?" New tools/ragdoll_spike/main.c: FKs the real mannequin_npc.gskel's rest pose (the exact same 65-joint skeleton already used for real multiplayer players via SKIN_MANNEQUIN, no new model needed), then runs a minimal Verlet/PBD point-mass + distance-constraint sim (gravity, ground plane, SHANKPIT's own real 64Hz tick rate) to test whether the approach is even stable on this engine's real skeleton data before any design doc gets written. Found and fixed two real bugs in the first cut: a Verlet ground-clamp velocity spike (clamped pos.y without clamping prev_pos.y, producing a teleport-velocity blowup) and a sign error in the distance-constraint correction for body B (pushed the constraint apart instead of together -- verified by hand-deriving the PBD update against C(pa,pb)=|pa-pb|-restLen). Result, real and conclusive: once both bugs were fixed, the sim is numerically stable (no NaN/divergence, converges by t=0.5s) at the real 65-joint/64-constraint scale and real tick rate -- PBD is a viable foundation. But it converges to every joint flat at y=0: distance-only constraints don't stop a knee/elbow folding backward, since bending never violates a bone-length constraint. Real, named next requirement: angular/joint-limit constraints, not per-bone collision shapes or mass tuning, are the actual next architecture question for a believable ragdoll. See tools/ragdoll_spike/main.c's own header for the full before/after (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- fix(net): the REAL cause of "no live respawn snapshot within timeout" -- NetPlayer (protocol.h) grew from 84 to 88 bytes when anim_override (S470) was added, and both real wire-format mirrors (rl_env_packet.py's Python ctypes class, apps2/emily-bot/snapshot.go's netPlayerSize) were still at 84 -- the exact same class of bug this codebase has already hit and fixed twice before (S459-44, S459-69), each time from a hand-maintained mirror not updated when the real struct grew. With a stale, 4-byte-too-small stride, decode_snapshot correctly parses the FIRST entity in a multi-entity snapshot but reads every entity after it from the wrong offset -- so a real training client, which almost always connects as the SECOND participant (behind a heuristic bot or self-play opponent, exactly matching real production config), could never find itself among a snapshot's own entities. Every earlier reproduction attempt used a lone-client smoke test, which could never have caught this (only one entity = nothing for the stale stride to misalign). Found by finally reproducing the EXACT real config (real --save-freq, real registry push, --heuristic-opponents 1) instead of an isolated test, then dumping raw packet bytes instead of guessing further. Fixed all three consumers + their own stale self-checking assertions. go test ./apps2/emily-bot/... (13 tests) and python -m unittest discover (39 tests) both green. Live-verified conclusively: the identical repro (real server + real heuristic bot + a real second client) fails before this fix and succeeds immediately after, zero other changes (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- fix(rl): per-generation training chunk (--save-freq) was ~5x too small and unconfigurable from Colab -- founder real-time, confirmed: "the traing was bad because the trainig wasnt going long enough." Defaulted to 4096 (only 2 real PPO rollout-collection+update cycles at SB3's own default n_steps=2048), nearly 5x smaller than BRAWLPIT/scripts/rl_train_packet.py's own analogous --save-freq=20_000 -- a real drift from the sibling implementation this file was explicitly built to mirror, not a deliberate choice, and a real, plausible explanation for the original S475 investigation's own "60 generations, flat/declining Elo" finding. colab_train.py also never exposed --save-freq as an overridable setting at all (every other real training knob already had a SHANKPIT_<NAME> env var passthrough, this one didn't). Fixed both: default raised to 20_000 (matching BRAWLPIT), new SHANKPIT_SAVE_FREQ env var passthrough in colab_train.py. python -m unittest discover (39 tests) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- fix(server): the per-tick [STATUS] log no longer floods under --fast-forward (S483 follow-up) -- founder pushed back hard on the earlier "couldn't reproduce it" answer, correctly ("are you sure? ... it is broke"), after a completely fresh Colab session with a freshly-pulled script still failed. Real root cause, found by reproducing with the ACTUAL game mode training uses (MODE_QUEUE, not MODE_DEATHMATCH -- earlier manual repro tests used the wrong mode) and measuring instead of guessing: the [STATUS] print throttled by tick count (tick % 600 == 0), a reasonable ~10s cadence at a normal server's ~60 ticks/sec, but --fast-forward decouples tick count from wall time (~500K ticks/sec measured live), so the same condition fires hundreds of times/sec even idle. Measured ~37KB/sec in a verbose build. This print used to go to DEVNULL (discarded instantly) until the same-day _spawn_server fix started capturing stdout to a real file for crash-diagnosis -- combined with this pre-existing fast-forward bug, writing that firehose continuously (worse on a network-backed --output-dir like a Colab Drive mount) for a multi-hour run is a real, serious I/O cost that plausibly stalls the server's own tick loop past a client's respawn window. Fixed at the source: real wall-clock throttling (net_should_log_every, same mechanism net_server_emit_summary already uses) instead of tick-count throttling. Live-verified both correctness (a real MODE_QUEUE client against the real TRAINING_GROUND level under --fast-forward still connects and stays alive exactly as before) and volume (~350x reduction, confirmed via real timed log measurements). make server/make lobby clean (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- fix(rl): _spawn_server (rl_train_packet.py) no longer silences the real training server's stdout/stderr to DEVNULL -- founder real-time bug report: a Colab run's very first env.reset() raised "TimeoutError: no live respawn snapshot within timeout" with zero other diagnostic output. Same class of bug S459-61 already fixed for the two eval bots, left unfixed for the server itself. Now captures to a real log file (output_dir/server_<port>.log) and checks for an early process exit right after the startup grace period, raising an immediate, loud RuntimeError with the log tail instead of a silent, misleading 30s timeout downstream. Live-verified both the happy path (real server, real non-empty log) and the failure path (SERVER_BIN pointed at /bin/false raises the new RuntimeError). Honest, not fully resolved: could not reproduce the founder's own actual timeout locally against the real live TRAINING_GROUND level + matching flags -- a real client connected and got a valid alive snapshot in ~1s, no crash. This fix makes the NEXT occurrence diagnosable, doesn't explain this one (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)

New story_ai_despawn_all_characters deactivates ONLY the specific slots g_story_ai itself spawned into (via each AIController's own player_id), leaving every other slot untouched -- used outside MODE_STORY instead of the full reset, which stays unchanged for MODE_STORY itself. - feat(physics): real per-material ground friction + global friction tuning bump (S478a/S478b) -- founder real-time: "how hard would it be to increase the friction some at the edges of blocks by default so its a little easier to stick to platforms? right now the physics are so slippery it can be unreasonably hard to parkour" -> "make the material friction stuff working per cube". Global FRICTION/STOP_SPEED doubled (0.15->0.30, 0.1->0.15) in packages/common/physics.h -- there's no literal "edge" concept in this physics, ground friction was always one flat constant. On top of that, friction is now a real per-material property (LevelBoxMaterial.friction, a new g_custom_level_material_friction[] table, a new PlayerState.ground_friction resolved per-tick in resolve_collision's landing branch from whichever custom-level box the player is actually standing on, consumed by apply_friction instead of the flat constant) -- matches how shader_name/specular/shininess already resolve per-material via material_idx. ## 2026-09-15
- S459-76: rl_train_packet.py now pushes updated Elo back onto already-registered checkpoints -- founder real-time: "the elos of the generation 0 bots arent going up and down... can we make sure the elos are set up to go up and down not just whatever the first elo into the registry is?" Real gap: record_match_result already correctly moves BOTH the new generation's and its prior generation's local Elo (a real, symmetric update), but the prior generation was pushed to the registry before that evaluation ran, and nothing ever pushed the update back -- a checkpoint's remote Elo was frozen at its push-time value forever. New rl_registry.py update_checkpoint_elo() calls IDUNA's new PATCH endpoint (see IDUNA CHANGELOG), wired in right after record_match_result using the already-tracked (previously unused) prev_remote_ids. Live-verified end to end: ran a real local training run with registry push, confirmed generation 0's rows moved from elo=1500 to real, distinct values (1484/1500/1516) after generation 1's own evaluation matches. Full python -m unittest suite (36 tests) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-75: new docs/ANTICHEAT_NORTHSTAR.md -- founder real-time: "northstar this for anticheat" (after a real research conversation on hardware-level USB HID input cheats, ATmega32U4/Arduino-based aimbots that anti-cheat software can't distinguish from a real mouse). Scopes a real, phased plan grounded directly in SHANKPIT's own existing architecture (server-authoritative combat, real UserCmd yaw/pitch telemetry already flowing through process_user_cmd at 60Hz, the real bot-pool infrastructure and S459-62's own active-opponent precedent) -- not a generic essay. Real design: Phase 0 passive telemetry (snap detection, consistency-under-fire scoring, no action taken yet), Phase 1 silent reroute of suspected players into a bot-only match, Phase 2 an escalating bot difficulty ladder culminating in sub-human-reaction-time "impossible" bots (a real, physically-grounded, near-unfakeable signal since no human can out-track a 0ms-reaction opponent), Phase 3 human-reviewed or bounded-cost outcomes, never an automatic permaban from this system alone. Named, not solved: screen-reading/CV-only cheats and memory-reading wallhacks are a different threat class this doc doesn't cover. NORTHSTAR only, no code written (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-74: fixed three real spray bugs -- founder real-time: "its really unreliable to do a spray you have to hit the button over and over... it jitters when you first use a spray... it sprays backwards and when you switch levels the sprays dont disappear they are floating in space where the walls used to be". (1) trace_map_boxes (backs both spray placement and the flashlight beam) was never a real raycast -- it only tested whether the segment's fixed FAR ENDPOINT happened to land inside a wall's volume, never a real segment-vs-box intersection, explaining exactly why facing a wall dead-on only worked at one specific standoff distance while running alongside it landed by chance. Replaced with a real closest-hit slab-method segment-vs-AABB test (same algorithm as scripts/rl_env_packet.py's own raycast, S459-73's own verified C-equivalent math). Live-verified with a direct C probe: 8 standoff distances from 1-35 units all now hit reliably at the exact correct wall surface with the correct normal; correct misses when pointed away or genuinely clear of the wall. (2) Sprays never got cleared on scene/level change, and every custom level shares the same SCENE_CUSTOM_LEVEL scene_id, so old sprays from a level you'd left kept rendering against geometry that no longer existed -- fixed by clearing the decal buffer in reset_client_render_state_for_net, the same real connect/reconnect boundary that already re-fetches level geometry. (3) spray_registry_fetch_list is a genuinely blocking network call (curl via popen) that used to fire inline on a player's first-ever T press, stalling the main loop mid-input and producing the reported jitter/backward-spray -- fixed by resolving the default spray proactively at scene entry (client_prefetch_default_spray, mirroring client_load_queue_level's own established prefetch pattern) instead of reactively on first use. apps/server and apps/lobby build clean, no new warnings. Full python -m unittest suite (36 tests) and go test (all packages) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-73: real, measured additional speedup on raycast() -- founder real-time: "ok yea optimize more if you can" (after S459-72's numpy-scalar fix, cProfile showed raycast/_slab as the actual remaining bottleneck, 34% of build_observation). Two changes: (1) Wall now precomputes its own AABB bounds (lo_x/hi_x/etc) once at construction instead of every raycast() call recomputing `w.x - w.sx/2` etc from scratch on every one of the 5 raycasts build_observation makes per tick against the same unchanging wall list; (2) the per-axis slab test is now inlined directly into raycast()'s own loop instead of a separate _slab() function call per axis (real Python function-call + tuple pack/unpack overhead on a hot path -- 1.25M such calls per 50k build_observation calls in cProfile). _slab itself kept as a real, standalone, still-tested reference implementation, not deleted. Verified EXACT numerical equivalence, not just "close enough": 200k random-input trials comparing old vs new raycast() output, zero mismatches -- also locked in as a new permanent regression test. Real, measured result: build_observation dropped from 62.46us/call (post-S459-72) to 50.75us/call -- combined with S459-72, a real 34.5% speedup from the original 77.54us/call baseline. cProfile confirms raycast's own share dropped from 34% to 15% of build_observation's total time. Full python -m unittest suite (36 tests, +1 new) and go test (all packages) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-72: real, measured 24% speedup on build_observation() -- founder real-time: "is python itself the bottleneck? do we need to write it in c?" Profiled a real env step first (2000 real steps against an isolated dev server): build_observation was 52-56% of total step time, NOT network I/O (34.8%) or GPU-relevant compute. Profiled inside build_observation itself: it called numpy's vectorized ufuncs (np.sin/cos/sqrt/arctan2/degrees/radians/tanh) on single scalar floats throughout -- a real, measured 5.7x-slower-than-necessary anti-pattern (direct microbenchmark: numpy scalar calls 2.64us vs math module 0.46us). Swapped all 14 real call sites to the stdlib math module. Live-verified with a clean before/after isolated benchmark (100k calls, 3 real peers + 3 real walls, same inputs both runs): 77.54us/call -> 62.46us/call, a real, measured 24% speedup, zero behavior change. cProfile afterward showed the REAL remaining bottleneck honestly: raycast/_slab (wall-collision math, pure Python loop) is now 34% of build_observation's own time -- genuine per-wall geometric compute that scales with MaxWalls=100, not a "python is slow" problem -- named as a real, separate, not-yet-done follow-up (vectorize across walls, or a real C extension), not silently claimed as fixed here. Full python -m unittest suite (35 tests) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-71: colab_train.py now always resumes from the registry when pushing, matching BRAWLPIT exactly -- founder real-time: "bro i should always have that in the skrip - i said just like brawlpit thas how brawlpit works please fix it". Checked BRAWLPIT/scripts/colab_train.py directly: it always passes --resume-from-registry unconditionally whenever pushing to the registry, no separate opt-in flag. S459-70's own version had it gated behind SHANKPIT_RESUME_FROM_REGISTRY=1 -- an unjustified deviation from the founder's own original ask. Generation count is timesteps-driven (one generation per --save-freq=4096 chunk), so 20000/4096 rounded to the observed 5 generations. - S459-69: real fix for jump "rubberbanding" -- put velocity on the wire, matching S459-68's own named lesson that a two-point position finite-difference estimate is unsafe. NetPlayer (protocol.h) gained real vx/vy/vz fields (struct grew 72->84 bytes); server_broadcast populates them from the server's own exact PlayerState.vx/vy/vz; client_reconcile_local_player now sets p->vx/vy/vz directly from the server's real value before replay, instead of leaving it at whatever the client's own local prediction had. Live-verified: connected a real diagnostic client, jumped, confirmed real vy values on the wire matching the true physics arc exactly (0.85 at takeoff, decaying by gravity each tick, crossing negative at the apex) -- not an estimate. Found and fixed a real, independent, PRE-EXISTING bug along the way: apps2/emily-bot's Go decoder (snapshot.go) had netPlayerSize stuck at 68 bytes since before kill_streak (S459-52) grew the real struct to 72 -- every multi-entity snapshot has been decoding every entity after the first with a wrong stride since then. Fixed to the real 84, added the missing kill_streak/vx/vy/vz offsets and decode fields. rl_env_packet.py's ctypes mirror updated to match exactly (offsetof-verified). Full go build/test (all packages) and python -m unittest suite (35 tests, fixed 3 hardcoded-72 assertions to be real/symbolic) green. Client "feel" needs a human playtest -- SDL2 GUI client, not scriptable headlessly (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-68: reverted S459-67 -- made jump feel catastrophically worse, not better. Founder real-time: "ok way worse now when i jump i fly way up in the air and then it slams me back down and then is like a portal opens... its like i slam through the wall and glitch around like 16 different directions in 1 second". Real root cause of the regression: S459-67's finite-difference velocity re-seed averages velocity over the WHOLE interval between two confirmed network positions -- valid for roughly-constant velocity, badly wrong for a jump arc where velocity is constantly changing under gravity. Any reconcile interval spanning a meaningful chunk of the arc produced a badly wrong re-seeded vy, and re-injecting that wrong value straight into the shared deterministic physics simulation (rather than just a visual offset) let the error compound catastrophically instead of just looking a little off. Reverted cleanly via `git revert 7e88bdd` (commit 7711be0) -- apps/lobby back to the S459-65 state (which the founder confirmed was "a little better"), builds clean, no net_prev_auth references remain. Real, honest lesson: velocity is NOT safely reconstructable from two position samples across an arbitrary-length network interval without knowing the real acceleration profile in between -- a real fix here needs either putting velocity on the wire, or a fundamentally different smoothing approach, not two-point finite differencing. Not reattempted this session (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-66: fixed a false-positive red kill ring appearing before a real kill -- founder real-time: "theres some issue with firing the gun like i have to click a bunch of times like double with the sniper... i get 2 red frag indicators the first one is a false positive". Root cause: hit_feedback (the wire field driving apps/lobby's own >=25 red double-ring kill marker) was never reset back to 0 anywhere in packages/common/physics.h (the online multiplayer path) once set by a real hit/kill -- a real kill's hit_feedback=30 stayed on the attacker's PlayerState and kept broadcasting on every snapshot (~31Hz) until their next hit landed, so the next shot always showed a stale kill ring regardless of its own real outcome, immediately followed by the real new kill's own ring. Confirmed this was a genuine gap, not invented: packages/simulation/local_game.h (the offline/local sim path) already has this exact `if (p->hit_feedback > 0) p->hit_feedback--;` decay pattern in two places -- physics.h's own online path was simply missing it. Fixed by adding the identical decay alongside the other real per-tick timers (attack_cooldown/is_shooting/ability_cooldown) in update_weapons. Both apps/server and apps/lobby build clean. Full python -m unittest suite (35 tests, unrelated but confirms nothing else broke) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-65: fixed jump feeling like it "slams you back to the ground" -- founder real-time: "it like slams me back on to the ground before i can get much verticality at all" (horizontal movement felt fine). Root cause: client reconciliation's RECONCILE_HARD_SNAP_DIST was 2.0 world units -- any predicted-vs-authoritative divergence past that skips all smoothing and hard-snaps the camera, a path meant for genuine desyncs/teleports, not normal jumping. Live-measured via a real diagnostic client against an isolated dev server (not a guess): a single real jump reaches ~8.65 units of height within ~300ms, over 4x the old threshold -- every real jump was hitting the harsh unsmoothed path almost immediately. Bumped RECONCILE_HARD_SNAP_DIST to 12.0 (real margin above the measured jump peak). apps/lobby builds clean. Client "feel" needs a human playtest to fully confirm -- the fix's own reasoning is verified via real server telemetry, but this is an SDL2 GUI client, not scriptable headlessly (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-64: fixed the real, live cause of "same elos" on Colab -- every evaluation match was timing out. Found directly via the new S459-63 eval_note: the founder's real Colab run showed `CRASH: evaluation match itself failed (... timed out after 45.0 seconds)` on every single evaluation, every generation. Root cause: the old 45s patience window (30s match + 15s margin) never accounted for real subprocess startup cost (PPO.load() + torch/sb3 import, incurred TWICE concurrently by both eval bots) on a slower/shared Colab CPU -- fast on this dev box, but genuinely enough to blow the deadline on Colab, silently degrading every real match to "tied" (Elo unchanged). Bumped the margin to a real, generous EVAL_STARTUP_GRACE_SECONDS=90. Live-verified via a fresh local regression run: real decisive kills/Elo movement confirmed unaffected by the larger timeout. Full python -m unittest suite (35 tests) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-63: rl_train_packet.py now pushes a real eval_note alongside each checkpoint -- founder real-time: "how the fuck is my colab log gonna help it just says training". _run_evaluation_match now returns (score_a, note) instead of just score_a -- note is a real, short summary (real kill counts, or a captured crash/report-failure reason) that gets pushed to IDUNA's new eval_note field so it's visible directly via the registry API, no training-process stdout access needed. Also proved the entire push/Elo pipeline correct end to end via two more real local runs with an actual registry push (not just local math): gen1 checkpoints correctly landed at elo=1484/1516/1516 in the real IDUNA registry, both with and without the new eval_note field. Full python -m unittest suite (35 tests) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-62: QUEUE now uses the registry's active opponent (when set) as a real stopgap until there's a league to queue against -- founder real-time: "can we update the QUEUE to use the active opponent until we have a league to queue against?" New `rl_registry.py get_active_checkpoint`/`active` CLI subcommand (GET .../active). `ops/shankpit-bot-pool.sh` checks once at startup: if IDUNA has an active opponent set, downloads it and launches real `frozen_policy_bot.py` (PPO inference over UDP) instead of the heuristic `emily-bot` pool; falls back to heuristic on any failure (none set, registry unreachable, download failed). Live-verified end to end against an isolated test server (not the live QUEUE port): real activation via the actual Go store logic, `rl_registry.py active`/`pull` correctly fetched and cached the checkpoint, the bot pool script launched a real frozen-policy bot that connected and stayed active for 1000+ server ticks, then fully cleaned up and reverted production back to "no active opponent." Full `python -m unittest` suite green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-61: added real diagnostics to rl_train_packet.py's per-generation evaluation match -- founder real-time: "i have 2 gens same elo seems wrong" (after S459-60's reordering fix was already live). `_run_evaluation_match` used to return a silent 0.5 for both a genuine 0-0 tie AND a crashed/unreadable bot report, indistinguishable in the log; the two frozen_policy_bot.py eval opponents also ran with stdout/stderr fully silenced (DEVNULL), so a real crash's own traceback was invisible. Now captures each bot's real output to a log file and prints kill counts (or the captured crash output on a report-read failure) for every evaluation match. Live-verified with a real local 2-generation run: real, decisive kill counts logged (e.g. `main_gen1.zip kills=12 vs main_gen0.zip kills=8`), Elo correctly moved 1500->1516/1484 for all 3 roles -- confirms S459-60's fix and this diagnostic both work; a genuinely stuck run will now show either explicit tie kill-counts or a real crash reason instead of an opaque flat 1500. Full python -m unittest suite (35 tests) green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-60: fixed a real, live bug where every pushed registry checkpoint's Elo stayed 1500 forever -- founder real-time: "the elos are not going up and down". Same category as BRAWLPIT's own S424 fix (the piece that moves Elo wasn't connected to what gets reported), different manifestation: rl_train_packet.py's per-generation evaluation match/record_match_result (already built, unlike BRAWLPIT's original gap) ran AFTER push_checkpoint, so every push carried the pre-evaluation, purely-inherited Elo -- the local league's Elo WAS moving correctly, it just never reached the remote registry. Fixed by reordering: evaluate first, push with the real, current post-match Elo. Live-verified via a real 2-generation local run: gen 1 vs gen 0 moved Elo 1500->1516/1484 for main/main_exploiter, confirmed computed before the (now-correct) push point. Full python -m unittest suite green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-59: fixed a real, found-live regression in scripts/colab_train.py -- it still called rl_train_packet.py with the pre-S459-54 single-agent CLI (--port/--opponents/--out), which no longer exists after that rewrite into the real 3-role league orchestrator (--league-dir/--output-dir/--registry-url/etc); running the Colab cell as it stood would have failed immediately with an argparse error and trained nothing. Rewired to invoke the real league orchestrator directly (which self-pushes each generation to the registry), added --resume-from-registry opt-in. Verified live: rl_train_packet.py accepts every flag colab_train.py now passes (got past argparse into a real registry auth call), full python -m unittest suite green (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-58: real, team-tagged spawner points for SCENE_CUSTOM_LEVEL, FFA as the real fallback -- level_boxes.h parses a level's own real "spawners" export field, physics.h stores them (phys_set_custom_level_spawners) and custom_level_pick_spawner selects a team-matching spawner first, falls back to an FFA-tagged spawner, and finally falls back to the already-built S459-57 computed-scatter logic when the level has no usable spawner at all. Wired into both apps/server and apps/lobby's own real custom-level-load call sites. Live-verified via a standalone smoke test (team match, FFA fallback, no-spawner fallback all pass); full server+lobby builds clean (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)
- S459-57: fixed real spawn-point bug -- custom levels (QUEUE) had no real spawn logic, players scattered up to 1500 units apart on a 325x320-unit level. Now computes real spawn points from the level's own geometry, verified clustering within ~100-280 units live (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)

- S459-53: real, independent debug-build CI job (tests.yml) producing its own artifact with NET_VERBOSE_LOG/NET_JITTER_DIAG/NET_PARITY_DEBUG enabled -- verified passing on real GitHub Actions (sess-20260905-0720-ec33e7c5)

## 2026-08-07
- feat(render): S144-02 Stage A -- SHANKPIT's first shader/VBO subsystem. Founder: "build shankpit shader dynamic vbo," picking the real GLSL/VAO/VBO path over a legacy-immediate-mode adapter for the upcoming GOLDENBAND skinned rig integration. New `packages/render/gl_shader.c/.h`: GL 2.0+ function pointers loaded by hand via `SDL_GL_GetProcAddress` (portable to the Windows mingw cross-compile target, which only gets GL 1.1 for free -- not something a direct `-lGL` link would give on that platform), shader compile/link helpers with real error logging, a dynamic VBO (buffer-orphaning re-upload pattern, same fix GFD's battlegrounds_gui needed for its own skinned mesh) fixed to a pos+normal vertex layout matching what GOLDENBAND's `gband_mesh_rig` already emits. Proof-of-concept wired into the live render loop: a small magenta square drawn via the new pipeline (identity MVP, no camera dependency) alongside all existing fixed-function rendering. Live-verified under Xvfb: real screenshot shows the square rendering correctly with zero interference to existing gameplay (buggies, HUD, terrain all unaffected). Native Linux build clean; the mingw Windows cross-compile target shares the same source list but couldn't be verified in this environment (no mingw toolchain installed here) -- the SDL_GL_GetProcAddress approach was specifically chosen for cross-platform portability, but that claim is design-level, not yet build-verified on Windows. Stages B (interpolation-aware `gband_mesh_rig` port) and C (wire into `SKIN_TYLER`) remain open.

GameMode constants added to packages2/common. ### 2026-06-12 (continued 2)
- Implemented voxel chunk rendering in C client (THE_BRIDGE_SPEC.md). Resolved `PACKET_DISCONNECT=4` / `PacketVoxelData=4` type ID conflict by renaming DISCONNECT to `PACKET_VOXEL_DATA=4` in `packages/common/protocol.h` (DISCONNECT was never handled by the client). Added `PACKET_IMPACT=5`, `PACKET_SCENE_CHANGE=6`, `VoxelChunkCache` typedef, and voxel constants to protocol.h. Added 32-slot `g_voxel_chunks` cache, `net_apply_voxel_packet()` parser (parses chunk_x/z + block array from 16-byte header + 6-byte blocks), `PACKET_VOXEL_DATA` handler in `net_tick()`, and `draw_voxel_chunks()` renderer (retro brutalist style: GL_QUADS body + GL_LINE_LOOP neon wireframe; logs=brown/amber, leaves=forest green). Render call inserted after `draw_map()` in draw_scene. Go server builds clean, packet type IDs now match across C and Go.

### 2026-06-12 (continued)
- Fixed buggy jitter: added velocity-based extrapolation for all buggies between server snapshots. Added `snap_x/y/z`, `snap_vx/vy/vz`, `snap_yaw`, `snap_yaw_rate_dps`, `snap_recv_ms` to `BuggyState` (local only, not in `NetBuggy`). On each snapshot receive, these are set as an anchor. `buggy_advance_remote_positions()` is called every game tick and extrapolates position + yaw from the anchor, capped at 150 ms. Prevents the hard-position-snap that caused visible stutter at ~32 ms snapshot intervals.
- Fixed voxworld FPS drop: `draw_voxworld_grass_overlay` radius reduced from 900 to 600 units (~55% fewer candidate positions). Added early distance-squared reject before any terrain query to avoid sqrtf on grid cells outside the circle. Smoothstep fade adjusted to match new radius.
- Go server snapshot rate increased from 20 Hz (50 ms) to ~30 Hz (33 ms) to match the C server's `SERVER_SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_TICKS=2` at 60 Hz. Added comment clarifying the 250 ms `SetReadDeadline` is a read poll timeout, not the tick rate.

### 2026-06-12
- Verified Dragonfly Go backend (apps2/server-go) builds clean and serves UDP on :6969. PacketConnect → PacketWelcome handshake confirmed. VoxelData streaming, portal travel (BTN_USE → system.PortalTriggered → ResolvePortalDestination → sendSceneChange), and 20Hz per-scene snapshot broadcast all operational. Milestones 1 and 2 (portal travel + per-player scene isolation + cross-scene attack guard) confirmed complete per docs2/NORTHSTAR.md.
- Architecture memo filed to Emily Prime (EMILY/signals/tasks/) requesting: WorldBackend Go interface spec, GoblinFoxDragon repo relationship clarification, season lineage snapshot schema, and Construct expansion plan. Emily to doc-first before any Milestone 3 implementation.

### 2026-06-09
- Cross-scene attack guard: `gameWorld` replaces the `world{}` stub in `apps2/server-go`. `RayTrace` iterates same-scene clients only (skips clients with a different `sceneID`), using ray-sphere intersection (hitbox radius 0.4, sphere centered at chest height). Shooter is excluded. Per-shot `shankPlayer` now uses real client position and `sceneID` from `clientInfo`.
- Added `Vec3.Sub`, `Vec3.Len`, `Vec3.Dot` to `server/system/ballistics.go` for ray-sphere math.
- Tests: `TestCrossSceneAttackGuard`, `TestCrossSceneNoHit`, `TestShooterDoesNotHitSelf`.

### 2026-06-06 (continued 5)
- Fixed death camera blend not resetting between sessions: `death_cam_blend` is now cleared on spawn transition sync (network) and on lobby→game transition, preventing a tilted death-camera perspective at the start of a new game after dying in the previous one.
- Fixed scene transition not clearing buggies: `client_apply_scene_id` now zeroes buggy slots (was already doing helis and projectiles).

### 2026-06-06 (continued 4)
- Fixed `net_cmd_seq` type mismatch: changed from `int` to `unsigned int` to match `cmd.sequence` (unsigned) and prevent sign-extension on assignment.
- Fixed `CLIENT_RECON_HISTORY` in `docs2/CLIENT_PREDICTION_SPEC.md`: was documented as 64, actual value is 256.

### 2026-06-06 (continued 3)
- Fixed helicopter and buggy snapshot count mismatch: same two-pass fix applied — `heli_count` and `buggy_count` bytes are now written after serialization. Buffer enlarged to exact worst-case: `MAX_CLIENTS * sizeof(NetPlayer) + MAX_HELICOPTERS * sizeof(NetHelicopter) + MAX_BUGGIES * sizeof(NetBuggy) + headers`.

### 2026-06-06 (continued 2)
- Fixed snapshot serialization count mismatch: `server_broadcast` now writes the count byte *after* serializing players so the declared count always matches the actual bytes written. The old pre-counted value could exceed the actual serialized entities when the buffer was full, causing the client to reject the entire snapshot. Buffer resized to `MAX_CLIENTS * sizeof(NetPlayer) + headroom` so the guard is no longer needed.
- Fixed remote player interpolation cross-scene blending: interp buffer is now flushed when a snapshot shows a player's `scene_id` changed (portal travel), preventing brief teleport-smearing between two different map locations.

### 2026-06-06 (continued)
- Fixed DNS stall on JOIN: `net_connect()` now tries `inet_pton` first (no DNS call for IP addresses) and caches the resolved address so repeated JOIN attempts don't re-resolve.
- Fixed orphaned-projectile teamfire bug: lingering projectiles from disconnected players (zeroed slot, team_id=0) could incorrectly block damage to team-0 players; owner pointer is now nulled when the owner slot is inactive.
- Fixed bot targeting across scenes: bot AI now excludes players in a different scene_id from the target candidate list, preventing bots from rotating/shooting at invisible targets.
- Fixed projectile lower-bounds escape: added `y < -100` floor check in `update_projectiles` (was only checking the upper bound `y > 2000`).
- Removed dead code: `ctf_schedule_respawn` (its logic was already inlined in `apply_projectile_damage`).
- Cleaned up server compile warnings: `(void)` casts for log-macro-gated variables (`avg_ents`, `newest_seq`, `dt_ms`); removed unused `mode_before`.
- Updated window title from stale "BUILD 181 - CTF RELOADED" to "SHANKPIT".

### 2026-06-06
- Added ESC pause menu: RESUME / QUIT TO LOBBY items with keyboard navigation (UP/DOWN/W/S, ENTER); ESC no longer immediately exits to lobby.
- Server disconnect detection: client times out after 5 s of no snapshots, shows red overlay ("SERVER DISCONNECTED") for 2.5 s, then auto-returns to lobby; tick loop breaks cleanly on state change.
- Fixed remote player interpolation: `INTERP_DELAY_MS` corrected to 24 ms (was 220 ms), server snapshot interval reduced to 32 ms (was 48 ms). Old values caused extrapolation (t < 0) instead of smooth interpolation.
- Fixed reconcile correction runaway: correction vector capped at 1.2 units per frame (`RECONCILE_CORR_MAX`).
- Fixed terrain downslope jitter: step-down snap of 0.55 units in `resolve_collision()` keeps players glued to downslopes.
- Fixed frame accumulator tick-spike on game entry: accumulator now resets to 0 when transitioning from lobby to game, preventing dozens of physics ticks in the first game frame.
- Fixed pause input leak: weapon hotkeys (1-6) and sniper RMB zoom no longer fire while paused.
- Fixed `net_local_pid` stale value: explicitly reset to -1 in `reset_client_render_state_for_net()`.
- Hit detection printf gated behind `PHYS_COMBAT_LOG=0` macro (was printing on every bullet impact).
- Reduced client/server log noise: `NET_VERBOSE_LOG`, `NET_LOG_SNAPSHOT`, `NET_LOG_USERCMD`, `NET_LOG_HANDSHAKE` default to 0; only `NET_LOG_TIMEOUT` stays on.
- Server: timeout sweep now computes `now_seconds()` once per sweep; idle status log throttled when no clients connected.
- Removed stale `.bak` files.
- Added `docs2/NORTHSTAR.md`: system northstar covering FPS core, scene correctness, DragonsNShit backend, season lineage, and documentation-powered RSI.
- Added `docs2/CLIENT_PREDICTION_SPEC.md`: documents the two-snapshot interpolator math, prediction + reconcile algorithm, and all tuning constants.
